# State rules — ALL collected states (consolidated)

Readable render of every state gathered so far in the 50-state rules search — **24 states, 1099 rules** with verbatim statutory language. Same format as `DATA_state_rules.md`, consolidated across waves 1–7. Discovery output only (no reconciliation). Arizona shown from its refined wave-2 re-run.

_Last updated 2026-07-22. Per-state provenance (wave / gather method) in the Source column._

## Alabama (AL) — 52 rules

### Eligibility

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AL-0001` | structural | none | **Every Alabama resident has a right to inspect and take a copy of any public record of the state, except as otherwise expressly provided by applicable law; the right (and the corresponding duty to respond under the procedural sections) runs to residents, not to any person.**<br><br>_“Every resident has a right to inspect and take a copy of any public record of this state, except as otherwise expressly provided by applicable law.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-40(a) (amended by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess., eff. Oct. 1, 2024) |
| `AL-0002` | structural | none | **&#x27;Resident&#x27; means an individual permanently domiciled in Alabama with an expectation to remain, as demonstrated by reasonable proof of residency such as an Alabama driver license or voter registration; the software branches request eligibility on this definition.**<br><br>_“RESIDENT. An individual who is permanently domiciled in Alabama with an expectation to remain in Alabama as demonstrated by reasonable proof of residency such as, but not limited to, an Alabama driver license or voter registration.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-43(b)(3) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0003` | structural | none | **The right to inspect and copy is granted to every resident without any condition that the requester state or justify a purpose; no statement of purpose is required to make a request.**<br><br>_“Every resident has a right to inspect and take a copy of any public record of this state, except as otherwise expressly provided by applicable law.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-40(a) (amended by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0004` | structural | none | **A public officer may request reasonable evidence from the requester to establish proof of residency.**<br><br>_“A public officer may request reasonable evidence to establish proof of residency.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(f) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0005` | structural | none | **A public officer has discretion to respond to public records requests made by nonresidents; responding to one nonresident request does not waive the officer&#x27;s right to deny other or future nonresident requests.**<br><br>_“A public officer shall have the discretion to respond to public records requests made by nonresidents, in which case, a public officer&#x27;s decision to respond to such requests shall not operate as a waiver of the public officer&#x27;s right to deny other or future requests made by nonresidents.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(f) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |

### Special Records

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AL-0006` | structural | none | **The judicial branch of state government and any office identified in Article VI of the Constitution of Alabama of 2022 are exempt from the request-processing requirements of Sections 36-12-43 through 36-12-45; requests to those bodies do not follow the standard/time-intensive procedural tracks.**<br><br>_“For purposes of this article, the judicial branch of state government and any office identified in Article VI of the Constitution of Alabama of 2022, are exempted from the requirements of Sections 36-12-43 through 36-12-45.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-40(b) (amended by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0035` | structural | none | **At or around the time of designating a request as time-intensive, the public officer shall record it in a log of currently pending time-intensive requests identifying the requester&#x27;s name and the date of acknowledgment; the log is confidential and not subject to disclosure under this article (though it may remain discoverable under applicable procedure rules).**<br><br>_“At or around the time of designating the request as time-intensive, the public officer shall make a record in a log maintained for keeping track of currently pending time-intensive requests. For each such currently pending request, the log shall identify the name of the requester and the date of acknowledgment pursuant to subdivision (3). The log shall be a confidential document that is not subject to disclosure pursuant to this article, provided the log may remain discoverable pursuant to proper discovery methods provided under applicable rules of procedure.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(b)(5) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0052` | structural | none | **The article shall not be construed to permit any party to a pending or threatened action, suit, or proceeding to obtain information relevant to that matter in lieu of the proper discovery methods provided under applicable rules of procedure.**<br><br>_“Nothing in this article shall be construed to permit any party to a pending or threatened action, suit, or proceeding to obtain information regarding a matter relevant to the pending or threatened action, suit, or proceeding in lieu of the proper discovery methods provided under applicable rules of procedure.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-46 (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |

### Deadlines

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AL-0007` | structural | none | **A &#x27;business day&#x27; is a day the public officer&#x27;s office is open to the public and conducting normal operations; the engine uses this to count all business-day deadlines.**<br><br>_“BUSINESS DAY. A day that the public officer&#x27;s office is open to the public and conducting normal operations.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-43(b)(1) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0024` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 10 days from receipt of the request | **For a standard request, the public officer shall acknowledge a proper request within 10 days of receiving the request.**<br><br>_“The public officer shall acknowledge a proper request within 10 days of receiving the request.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(a)(3) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0025` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 15 business-days from acknowledgment of receipt | **For a standard request, the public officer shall provide a substantive response fulfilling or denying a proper request within 15 business days of acknowledging receipt.**<br><br>_“The public officer shall provide a substantive response fulfilling or denying a proper request within 15 business days of acknowledging receipt.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(a)(4) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0026` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · +15 business-days per written extension notice (repeatable increments) | **The public officer may extend the standard substantive-response period in 15-business-day increments upon written notice to the requester.**<br><br>_“Although the public officer may extend this period in 15-business-day increments upon written notice to the requester, the public officer should process a standard request as expeditiously as possible considering the requester&#x27;s time constraints, the public officer&#x27;s workload, and the nature of the request.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(a)(4) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0030` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 10 business-days from receipt of the request | **For a time-intensive request, the public officer shall acknowledge the request within 10 business days of receiving the request.**<br><br>_“The public officer shall acknowledge the request within 10 business days of receiving the request.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(b)(3) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0033` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 45 business-days from the requester&#x27;s election to proceed | **If the requester elects to proceed with a time-intensive request, the public officer shall provide a substantive response fulfilling or denying the request within 45 business days after the requester elected to proceed.**<br><br>_“If the requester elects to proceed with a time-intensive request, the public officer shall provide a substantive response fulfilling or denying the request within 45 business days after the requester elected to proceed with his or her time-intensive request.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(b)(4) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0034` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · +45 business-days per written extension notice (repeatable increments) | **The public officer may extend the 45-business-day time-intensive response period in 45-business-day increments by notifying the requester in writing.**<br><br>_“The public officer may extend this period in 45-business-day increments by notifying the requester in writing.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(b)(4) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |

### Custody/Routing

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AL-0008` | structural | none | **&#x27;Public officer&#x27; means a public officer or his or her designee responsible for responding to public records requests; this identifies who must respond and to whom requests route.**<br><br>_“PUBLIC OFFICER. A public officer or his or her designee responsible for responding to public records requests.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-43(b)(2) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0049` | structural | none | **When denying a request because the requested public record does not exist within the agency, the public officer may, if known, identify the proper custodian or location for the requested record.**<br><br>_“A statement that denies the request on the grounds that the requested public record does not exist within the government agency. If known to the public officer, the public officer may identify the proper custodian or location for the requested public record.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-43(b)(5)e (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |

### Classification

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AL-0009` | structural | none | **A &#x27;standard request&#x27; is one that seeks specifically and discretely identified records the public officer determines would take less than eight hours of staff time to process (identifying, retrieving, and redacting/withholding protected information), requiring no or minimal clarification; it routes to the standard-request track.**<br><br>_“STANDARD REQUEST. A public records request that seeks one or more specifically and discretely identified public records that the public officer determines would take less than eight hours of staff time to process considering the time needed to identify and retrieve any responsive records and to redact or take other measures to withhold protected information. A standard request should require no or minimal clarification by the requester.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-43(b)(4) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0010` | structural | none | **A &#x27;time-intensive request&#x27; is one the public officer determines would take more than eight hours of staff time to process (identifying, retrieving, and redacting/withholding protected information); it routes to the time-intensive track.**<br><br>_“TIME-INTENSIVE REQUEST. A public records request that the public officer determines would take more than eight hours of staff time to process considering the time needed to identify and retrieve any responsive records and any time needed to redact or take other measures to withhold protected information.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-43(b)(6) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0031` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 15 business-days from acknowledgment of receipt | **The public officer shall notify the requester within 15 business days after acknowledging receipt that the request qualifies as a time-intensive request, and shall at that time notify the requester of any likely fees.**<br><br>_“The public officer shall notify the requester within 15 business days after acknowledging receipt that the request qualifies as a time-intensive request. At that time, the public officer shall notify the requester of any likely fees and allow the requester to withdraw the time-intensive request and submit a new request that is not a time-intensive request.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(b)(4) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0032` | structural | none | **Upon the time-intensive designation notice, the officer shall allow the requester to withdraw the time-intensive request and submit a new request that is not time-intensive; the requester may instead elect to proceed.**<br><br>_“At that time, the public officer shall notify the requester of any likely fees and allow the requester to withdraw the time-intensive request and submit a new request that is not a time-intensive request. If the requester elects to proceed with a time-intensive request, the public officer shall provide a substantive response...”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(b)(4) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |

### Communications

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AL-0011` | structural | none | **A &#x27;substantive response&#x27; is a response setting forth the public officer&#x27;s ultimate position on the substance of the request; it includes (in whole or part) providing the records, stating access will be provided at a set/agreed time and place, stating records are ready upon payment of a reasonable fee, or a denial with reasons, on the ground the record does not exist, for failure to substantially complete the request form, for failure to comply with written procedures, or because the records are not public.**<br><br>_“SUBSTANTIVE RESPONSE. A response to a proper public records request that sets forth the public officer&#x27;s ultimate position on the substance of the request. The term includes, but is not limited to, the following, in whole or in part: a. A statement that the public records are provided as attached or enclosed. b. A statement that access to the requested public records will be provided at a set time, place, and location during regular business hours or at a time, place, and location mutually agreeable to the public officer and the requester. c. A statement that the public officer is prepared to provide the requested public records to the requester upon payment of a reasonable fee. d. A statement that denies the request with reasons stated therefor. e. A statement that denies the request on the grounds that the requested public record does not exist within the government agency. If known to the public officer, the public officer may identify the proper custodian or location for the requested public record. f. A statement that denies the request for failure to substantially complete a standard request form. g. A statement that denies the request for failure to substantially comply with the written procedures established by the public officer for such request. h. A statement that denies the request because the records sought are not public.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-43(b)(5) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0047` | structural | none | **A public officer who receives a request that may appear to relate to critical infrastructure or critical energy infrastructure information shall notify the owner of such infrastructure in writing of the request and provide the owner an opportunity to comment on the request and on the threats to public safety or welfare that could reasonably be expected from public disclosure.**<br><br>_“Any public officer who receives a request for records that may appear to relate to critical infrastructure or critical energy infrastructure information, shall notify the owner of such infrastructure in writing of the request and provide the owner an opportunity to comment on the request and on the threats to public safety or welfare that could reasonably be expected from public disclosure of the records.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-40(a) (amended by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |

### Intake

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AL-0012` | structural | none | **A resident may request access to a public record by following the written procedures established by the public officer having custody; those procedures may set a standard form, a designated recipient (e.g., a public records coordinator), permissible transmission methods, and other submission policies.**<br><br>_“A resident may request access to a public record by following the written procedures for accepting such requests established by the public officer having custody of the public record. The written procedures may establish any of the following: a. A standard request form for use in submitting a public records request. b. A designee, such as a public records coordinator, to whom a public records request shall be addressed. c. The permissible method or methods of transmitting a public records request. d. Any other policies pertaining to the process for submitting a public records request.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-45(a)(1) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0013` | structural | none | **The public officer shall make its written procedures concerning public records reasonably available to the public.**<br><br>_“The public officer shall make his or her written procedures concerning public records reasonably available to the public.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-45(a)(2) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0014` | structural | none | **A public officer is not obligated to respond to a public records request that is not made pursuant to the officer&#x27;s written procedures.**<br><br>_“A public officer shall not be obligated to respond to a public records request that is not made pursuant to the public officer&#x27;s written procedures.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-45(a)(3) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0015` | structural | none | **For a standard request, the public officer may require the requester to submit using a standard request form or by following the officer&#x27;s written procedures for accepting requests.**<br><br>_“The public officer may require the requester to submit his or her request using a standard request form or by following the written procedures for accepting requests for public records established by the public officer.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(a)(1) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0016` | structural | none | **For a time-intensive request, the public officer shall require the requester to submit using a standard request form or by following the officer&#x27;s written procedures for accepting requests.**<br><br>_“The public officer shall require the requester to submit his or her request using a standard request form or by following the written procedures for accepting requests for public records established by the public officer.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(b)(1) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0017` | structural | none | **In the absence of written procedures to the contrary, a resident may submit a request by hand-delivering it or mailing it to the main office or principal place of business of the public officer having custody of the record.**<br><br>_“In the absence of written procedures to the contrary, each of the following shall apply: (1) A resident may submit a public records request by delivering the request by hand or by mailing the request to the main office or principal place of business of the public officer having custody of the public record.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-45(b)(1) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0018` | structural | none | **Receipt of a hand-delivered request occurs when it is received at the officer&#x27;s main office or principal place of business; receipt of a mailed request occurs on the date of actual receipt by the officer; a certified-mail or similar signed postage receipt is prima facie evidence of receipt.**<br><br>_“Receipt of a hand-delivered public records request occurs when the request is received at the public officer&#x27;s main office or principal place of business. Receipt of a mailed public records request occurs on the date of actual receipt by the public officer. A certified mail receipt or similar signed postage receipt shall be prima facie evidence of receipt by the public officer.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-45(b)(2) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0019` | structural | none | **The standard request form shall read substantially as the statutory form (requester contact info, agency, date, and records requested), and the requester, by submitting, certifies being an Alabama resident with standing to request; the officer may require additional information or reorder/reformat fields.**<br><br>_“The standard request form shall read substantially as follows, provided a public officer may require additional information or modify the order or format in which the information is requested: &quot;Requester&#x27;s contact information: Name... Phone number... Email address... Street address... City... State... Zip... Agency you are requesting public records from... Date of request... Records requested: (Be as specific as possible...) ... Payment of fees may be required before your request is fulfilled. By submitting this request, you certify that you are an Alabama resident with standing to make a request for public records pursuant to Alabama law.&quot;”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-45(b)(3) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |

### Clarification

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AL-0020` | structural | none | **A request shall identify the requested public record with reasonable specificity, and a public officer is not obligated to respond to a request that is vague, ambiguous, overly broad, or unreasonable in scope.**<br><br>_“A request made pursuant to this article shall identify the requested public record with reasonable specificity. A public officer shall not be obligated to respond to a public records request that is vague, ambiguous, overly broad, or unreasonable in scope.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(c) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0023` | structural | restarts · Timelines tolled on request for clarification; restart from zero upon receipt of clarification/additional information (treated as a new request) | **If a public officer responds by seeking clarification or additional information, the timelines are tolled and restart once the officer receives the requested clarification/information, as if the requester had submitted a new request.**<br><br>_“If a public officer responds to a request by seeking clarification or additional information, the timelines established in this section shall be tolled and shall restart once the public officer receives the requested clarification or additional information as if the requester had submitted a new request. A public officer&#x27;s decision to seek clarification or additional information with respect to any particular request shall not operate as a waiver of the public officer&#x27;s right to seek clarification or additional information in response to other, future requests.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(g) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |

### Production

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AL-0021` | structural | none | **A public officer is not required to create a new public record if the requested record does not already exist.**<br><br>_“A public officer shall not be required to create a new public record if the requested record does not already exist.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(d) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0022` | structural | none | **A public officer is not required to respond to requests that seek information or other materials that are not public records.**<br><br>_“A public officer shall not be required to respond to requests that seek information or other materials that are not public records.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(e) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0050` | structural | none | **A substantive response may provide that access to the requested records will be given at a set time, place, and location during regular business hours, or at a time, place, and location mutually agreeable to the public officer and the requester.**<br><br>_“A statement that access to the requested public records will be provided at a set time, place, and location during regular business hours or at a time, place, and location mutually agreeable to the public officer and the requester.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-43(b)(5)b (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |

Code § 36-12-41 (amended by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |

### Denials

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AL-0027` | parameter · fixed | terminal · Earlier of 30 business-days or 60 calendar-days from acknowledgment of receipt | **There is a rebuttable presumption that a proper standard request has been denied if a substantive response is not provided within the earlier of 30 business days or 60 calendar days following acknowledgment of receipt.**<br><br>_“There shall be a rebuttable presumption that a proper standard request has been denied by the public officer if: a. A substantive response is not provided to the standard request within the earlier of 30 business days or 60 calendar days following acknowledgment of receipt by the public officer;”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(a)(5)a (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0028` | parameter · fixed | terminal · Earlier of 30 business-days or 60 calendar-days from payment of estimated fees | **There is a rebuttable presumption that a proper standard request has been denied if the public records are not produced within the earlier of 30 business days or 60 calendar days following payment of the estimated fees to the public officer.**<br><br>_“b. The public records are not produced within the earlier of 30 business days or 60 calendar days following the payment of the estimated fees to the public officer.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(a)(5)b (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0029` | structural | none | **No presumption of denial arises for a standard request if the request is not proper or the officer is not obligated to respond, the officer has responded in part, the officer and requester have agreed on the time or substance of the response, negotiations are ongoing, or the officer has reasonably communicated the request&#x27;s status.**<br><br>_“There shall be no presumption that a proper standard request has been denied if: a. The request is not proper or the public officer is not obligated or required to respond as provided in this section; b. The public officer has responded in part; c. The public officer and requester have reached an agreement regarding the time or substance, or both, of the response; d. Negotiations are ongoing between the public officer and the requester; or e. The public officer has reasonably communicated the status of the request to the requester.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(a)(6) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0036` | parameter · fixed | terminal · Earlier of 180 business-days or 270 calendar-days from election to proceed | **There is a rebuttable presumption that a proper time-intensive request has been denied if a substantive response is not provided within the earlier of 180 business days or 270 calendar days following the requester&#x27;s election to proceed.**<br><br>_“There shall be a rebuttable presumption that a proper time-intensive request has been denied by the public officer if: a. A substantive response is not provided within the earlier of 180 business days or 270 calendar days following the requester&#x27;s election to proceed with a time-intensive request.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(b)(6)a (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0037` | parameter · fixed | terminal · Earlier of 180 business-days or 270 calendar-days from payment of estimated fees | **There is a rebuttable presumption that a proper time-intensive request has been denied if the records are not produced within the earlier of 180 business days or 270 calendar days following payment of the estimated fees to the public officer.**<br><br>_“b. The records are not produced within the earlier of 180 business days or 270 calendar days following the payment of the estimated fees to the public officer.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(b)(6)b (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0038` | structural | none | **No presumption of denial arises for a time-intensive request if the request is not proper or the officer is not obligated to respond, the officer has responded in part, the officer and requester have agreed on the time or substance of the response, negotiations are ongoing, or the officer has reasonably communicated the request&#x27;s status.**<br><br>_“There shall be no presumption that a proper time-intensive request has been denied if: a. The public officer has reasonably communicated the status of the request to the requester.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(b)(7) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |

### Fees

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AL-0039` | parameter · soft-standard | none | **A public officer may require the requester to pay a reasonable fee set by the public officer before the requester may receive any public records (standard request); more generally, a certified copy is furnished on payment of a reasonable fee.**<br><br>_“The public officer may require the requester to pay a reasonable fee set by the public officer before the requester may receive any public records.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(a)(2), § 36-12-41 (SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0040` | structural | none | **If the public officer elects to charge a fee for a standard request, the officer shall notify the requester of the estimated fee and withhold any public records until receipt of payment.**<br><br>_“If the public officer elects to charge a fee, the public officer shall notify the requester of the estimated fee and withhold any public records until receipt of payment.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(a)(2) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0044` | structural | none | **For a time-intensive request, the public officer shall require the requester to pay a reasonable fee set by the public officer before providing a substantive response.**<br><br>_“The public officer shall require the requester to pay a reasonable fee set by the public officer before providing a substantive response to the requester.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(b)(2) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0045` | structural | none | **For a time-intensive request, the public officer shall notify the requester in advance of any likely fees and shall withhold any substantive response until receipt of payment.**<br><br>_“The public officer shall notify the requester in advance of any likely fees and shall withhold any substantive response until receipt of payment.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(b)(2) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |

### Payment

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `AL-0041` | structural | none | **For a standard request where a fee is charged, the public officer shall withhold any public records until receipt of payment.**<br><br>_“If the public officer elects to charge a fee, the public officer shall notify the requester of the estimated fee and withhold any public records until receipt of payment.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(a)(2) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0042` | structural | none | **The requester may opt not to pay the fee and thus not receive any substantive response (standard request).**<br><br>_“The requester may opt not to pay the fee and thus not receive any substantive response.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(a)(2) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0043` | structural | none | **For a standard request, the public officer has discretion to require the requester to pay all or a portion of the estimated fee prior to searching for any responsive public records.**<br><br>_“Additionally, the public officer shall have the discretion to require the requester to pay all or a portion of the estimated fee prior to searching for any responsive public records.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(a)(2) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |
| `AL-0046` | structural | none | **For a time-intensive request, the public officer has discretion to require the requester to pay all or a portion of the estimated fee prior to searching for any responsive public records.**<br><br>_“Additionally, the public officer shall have the discretion to require the requester to pay all or a portion of the estimated fee prior to searching for any responsive public records.”_ | Ala. Code § 36-12-44(b)(2) (added by SB270, 2024 Reg. Sess.) |

### Material negatives

- `appeal.administrative.none` — SB270 creates no administrative or AG-review appeal path for a records denial. The only statutory consequence of non-response is the rebuttable constructive-denial presumption (AL-0027/0028/0036/0037), which positions the requester to seek judicial relief. Absence of a found administrative appeal is not proof none exists elsewhere.
- `appeal.judicial.none` — The Act does not itself prescribe a judicial appeal/enforcement procedure, timeline, or venue. Enforcement historically proceeds by mandamus in circuit court under prior Alabama case law (e.g., the Open Records Act line of cases); that remedy/procedure was intentionally excluded here under the inclusion test (remedy/enforcement-amount) except for the deemed-denial trigger. A dedicated case-law pass is needed for the mandamus standard.
- `redaction.segregability` — No explicit statewide segregability / &#x27;redact-and-release the non-exempt remainder&#x27; mandate. Redaction is contemplated only as a processing-time factor in the standard/time-intensive definitions and as preserved by the savings clause (36-12-43(c)); the duty to release segregable portions is not affirmatively stated in SB270.
- `fee.copy_rate_per_page` — No statewide fixed copy rate, per-page ceiling, or itemized fee schedule. Fees are governed only by the &#x27;reasonable fee set by the public officer&#x27; soft standard (AL-0039); each officer/municipality sets the actual amount.
- `fee.waiver_indigency` — No statutory fee-waiver, reduced-fee, or public-interest/indigency provision found in SB270.
- `records.format_electronic` — No explicit rule governing electronic-record format, native format, or a requester&#x27;s right to choose format. The Act speaks to copies and inspection generally without a format-selection provision.
- `custody.preservation_hold` — No request-triggered or litigation-triggered preservation/legal-hold obligation is created by SB270 (the litigation provision, 36-12-46 / AL-0052, bars using the Act in lieu of discovery rather than imposing a hold). Routine retention schedules are governed separately and excluded.
- `intake.acknowledgment_content` — The statute requires an acknowledgment (AL-0024/0030) but does not prescribe required content or form for the acknowledgment itself.

### Structural branches

- `classification.standard_request_threshold` — Two-track processing fork: requests estimated at &lt;8 staff hours run the standard track (AL-0009).
- `classification.time_intensive_threshold` — Requests estimated at &gt;8 staff hours run the separate time-intensive track with its own longer timeline (AL-0010).
- `classification.time_intensive_withdrawal_option` — After the time-intensive designation notice, the request branches: requester may withdraw and resubmit a non-time-intensive request, or elect to proceed (which starts the 45-business-day clock) (AL-0032).
- `clarification.tolling_restart` — Seeking clarification/additional information tolls all timelines and restarts them from zero on receipt, as if a new request (AL-0023).
- `coverage.excluded_public_bodies` — Judicial branch and Article VI constitutional offices are routed out of the 36-12-43 through 36-12-45 procedural machinery (AL-0006).
- `redaction.critical_infrastructure_owner_notice` — Requests touching critical infrastructure trigger a mandatory written notice-and-comment branch to the infrastructure owner before disposition (AL-0047).
- `custody.custodian_referral` — Non-existent-record denials may branch to a referral identifying the proper custodian/location (AL-0049).
- `intake.default_delivery_method` — Absent adopted written procedures, the engine falls back to a statutory default submission path (hand/mail to main office) and receipt-determination rules (AL-0017, AL-0018).
- `denials.constructive_denial_exceptions` — Deemed-denial presumption can be suspended indefinitely when the officer has responded in part, reached agreement, is negotiating, or has reasonably communicated status (AL-0029, AL-0038).
- `tracking.time_intensive_log` — Designating a request time-intensive requires creating an entry in a confidential pending-request log (AL-0035).
- `payment.requester_opt_out` — A standard request enters a terminal abandoned state if the requester opts not to pay the estimated fee (AL-0042).

### Material negatives

- `intake.acknowledgment_window` — No statutory duty to acknowledge a request within a fixed period. Arizona sets no numeric acknowledgment deadline; the only timing standard is &#x27;promptly furnish&#x27; (AZ-0011) and constructive denial on failure to promptly respond (AZ-0012). Absence of a found rule is not proof none exists.
- `deadlines.numeric_response_deadline` — No numeric statutory response/production deadline (no &#x27;X business days&#x27;). The standard is the soft &#x27;promptly&#x27; term. Captured as a soft-standard rule, not a value.
- `clarification.duty_to_assist_or_narrow` — No explicit statutory clarification clock, duty to assist the requester in narrowing an unclear or overbroad request, or non-response/abandonment trigger found in Title 39, Chapter 1.
- `request.tracking_log_requirement` — No statutory requirement that a municipality assign a tracking number or maintain a request log/register for public-records requests found.
- `redaction.segregability_duty` — No general statutory segregate-and-release / redaction-of-exempt-portions procedure stated in the core access statutes; withholding is instead documented via the § 39-121.01(D)(2) index (AZ-0015). Segregability obligations, if any, arise from specific exemption statutes and case law not inventoried this pass.
- `appeal.administrative_internal_stage` — No internal/administrative appeal stage for a denied requester before court; review is directly to superior court by special action (AZ-0025). Emitted affirmatively as the absence of an administrative appeal branch, relevant to cross-state comparison of appeal paths.
- `payment.deposit_for_large_requests` — No general statutory advance-deposit/estimate regime for large non-commercial requests; the only advance-payment authority found is for copying + postage on mailed copies (AZ-0022).
- `fee.statewide_copy_rate` — No statewide statutory per-page copy rate for ordinary (non-commercial) requests; the statute grants only permission to charge a fee (AZ-0018), leaving the value to the municipality.

### Structural branches

- `production.response_promptness` — AZ-0011: the response duty is a soft &#x27;promptly&#x27; standard, not a numeric deadline — the engine applies a standard rather than counting days.
- `denial.constructive_denial_nonresponse` — AZ-0012: failure to promptly respond OR to provide the withholding index is a terminal event that moves the request to a denied state and opens judicial review.
- `review.administrative.governor_commercial_purpose_petition` — AZ-0024: Arizona-specific custodian-initiated review path decided by the Governor, with a 30-day window whose lapse forces release (AZ-0023).
- `appeal.judicial.special_action` — AZ-0025: review of a denial runs directly to superior court via special action — no internal administrative appeal stage precedes it.
- `appeal.judicial.victim_special_action_in_criminal_case` — AZ-0026: a separate judicial branch for victims, litigated inside the pending criminal case.
- `production.electronic_records_native_format_metadata` — AZ-0014: electronic records must be produced in native electronic form including embedded metadata (judicially established).
- `denial.withholding_index` — AZ-0015: on request, an agency custodian must produce a Vaughn-style index of withheld records and reasons; its omission is itself a denial trigger.
- `special_records.crime_victim_report_priority` — AZ-0027: crime-victim police-report requests are processed on a priority track.
- `production.onsite_copying_no_facilities` — AZ-0013: when the custodian lacks copying facilities, the requester is granted supervised access to make copies on site.

## California (CA) — 31 rules

### Eligibility

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CA-0001` | structural | none | **Every person has a right to inspect any public record; the right to request/inspect is not limited to residents, citizens, or an enumerated class of requesters.**<br><br>_“Public records are open to inspection at all times during the office hours of a state or local agency and every person has a right to inspect any public record, except as otherwise provided.”_ | Cal. Gov. Code § 7922.525(a) (Statute) |
| `CA-0002` | structural | none | **An agency may not condition or limit access to an otherwise-disclosable public record based on the purpose for which the record is requested; requesters need not state a reason.**<br><br>_“This division does not allow limitations on access to a public record based upon the purpose for which the record is being requested, if the record is otherwise subject to disclosure.”_ | Cal. Gov. Code § 7921.300 (Statute) |
| `CA-0003` | structural | none | **Records must be made available to any person; there is no residency, citizenship, or in-state requirement to request records.**<br><br>_“Except with respect to public records exempt from disclosure by express provisions of law, each state or local agency, upon a request for a copy of records that reasonably describes an identifiable record or records, shall make the records promptly available to any person upon payment of fees covering direct costs of duplication, or a statutory fee if applicable.”_ | Cal. Gov. Code § 7922.530(a) (Statute) |

### Coverage

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CA-0004` | structural | none | **The CPRA applies to a &#x27;local agency,&#x27; which includes a county; a city (general law or chartered); a city and county; a school district; a municipal corporation; a district; a political subdivision; any board/commission/agency of the foregoing; and other local public agencies.**<br><br>_“As used in this division, &quot;local agency&quot; includes any of the following: (a) A county. (b) A city, whether general law or chartered. (c) A city and county. (d) A school district. (e) A municipal corporation. (f) A district. (g) A political subdivision. (h) Any board, commission, or agency of the foregoing. (i) Another local public agency. (j) An entity that is a legislative body of a local agency pursuant to subdivision (c) or (d) of Section 54952.”_ | Cal. Gov. Code § 7920.510 (Statute) |

Gov. Code § 7920.530(a) (Statute) |

### Intake

Gov. Code § 7921.005 (Statute) |

### Material negatives

- `appeal.administrative` — No statutory administrative appeal or AG-review stage exists in the CPRA. Enforcement is exclusively judicial (§ 7923.000). Absence of a found rule is not proof none exists in local ordinance, but the state statute provides none.
- `denial.deemed_constructive` — No explicit statutory &#x27;deemed denial&#x27; / constructive-denial clock (e.g., &#x27;silence for N days = denial&#x27;). Failure to meet the 10-day determination duty exposes the agency to judicial enforcement but the statute does not convert silence into an automatic denial with a terminal clock.
- `fee.copy_rate_per_page` — No statewide fixed per-page copy rate. The statute caps fees at &#x27;direct costs of duplication&#x27; (§ 7922.530(a)); the city supplies the actual value under that ceiling. (Some jurisdictions and courts treat ~$0.10-0.25/page as within direct cost, but that is not a statewide statutory number.)
- `intake.form_requirement` — No statutory required request form, and no requirement that a request be in writing to trigger the access right (inspection may be requested orally). A WRITTEN request is what triggers the written-denial duties of § 7922.540 — but writing is not a precondition to access itself.
- `intake.acknowledgment_window` — No separate mandatory acknowledgment deadline distinct from the 10-day determination. The 10-day determination under § 7922.535(a) is the only intake-side statutory clock.
- `payment.deposit` — No statutory advance-deposit or fee-estimate-and-hold scheme for ordinary requests; the statute conditions copy release on &#x27;payment of fees covering direct costs of duplication&#x27; (§ 7922.530(a)) but does not authorize a deposit process.
- `clarification.clock_toll` — No explicit statutory tolling or pausing of the 10-day determination clock while the agency seeks clarification. The assistance duty (§ 7922.600) exists but carries no attached toll of the § 7922.535 deadline.
- `deadline.production_numeric` — No numeric statutory deadline for actual PRODUCTION of records; production is governed by the soft &#x27;promptly&#x27; standard (§ 7922.530(a)) and the agency&#x27;s self-set &#x27;estimated date&#x27; (§ 7922.535(a)), not a fixed day-count.

### Structural branches

- `notice.determination_with_reasons` — Mandatory 10-day determination stage: agency issues a notice stating whether the request seeks disclosable records and the reasons (§ 7922.535(a)).
- `notice.estimated_availability_date` — When records are disclosable, the agency commits to a per-request &#x27;estimated date and time&#x27; of availability — a stored commitment the engine tracks, not a fixed value (§ 7922.535(a)).
- `notice.extension_written` — Optional unusual-circumstances extension branch: written notice from agency head/designee stating reasons and the expected determination date, capped at +14 days (§ 7922.535(b)).
- `production.promptly` — Soft-standard production deadline (&#x27;promptly&#x27;) distinct from the determination clock — engine applies a standard, not a city-entered number (§ 7922.530(a)).
- `production.website_redirect` — Website-posting production alternative with a direct-copy fallback when the requester cannot access/reproduce from the site (§ 7922.545).
- `inspection.self_copy_own_device` — On-premises inspection branch permitting requester no-contact self-copying with their own equipment, subject to reasonable agency limits (§ 7922.530(b)-(c)).
- `redaction.segregability` — Redact-and-release step: reasonably segregable non-exempt portions must be produced after deleting exempt portions (§ 7922.525(b)).
- `fee.data_extraction_cost_shift` — Conditional fee-shift branch where interval-only production or data compilation/extraction/programming moves construction/programming costs to the requester (§ 7922.575(b)).
- `denial.justification_standard` — Withholding-justification decision requiring either an express exemption or a case-specific public-interest balancing test (the latter needs human legal review) (§ 7922.000).
- `appeal.judicial.mandate` — California&#x27;s sole review path is judicial: writ of mandate / injunctive / declaratory relief in court — there is no administrative appeal stage (§ 7923.000).

## Colorado (CO) — 25 rules

### Eligibility

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CO-0001` | structural | none | **Any person may inspect any public record at reasonable times, except as otherwise provided in the Act or by law; no requester qualification is imposed.**<br><br>_“All public records shall be open for inspection by any person at reasonable times, except as provided in this part 2 or as otherwise provided by law.”_ | C.R.S. § 24-72-203(1)(a) |
| `CO-0002` | structural | none | **CORA imposes no residency or citizenship requirement; the right to inspect runs to &#x27;any person&#x27; without geographic qualification.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“The Act extends inspection to &#x27;any person&#x27; and states no residency, citizenship, or geographic qualification; the absence of any residency condition is derived from the &#x27;any person&#x27; grant in 24-72-203(1)(a).”_ | C.R.S. § 24-72-203(1)(a) |
| `CO-0003` | structural | none | **A requester need not state a purpose or reason to inspect general public records; the right runs to any person at reasonable times.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“The general inspection right in 24-72-203(1)(a) is unconditional as to purpose; no statement-of-purpose is required for ordinary public records (derived from the unqualified &#x27;any person ... at reasonable times&#x27; grant).”_ | C.R.S. § 24-72-203(1)(a) |

### Custody/Routing

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CO-0004` | structural | none | **The &#x27;custodian&#x27; is the official custodian or any authorized person having personal custody and control of the public records in question; this identifies who must respond and drives routing.**<br><br>_“&quot;Custodian&quot; means and includes the official custodian or any authorized person having personal custody and control of the public records in question.”_ | C.R.S. § 24-72-202(1) |
| `CO-0005` | structural | none | **The custodian may make and enforce rules reasonably necessary for the protection of the records and to prevent unnecessary interference with the discharge of duties.**<br><br>_“The official custodian of any public records may make such rules with reference to the inspection of such records as are reasonably necessary for the protection of such records and the prevention of unnecessary interference with the regular discharge of the duties of the custodian or the custodian&#x27;s office.”_ | C.R.S. § 24-72-203(2)(a) |
| `CO-0006` | structural | none | **If the requested records are not in the custody or control of the person to whom application is made, that person shall so notify the applicant in writing and, if known, state which custodian has custody or control.**<br><br>_“If the public records requested are not in the custody or control of the person to whom application is made, such person shall forthwith notify the applicant of this fact, in writing if requested by the applicant. In such notification, such person shall state, in detail to the best of such person&#x27;s knowledge and belief, the reason for the absence of the records from such person&#x27;s custody or control, their location, and what person then has custody or control of the records.”_ | C.R.S. § 24-72-203(2)(b) |

### Deadlines

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CO-0007` | parameter · ceiling | sets-deadline · presumptive ceiling: 3 working days from request receipt (rebuttable presumption of &#x27;reasonable time&#x27;) | **When records are not readily available, the custodian must set a date and hour for inspection within a reasonable time after the request; a reasonable time is presumed to be three working days or less.**<br><br>_“The date and hour set for the inspection of records not readily available at the time of the request shall be within a reasonable time after the request. As used in this subsection (3), a &quot;reasonable time&quot; shall be presumed to be three working days or less.”_ | C.R.S. § 24-72-203(3)(b) |
| `CO-0008` | parameter · ceiling | sets-deadline · ceiling: up to 7 additional working days beyond the presumptive 3-working-day period | **The three-working-day period may be extended when extenuating circumstances exist, but the extension shall not exceed seven working days.**<br><br>_“Such period may be extended if extenuating circumstances exist. However, such period of extension shall not exceed seven working days.”_ | C.R.S. § 24-72-203(3)(b) |
| `CO-0010` | structural | none | **Extenuating circumstances can never apply to a request that relates to a single, specifically identified document.**<br><br>_“In no event can extenuating circumstances apply to a request that relates to a single, specifically identified document.”_ | C.R.S. § 24-72-203(3)(b) |
| `CO-0011` | structural | none | **Extenuating circumstances are limited to enumerated situations, including a broadly stated request encompassing all or substantially all of a large category of records without sufficient specificity to allow preparation within the three-day period.**<br><br>_“Extenuating circumstances shall apply only when ... [t]he request for records is for a broadly stated request that encompasses all or substantially all of a large category of records and the request is without sufficient specificity to allow the custodian reasonably to prepare or gather the records within the three-day period; [and other enumerated situations including requests requiring gathering of a large volume of records that would interfere with the custodian&#x27;s other public-service duties, and, for the general assembly, when it is in session].”_ | C.R.S. § 24-72-203(3)(b) |

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |

| `CO-0013` | structural | none | **The custodian need not produce in the sortable/searchable format if doing so would violate a copyright or licensing agreement, or if it is not technologically or practically feasible to remove withholdable information or to provide the format without purchasing new software.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“[The custodian is not required to produce a record in a sortable or searchable format if doing so would violate a copyright or licensing agreement, or if it is not technologically or practically feasible to remove information that may be withheld or to provide the record in the requested format without the purchase of software.]”_ | C.R.S. § 24-72-203(3.5) |

### Fees

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CO-0016` | parameter · ceiling | none | **The custodian may charge a fee not to exceed twenty-five cents per standard page for a copy of a public record.**<br><br>_“A custodian may charge a fee not to exceed twenty-five cents per standard page for a copy of a public record or a fee not to exceed the actual cost of providing a copy, printout, or photograph of a public record in a format other than a standard page.”_ | C.R.S. § 24-72-205(5)(a) |
| `CO-0017` | parameter · ceiling | none | **For a copy, printout, or photograph in a format other than a standard page, the custodian may charge a fee not to exceed the actual cost of providing it.**<br><br>_“... a fee not to exceed the actual cost of providing a copy, printout, or photograph of a public record in a format other than a standard page.”_ | C.R.S. § 24-72-205(5)(a) |
| `CO-0018` | parameter · ceiling | none | **After the first hour, the custodian may charge a fee for research and retrieval of public records not to exceed thirty dollars per hour.**<br><br>_“After the first hour of time has been expended, the custodian may charge a fee for the research and retrieval of public records that shall not exceed thirty dollars per hour.”_ | C.R.S. § 24-72-205(6)(a) |
| `CO-0019` | parameter · fixed | none | **The custodian shall not impose any charge for the first hour of time expended in research and retrieval of public records.**<br><br>_“Under any such policy, the custodian shall not impose a charge for the first hour of time expended in connection with the research and retrieval of public records.”_ | C.R.S. § 24-72-205(6)(a) |
| `CO-0020` | structural | none | **A custodian may impose a research-and-retrieval fee only if, before receiving the request, it has posted on its website or otherwise published a written policy specifying the applicable conditions and the amount of any current fee.**<br><br>_“A custodian may impose a fee in response to a request for the research and retrieval of public records only if the custodian has, prior to the date of receiving the request, either posted on the custodian&#x27;s website or otherwise published a written policy that specifies the applicable conditions concerning the research and retrieval of public records by the custodian, including the amount of any current fee.”_ | C.R.S. § 24-72-205(6)(a) |
| `CO-0021` | parameter · fixed | none | **No transmission fee may be charged to the requester for transmitting public records via electronic mail.**<br><br>_“No transmission fees may be charged to the record requester for transmitting public records via electronic mail.”_ | C.R.S. § 24-72-205(1)(b) |

### Appeals

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CO-0022` | structural | none | **A person denied the right to inspect a record, or who alleges a violation of 24-72-203(3.5), may apply to the district court where the record is found for an order directing the custodian to show cause why inspection should not be permitted.**<br><br>_“Any person denied the right to inspect any record covered by this part 2 or who alleges a violation of section 24-72-203 (3.5) may apply to the district court of the district wherein the record is found for an order directing the custodian of such record to show cause why the custodian should not permit the inspection of such record.”_ | C.R.S. § 24-72-204(5)(a) |
| `CO-0023` | structural | sets-deadline · 14 days: written notice to custodian must precede filing by at least 14 days | **At least fourteen days before filing an application with the district court, the person denied inspection must file a written notice with the custodian.**<br><br>_“... except that, at least fourteen days prior to filing an application with the district court, the person who has been denied the right to inspect the record shall file a written notice with the custodian ...”_ | C.R.S. § 24-72-204(5)(a) |
| `CO-0024` | structural | sets-deadline · 3 business days: expedited written notice (with factual basis) must precede filing by at least 3 business days | **If the requester states an expedited need in the required written notice, the notice (including a factual basis for the expedited need) must be provided to the custodian at least three business days before filing the application.**<br><br>_“If the person who has been denied access to inspect a record states in the required written notice to the custodian that the person needs to pursue access to the record on an expedited basis, the person must provide such written notice, including a factual basis of the expedited need for the record, to the custodian at least three business days prior to the date on which the person files the application with the district court.”_ | C.R.S. § 24-72-204(5)(a) |
| `CO-0025` | structural | sets-deadline · undefined-soft: &quot;earliest practical time&quot; from filing of the application | **The hearing on a show-cause application must be held at the earliest practical time.**<br><br>_“Hearing on the application described in subsection (5)(a) of this section must be held at the earliest practical time.”_ | C.R.S. § 24-72-204(5)(b) |

### Material negatives

- `intake.acknowledgment_window` — CORA sets a response/inspection window (presumptive 3 working days, 24-72-203(3)(b)) but prescribes no separate mandatory acknowledgment-of-receipt deadline distinct from providing access. Absence of a found rule is not proof none exists.
- `denial.deemed_denial_clock` — No express statutory &#x27;deemed denial&#x27; or constructive-denial clock (silence for N days = denial). CORA&#x27;s remedy trigger is an affirmative denial of access (24-72-204(4)-(5)); it does not convert non-response into an automatic denial after a fixed period. Keeper-sliver #3 therefore yields a negative for Colorado.
- `intake.written_request_required` — No statute mandates that a request be in writing or on a specific form. The custodian may adopt reasonable rules (24-72-203(2)(a)); intake format is thus a municipal-level rule, not a statewide fixed requirement.
- `payment.deposit_or_prepayment_cap` — The statute authorizes copy and research/retrieval fees but states no statutory deposit/prepayment cap or mandatory prepayment procedure. Custodian prepayment practices are local and not fixed by statute in the sections reviewed.
- `clarification.vague_request_clock` — No separate statutory clarification-and-toll mechanism for vague requests. A broadly stated/insufficiently specific request instead qualifies as an &#x27;extenuating circumstance&#x27; extending the response period (24-72-203(3)(b)); there is no distinct clarification clock that tolls or resets the deadline.
- `fee.commercial_purpose_surcharge` — The sections reviewed do not establish a general commercial-purpose fee surcharge for ordinary records. (Special provisions for records of names/addresses used for solicitation exist under 24-72-204/205.5 and belong to a later special-records pass.)

### Structural branches

- `response.extension_extenuating` — Extenuating-circumstances extension path: on qualifying triggers the engine adds up to 7 working days, but only after issuing a written finding within the base 3-working-day window (CO-0009) and only if the request is not for a single identified document (CO-0010).
- `production.native_digital_format` — Tiered digital-format production branch: sortable-stored records produced sortable, searchable-stored records produced searchable (CO-0012), with a feasibility/licensing off-ramp (CO-0013).
- `denial.written_grounds_on_request` — On denial, a requester-triggered branch producing a written, law-citing statement of grounds &#x27;forthwith&#x27; (CO-0014), which also serves as the predicate document for the judicial path.
- `fee.research_policy_prerequisite` — Fee-eligibility gate: research/retrieval fees may be charged only after a pre-published written fee policy exists; without it the fee branch is unavailable (CO-0020).
- `appeal.judicial.show_cause_application` — Judicial-only enforcement path (no internal administrative appeal): mandatory 14-day pre-suit notice to custodian (CO-0023), a 3-business-day expedited variant (CO-0024), then a district-court show-cause hearing at the earliest practical time (CO-0025).

---

## Connecticut (CT) — 42 rules

### Eligibility

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CT-0001` | structural | none | **Every person has the right to inspect, copy, or receive a copy of any public record maintained or kept on file by any public agency (subject to statutory exemptions).**<br><br>_“Except as otherwise provided by any federal law or state statute, all records maintained or kept on file by any public agency, whether or not such records are required by any law or by any rule or regulation, shall be public records and every person shall have the right to (1) inspect such records promptly during regular office or business hours, (2) copy such records in accordance with subsection (g) of section 1-212, or (3) receive a copy of such records in accordance with section 1-212.”_ | Conn. Gen. Stat. § 1-210(a) |
| `CT-0002` | structural | none | **Access is granted to &#x27;every person&#x27; / &#x27;any person&#x27; with no in-state residency condition; a requester need not be a Connecticut resident.**<br><br>_“all records maintained or kept on file by any public agency ... shall be public records and every person shall have the right to (1) inspect such records promptly during regular office or business hours”_ | Conn. Gen. Stat. § 1-210(a) |
| `CT-0003` | structural | none | **A requester need not state or justify a purpose to inspect, copy, or receive public records; the right is unconditional as to motive.**<br><br>_“every person shall have the right to (1) inspect such records promptly during regular office or business hours, (2) copy such records in accordance with subsection (g) of section 1-212, or (3) receive a copy of such records in accordance with section 1-212.”_ | Conn. Gen. Stat. § 1-210(a) |
| `CT-0004` | structural | none | **&#x27;Person&#x27; who may request records includes natural persons and enumerated organizational entities.**<br><br>_“&#x27;Person&#x27; means natural person, partnership, corporation, limited liability company, association or society.”_ | Conn. Gen. Stat. § 1-200(4) |

### Special Records

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CT-0005` | structural | none | **&#x27;Public agency&#x27; subject to FOIA includes any office, department, board, commission, authority or official of any city, town, borough, municipal corporation, school district or other political subdivision, plus functional-equivalent private entities.**<br><br>_“&#x27;Public agency&#x27; or &#x27;agency&#x27; means: (A) Any executive, administrative or legislative office of the state or any political subdivision of the state and any state or town agency, any department, institution, bureau, board, commission, authority or official of the state or of any city, town, borough, municipal corporation, school district, regional district or other district or other political subdivision of the state ...; (B) Any person to the extent such person is deemed to be the functional equivalent of a public agency pursuant to law; or (C) Any &#x27;implementing agency&#x27;, as defined in section 32-222.”_ | Conn. Gen. Stat. § 1-200(1) |
| `CT-0006` | structural | none | **A &#x27;public record&#x27; is any recorded data or information relating to the conduct of the public&#x27;s business prepared, owned, used, received or retained by a public agency (or which the agency is entitled to receive by law/contract), in any medium.**<br><br>_“&#x27;Public records or files&#x27; means any recorded data or information relating to the conduct of the public&#x27;s business prepared, owned, used, received or retained by a public agency, or to which a public agency is entitled to receive a copy by law or contract under section 1-218, whether such data or information be handwritten, typed, tape-recorded, videotaped, printed, photostated, photographed or recorded by any other method.”_ | Conn. Gen. Stat. § 1-200(5) |

Gen. Stat. § 1-211(a) |

| `CT-0030` | structural | sets-deadline · undefined-soft: &quot;within a reasonable time after such disclosure&quot; for post-disclosure notice | **When an agency receives a personnel/medical-file request and reasonably believes disclosure would NOT invade privacy, it shall first disclose the records, then within a reasonable time make a reasonable attempt to send the employee(s) and their bargaining representative a copy or brief description of the request.**<br><br>_“Whenever a public agency receives a request to inspect or copy records contained in any of its employees&#x27; personnel or medical files and similar files, and the agency reasonably believes that the disclosure of such records would not legally constitute an invasion of privacy, the agency shall first disclose the requested records to the person making the request ... and subsequently, within a reasonable time after such disclosure, make a reasonable attempt to send a written or an electronic copy of the request ... or a brief description of such request, to each employee concerned and the collective bargaining representative, if any, of each employee concerned.”_ | Conn. Gen. Stat. § 1-214(b)(2) |

Gen. Stat. § 1-214(c) |

| `CT-0041` | structural | none | **When an agency receives a request that specifically names a person who has filed a confidentiality request for their residential address, the agency shall copy the record and redact that person&#x27;s residential address before disclosing it (reasonable-effort redaction for list-type requests).**<br><br>_“Any public agency that receives a request for a record subject to disclosure under this chapter where such request (A) specifically names a person who has requested that his or her address be kept confidential under subdivision (1) of this subsection, shall make a copy of the record requested to be disclosed and shall redact the copy to remove such person&#x27;s residential address prior to disclosing such record, (B) is for an existing list that is derived from a readily accessible electronic database, shall make a reasonable effort to redact the residential address ... prior to the release of such list, or (C) is for any list that the public agency voluntarily creates in response to a request for disclosure, shall make a reasonable effort to redact the residential address ... prior to the release of such list.”_ | Conn. Gen. Stat. § 1-217(a)(2) |

### Appeals

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CT-0033` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 30 calendar-days from the denial | **A person denied access (or any FOIA right) may appeal to the Freedom of Information Commission by filing a notice of appeal not later than thirty days after the denial.**<br><br>_“Any person denied the right to inspect or copy records under section 1-210 or wrongfully denied the right to attend any meeting of a public agency or denied any other right conferred by the Freedom of Information Act may appeal therefrom to the Freedom of Information Commission, by filing a notice of appeal with said commission. A notice of appeal shall be filed not later than thirty days after such denial, except in the case of an unnoticed or secret meeting, in which case the appeal shall be filed not later than thirty days after the person filing the appeal receives actual or constructive notice that such meeting was held.”_ | Conn. Gen. Stat. § 1-206(b)(1) |
| `CT-0034` | structural | none | **A notice of appeal is deemed filed on the date the commission receives it, or on the postmark date if received more than thirty days after the denial.**<br><br>_“For purposes of this subsection, such notice of appeal shall be deemed to be filed on the date it is received by said commission or on the date it is postmarked, if received more than thirty days after the date of the denial from which such appeal is taken.”_ | Conn. Gen. Stat. § 1-206(b)(1) |
| `CT-0035` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 1 year from filing of notice of appeal | **The Freedom of Information Commission shall, after due notice, hear and decide the appeal not later than one year after the filing of the notice of appeal.**<br><br>_“Said commission shall, after due notice to the parties, hear and decide the appeal not later than one year after the filing of the notice of appeal.”_ | Conn. Gen. Stat. § 1-206(b)(1) |
| `CT-0036` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · hearing within 30 days of notice receipt; decision within 60 days after the hearing | **For appeals the commission&#x27;s regulations designate as privileged, the appeal shall be heard not later than thirty days after receipt of the notice of appeal and decided not later than sixty days after the hearing.**<br><br>_“The commission shall adopt regulations in accordance with chapter 54, establishing criteria for those appeals which shall be privileged in their assignment for hearing. Any such appeal shall be heard not later than thirty days after receipt of a notice of appeal and decided not later than sixty days after the hearing.”_ | Conn. Gen. Stat. § 1-206(b)(1) |
| `CT-0037` | structural | none | **A party aggrieved by a decision of the Freedom of Information Commission may appeal to the Superior Court in accordance with the Uniform Administrative Procedure Act (§ 4-183).**<br><br>_“Any party aggrieved by the decision of said commission may appeal therefrom, in accordance with the provisions of section 4-183.”_ | Conn. Gen. Stat. § 1-206(d) |

### Review

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `CT-0038` | structural | terminal · relief period not to exceed one year during which future requests need not be processed | **A public agency may petition the FOI Commission for relief from a vexatious requester; upon granting the petition the commission may order that the agency need not comply with future requests from that requester for a specified period not exceeding one year.**<br><br>_“Upon a grant of such petition, the commission may provide appropriate relief commensurate with the vexatious conduct, including, but not limited to, an order that the agency need not comply with future requests from the vexatious requester for a specified period of time, but not to exceed one year.”_ | Conn. Gen. Stat. § 1-206(b)(6) |

### Material negatives

- `intake.acknowledgment_window` — Connecticut FOIA sets NO general acknowledgment/response deadline for granting access. The only fixed statutory window is the four-business-day (ten for personnel/medical) deadline to issue a DENIAL (§ 1-206(a)); grants are governed only by the soft &#x27;promptly&#x27; standard (§§ 1-210(a), 1-212(a)). Absence of a found rule is not proof none exists.
- `clarification.request_clarification_clock` — No statutory provision requires or authorizes a clarification/narrowing round-trip with a tolling clock. CT law is silent; agencies handle vague requests case-by-case (and may pursue vexatious-requester relief under § 1-206(b)(6)).
- `fee.commercial_purpose_surcharge` — Connecticut FOIA draws no commercial-vs-noncommercial fee distinction and defines no &#x27;commercial purpose&#x27;. Copy fees are the same per-page/cost ceilings regardless of requester purpose (§ 1-212).
- `fee.search_labor_charge` — No statute authorizes a separate search or staff-time fee for PAPER copies; the charge is per-page only (§ 1-212(a)). For computer-stored records, staff time for producing/formatting may be included but search/retrieval costs are expressly excluded except limited third-party computer-time charges (§ 1-212(b)).
- `search.reasonable_search_standard` — Chapter 14 contains no express &#x27;adequate/reasonable search&#x27; standard governing how an agency must search for responsive records; the obligation is implied through the access right and FOIC adjudication rather than a codified processing rule.
- `intake.submission_channel_requirement` — No statute prescribes a required submission channel or a mandatory request form for records requests generally; only that a request for a COPY be in writing (§ 1-212(a)). Inspection carries no writing requirement.
- `denials.written_denial_reason_requirement` — Section 1-206(a) requires a denial to be &#x27;in writing&#x27; within the deadline but does not, on its face, require the agency to state the statutory basis/reason for the denial; any reason-statement obligation arises in FOIC practice rather than the statute.
- `production.records_retention_hold_on_request` — No statute imposes an automatic litigation/preservation hold triggered by a records request or a pending FOIC appeal; retention is governed by separate records-management law (Conn. Gen. Stat. §§ 11-8 et seq.), outside Chapter 14&#x27;s request-processing rules.

### Structural branches

- `redaction.withhold_pending_objection` — Personnel/medical-file request enters a hold state once an employee or bargaining representative files an objection; agency cannot disclose until FOIC orders it (§ 1-214(c)).
- `redaction.personnel_privacy_notice` — Privacy-invasion branch: agency must notify affected employees and unions and pause disclosure pending the objection window (§ 1-214(b)(1)).
- `redaction.personnel_disclose_then_notify` — No-invasion branch: agency discloses first, then notifies employees within a reasonable time (§ 1-214(b)(2)).
- `classification.vexatious_requester_hold` — FOIC may relieve an agency from processing a requester&#x27;s future requests for up to one year on a vexatious-requester petition (§ 1-206(b)(6)).
- `routing.inmate_request_review` — Inmate/confined-person requests are routed to the Commissioner of Correction (or DMHAS) for exemption review before compliance (§ 1-210(c)).
- `routing.security_record_review` — Requests for § 1-210(b)(19) security records are routed to DAS/DESPP for withholding directive, redirecting who the appeal runs against (§ 1-210(d)).
- `appeal.administrative.expedited_hearing` — Privileged appeals follow an expedited 30-day-hearing / 60-day-decision track distinct from the general one-year track (§ 1-206(b)(1)).
- `appeal.judicial.foic_decision_review` — Court-review path for FOIC decisions via UAPA § 4-183, distinct from the internal FOIC appeal stage (§ 1-206(d)).
- `denials.constructive_denial_on_silence` — Non-response within the applicable business-day window converts the request into a deemed denial, opening the appeal path (§ 1-206(a)).
- `redaction.protected_address_on_request` — A request naming a person with a confidential-address filing forces a redaction step before disclosure (§ 1-217(a)(2)).
