## Florida (FL) — 30 rules

### Eligibility

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `FL-0001` | structural | none | **Every person has the right to inspect or copy any public record made or received in connection with the official business of any public body, officer, or employee of the state (or persons acting on their behalf), except records exempted under this section or made confidential by the Constitution.**<br><br>_“Every person has the right to inspect or copy any public record made or received in connection with the official business of any public body, officer, or employee of the state, or persons acting on their behalf, except with respect to records exempted pursuant to this section or specifically made confidential by this Constitution.”_ | Art. I, § 24(a), Fla. Const. |
| `FL-0002` | structural | none | **The right of access runs to &#x27;every person&#x27; / &#x27;any person&#x27; with no residency or citizenship qualification; a custodian may not condition access on the requester being a Florida resident.**<br><br>_“Every person has the right to inspect or copy any public record made or received in connection with the official business of any public body, officer, or employee of the state, or persons acting on their behalf ... [and] It is the policy of this state that all state, county, and municipal records are open for personal inspection and copying by any person.”_ | Art. I, § 24(a), Fla. Const.; § 119.01(1), Fla. Stat. |
| `FL-0003` | structural | none | **A custodian may not require the requester to state a reason or purpose for the request; the requester&#x27;s motivation does not affect the right of access.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“The motivation of the person seeking the records does not impact the person&#x27;s right to see them under the Public Records Act.”_ | Curry v. State, 811 So. 2d 736 (Fla. 4th DCA 2002); Lorei v. Smith, 464 So. 2d 1330 (Fla. 2d DCA 1985) |

### Intake

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `FL-0004` | structural | none | **A custodian may not require that a public-records request be made in writing, on a particular form, or in person, and may not require the requester to disclose their identity as a condition of access.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“Requesters are not required to provide a reason for their request. They are also not required to disclose their identity or submit the request in writing.”_ | Interpreting § 119.07(1)(a), Fla. Stat.; Fla. AG guidance / Government-in-the-Sunshine Manual |
| `FL-0005` | structural | none | **All state, county, and municipal records are open for personal inspection and copying by any person unless a specific exemption applies.**<br><br>_“It is the policy of this state that all state, county, and municipal records are open for personal inspection and copying by any person.”_ | § 119.01(1), Fla. Stat. |

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |

| `FL-0006` | structural | none | **As an agency increases its use of electronic recordkeeping it must provide reasonable public access to records electronically maintained and must ensure exempt/confidential records are not disclosed except as permitted by law.**<br><br>_“Automation of public records must not erode the right of access to those records. As each agency increases its use of and dependence on electronic recordkeeping, each agency must provide reasonable public access to records electronically maintained and must ensure that exempt or confidential records are not disclosed except as otherwise permitted by law.”_ | § 119.01(2)(a), Fla. Stat. |
| `FL-0016` | structural | none | **An agency must provide a copy of a record in the medium requested if it maintains the record in that medium; providing a copy in a medium not routinely used, or compiling/manipulating data, is charged under s. 119.07(4).**<br><br>_“An agency must provide a copy of the record in the medium requested if the agency maintains the record in that medium, and the agency may charge a fee in accordance with this chapter. Stat. |

### Special Records

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `FL-0030` | structural | sets-deadline · 30 calendar-days from the date the written request to inspect or copy was served on or made to the custodian | **Even if the custodian asserts a requested record is not a public record subject to inspection, the record must not be disposed of for 30 days after a written request to inspect or copy it was served on or made to the custodian.**<br><br>_“Even if an assertion is made by the custodian of public records that a requested record is not a public record subject to public inspection or copying under this subsection, the requested record shall, nevertheless, not be disposed of for a period of 30 days after the date on which a written request to inspect or copy the record was served on or otherwise made to the custodian of public records by the person seeking access to the record.”_ | § 119.07(1)(h), Fla. Stat. |

### Material negatives

- `intake.acknowledgment_window` — Florida sets NO fixed numeric acknowledgment or response deadline. The only standards are &#x27;promptly&#x27;/&#x27;good faith&#x27; (§ 119.07(1)(c)) and the &#x27;limited reasonable time&#x27; of Tribune Co. v. Cannella. Captured as soft-standard rules FL-0007 and FL-0014, not numeric parameters. Absence of a day-count is itself the finding.
- `payment.advance_deposit` — Chapter 119 contains NO explicit statutory deposit/advance-payment provision. AG opinions and practice permit requiring payment of estimated fees before producing copies, but no codified clock or threshold was found. Deferred to an AG-opinion pass; not emitted as a rule to avoid asserting a statute that does not exist.
- `clarification.request_specificity` — No statute imposes a mandatory clarification stage or a nonresponse-abandonment clock. Case law recognizes that overbroad requests may be met with a request to narrow, but no explicit statutory clarification clock or deemed-abandonment trigger was located. Deferred to case-law/AG pass.
- `appeal.administrative.internal_review` — Florida provides NO internal administrative appeal stage and no statewide AG administrative-review adjudication for records denials. Enforcement is judicial (civil action / mandamus). Emitted affirmatively as the judicial path FL-0029; the absence of an administrative appeal is a structural fact, not a gap to fill with a value.
- `request.tracking_number` — No statutory requirement that agencies assign or track a request number, log requests, or report request metrics for an individual municipal request. Nothing for the software to enforce here.
- `fee.free_first_pages_or_de_minimis` — No statutory free-copy allowance or de minimis waiver threshold was found; the fee schedule (§ 119.07(4)) applies from the first copy. Absence noted; cities may adopt their own waiver policy.

### Structural branches

- `appeal.judicial.in_camera_inspection` — FL-0029: Florida&#x27;s exemption dispute path is judicial — a civil action with mandatory in camera inspection for enumerated exemptions. There is NO statutory internal/administrative appeal stage; the engine must express a court-review fork rather than an appeal.administrative.* stage.
- `preservation.hold_on_disputed_public_record_status` — FL-0030: a distinct state the request enters — a 30-day no-disposal hold on the record whenever a written request is made and the custodian disputes the record&#x27;s public status.
- `deadlines.production_reasonable_time` — FL-0014: production timing is a soft &#x27;limited reasonable time&#x27; standard the engine applies, not a city-filled numeric window — a structural deadline path distinct from a parameter value.
- `intake.acknowledgment_window` — FL-0007: acknowledgment is governed by the soft standard &#x27;promptly&#x27; — a structural deadline the engine applies, with no numeric value to configure.
- `denials.constructive_denial_unjustified_delay` — FL-0028: the request can transition to a constructive-denial (unlawful refusal) state once delay exceeds the reasonable time — a terminal branch triggered by a soft standard, not a fixed clock.
- `routing.contractor_provide_on_request` — FL-0011..FL-0013: when a contractor holds agency records, a distinct routing/production sub-path applies (maintain, provide within reasonable time at chapter cost, transfer/keep at contract completion).
- `redaction.redact_and_produce_remainder` — FL-0017..FL-0019: mandatory segregability path — assert exemption with statutory citation, redact, produce remainder, and generate written particularized reasons on request.

## Georgia (GA) — 41 rules

### Intake

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `GA-0001` | structural | none | **All public records are open for personal inspection and copying by any person, except records specifically exempted by court order or by law; no requester qualification is imposed for general access.**<br><br>_“All public records shall be open for personal inspection and copying, except those which by order of a court of this state or by law are specifically exempted from disclosure. Records shall be maintained by agencies to the extent and in the manner required by Article 5 of this chapter.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(a) |
| `GA-0002` | structural | none | **A requester need not state a reason or purpose to obtain general public records; a purpose/&#x27;need&#x27; showing is required only for enumerated special record types (e.g., motor vehicle accident reports).** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“The general right in O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(a)-(b) imposes no purpose requirement; a &#x27;need&#x27; showing is imposed only for specific record types (see O.C.G.A. § 50-18-72(a)(5), motor vehicle accident reports, which defines &#x27;need&#x27; by enumerated categories).”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(a)-(b); contrast § 50-18-72(a)(5) |
| `GA-0003` | structural | none | **There is no Georgia residency or citizenship condition on the right to request public records; the right runs to any person.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“The Open Records Act conditions access on the record&#x27;s public status, not the requester&#x27;s residency; no residency or citizenship qualification appears in O.C.G.A. § 50-18-70 or § 50-18-71.”_ | O.C.G.A. §§ 50-18-70, 50-18-71 |
| `GA-0004` | structural | none | **A request may be made to the custodian orally or in writing; an oral request is a valid intake path (though it does not unlock the statutory enforcement remedies — see GA-0007).**<br><br>_“A request made pursuant to this article may be made to the custodian of a public record orally or in writing.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(b)(1)(B) |
| `GA-0006` | structural | none | **An agency shall permit receipt of written requests by e-mail or facsimile in addition to other approved methods, provided the agency uses e-mail or facsimile in the normal course of its business.**<br><br>_“An agency shall permit receipt of written requests by e-mail or facsimile transmission in addition to any other methods of transmission approved by the agency, provided such agency uses e-mail or facsimile in the normal course of its business.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(b)(2) |

### Custody/Routing

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `GA-0005` | structural | none | **An agency may (but is not obligated to) require that all written requests be directed to a designated official chosen from an enumerated list (director/chair/CEO, senior satellite official, designated records custodian clerk, or designated open records officer).**<br><br>_“An agency may, but shall not be obligated to, require that all written requests be made upon the responder&#x27;s choice of one of the following: the agency&#x27;s director, chairperson, or chief executive officer, however denominated; the senior official at any satellite office of an agency; a clerk specifically designated by an agency as the custodian of agency records; or a duly designated open records officer of an agency.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(b)(1)(B) |
| `GA-0008` | structural | none | **An agency that designates one or more open records officers shall make the designation in writing, immediately provide notice of them to any person on request (orally or in writing), notify the county legal organ, and, if it has a website, prominently display the designation.**<br><br>_“Any agency that designates one or more open records officers upon whom requests for inspection or copying of records may be delivered shall make such designation in writing and shall immediately provide notice to any person upon request, orally or in writing, of those open records officers. If the agency has elected to designate an open records officer, the agency shall so notify the legal organ of the county in which the agency&#x27;s principal offices reside and, if the agency has a website, shall also prominently display such designation on the agency&#x27;s website.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(b)(2) |
| `GA-0009` | structural | none | **The absence or unavailability of the designated agency officer or employee may not be permitted to delay the agency&#x27;s response to a request.**<br><br>_“provided, however, that the absence or unavailability of the designated agency officer or employee shall not be permitted to delay the agency&#x27;s response.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(b)(1)(B) |
| `GA-0040` | structural | none | **Requests by civil litigants for records sought for use in ongoing civil or administrative litigation against the agency shall be made in writing and copied to the agency&#x27;s counsel of record contemporaneously; the agency shall provide duplicate sets of produced records to that counsel at no cost unless counsel declines.**<br><br>_“Requests by civil litigants for records that are sought as part of or for use in any ongoing civil or administrative litigation against an agency shall be made in writing and copied to counsel of record for that agency contemporaneously with their submission to that agency. The agency shall provide, at no cost, duplicate sets of all records produced in response to the request to counsel of record for that agency unless the counsel of record for that agency elects not to receive the records.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(e) |

This classifies whether a request is enforcement-eligible.**<br><br>_“The enforcement provisions of Code Sections 50-18-73 and 50-18-74 shall be available only to enforce compliance and punish noncompliance when a written request is made consistent with this subsection and shall not be available when such request is made orally.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(b)(3) |

### Deadlines

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `GA-0010` | structural | sets-deadline · 3 business-day response clock does not start until written request is delivered to the required designated individual | **Where an agency requires that requests be directed to designated individuals, the three-day response period does not begin to run until the request is made in writing upon such individuals.**<br><br>_“In the event an agency requires that requests be made upon the individuals identified in subparagraph (B) of paragraph (1) of this subsection, the three-day period for response to a written request shall not begin to run until the request is made in writing upon such individuals.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(b)(2) |
| `GA-0011` | parameter · ceiling | sets-deadline · ceiling: 3 business-days from receipt of request | **An agency shall produce responsive records within a reasonable amount of time not to exceed three business days of receipt of the request; the agency is not required to produce records that did not exist at the time of the request.**<br><br>_“Agencies shall produce for inspection all records responsive to a request within a reasonable amount of time not to exceed three business days of receipt of a request; provided, however, that nothing in this chapter shall require agencies to produce records in response to a request if such records did not exist at the time of the request.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(b)(1)(A) |
| `GA-0039` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · fixed: 90 business-days from date agency received the request | **For records (other than nonclerical staff salary information) of intercollegiate sports programs of any University System of Georgia unit, the period for any production, access, response, or notice is 90 business days from the date the agency received the request.**<br><br>_“Any other provision of this Code section to the contrary notwithstanding, the period within which any production, access, response, or notice is required from an agency with respect to a request for records, other than salary information for nonclerical staff, of intercollegiate sports programs of any unit of the University System of Georgia, including athletic departments and related private athletic associations, shall be 90 business days from the date the agency received the request.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(d.1) |

### Production

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `GA-0012` | structural | sets-deadline · 3 business-days from receipt of request (for the subset that can be located) | **Where some but not all responsive records are available within three business days, the agency shall make available within that period those records that can be located and produced.**<br><br>_“In those instances where some, but not all, records are available within three business days, an agency shall make available within that period those records that can be located and produced.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(b)(1)(A) |
| `GA-0014` | structural | sets-deadline · undefined-soft: &quot;as soon as practicable&quot; from receipt of request | **For records that could not be produced within three business days, the agency shall provide the responsive records or access to them as soon as practicable (a soft, undefined standard, not a fixed day count).**<br><br>_“and provide the responsive records or access thereto as soon as practicable.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(b)(1)(A) |
| `GA-0028` | structural | none | **An agency may, in its discretion, provide copies of a record in lieu of providing access to the record when portions contain confidential information that must be redacted.**<br><br>_“Notwithstanding any other provision of this chapter, an agency may, in its discretion, provide copies of a record in lieu of providing access to the record when portions of the record contain confidential information that must be redacted.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(b)(1)(B) |
| `GA-0029` | structural | none | **No public officer or agency is required to prepare new reports, summaries, or compilations not in existence at the time of the request.**<br><br>_“No public officer or agency shall be required to prepare new reports, summaries, or compilations not in existence at the time of the request.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(j) |
| `GA-0030` | structural | none | **A requester may request that electronic records, data, or data fields be produced in the format in which the agency keeps them, or in a standard export format such as a flat-file ASCII format, if the agency&#x27;s existing computer programs support such export; and the agency shall produce electronic copies or, if the requester prefers, printouts.**<br><br>_“A requester may request that electronic records, data, or data fields be produced in the format in which such data or electronic records are kept by the agency, or in a standard export format such as a flat file electronic American Standard Code for Information Interchange (ASCII) format, if the agency&#x27;s existing computer programs support such an export format. In such instance, the data or electronic records shall be downloaded in such format onto suitable electronic media by the agency.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(f) |
| `GA-0031` | structural | none | **An agency shall not refuse to produce electronic records, data, or data fields on the ground that exporting or redacting requires inputting range, search, filter, or report commands, so long as those commands can be executed using existing programs the agency uses in the ordinary course of business.**<br><br>_“An agency shall not refuse to produce such electronic records, data, or data fields on the grounds that exporting data or redaction of exempted information will require inputting range, search, filter, report parameters, or similar commands or instructions into an agency&#x27;s computer system so long as such commands or instructions can be executed using existing computer programs that the agency uses in the ordinary course of business to access, support, or otherwise manage the records or data.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(f) |
| `GA-0032` | structural | none | **An agency may provide access to records through a public website in lieu of separate printouts or copies, but if it receives a request for data fields it shall not refuse the responsive data on the ground that it is available through a website when the requester seeks the data in the electronic format in which it is kept.**<br><br>_“In lieu of providing separate printouts or copies of records or data, an agency may provide access to records through a website accessible by the public. However, if an agency receives a request for data fields, an agency shall not refuse to provide the responsive data on the grounds that the data is available in whole or in its constituent parts through a website if the requester seeks the data in the electronic format in which it is kept.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(h) |
| `GA-0033` | structural | none | **If an agency contracts with a private vendor to collect or maintain public records, the agency shall ensure the arrangement does not limit public access and that the vendor does not impede record access or the method of delivery established by the agency or by law.**<br><br>_“if an agency contracts with a private vendor to collect or maintain public records, the agency shall ensure that the arrangement does not limit public access to those records and that the vendor does not impede public record access and method of delivery as established by the agency or as otherwise provided for in this Code section.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(h) |

| `GA-0015` | structural | sets-deadline · within 3 business-days of request, or within 3 business-days after retrieval where search/retrieval is delayed | **When an agency withholds all or part of a record, it shall notify the requester of the specific legal authority for the exemption by Code section, subsection, and paragraph within three business days (or, if search/retrieval is delayed, no later than three business days after retrieval).**<br><br>_“In any instance in which an agency is required to or has decided to withhold all or part of a requested record, the agency shall notify the requester of the specific legal authority exempting the requested record or records from disclosure by Code section, subsection, and paragraph within a reasonable amount of time not to exceed three business days or in the event the search and retrieval of records is delayed pursuant to this subsection or pursuant to subparagraph (b)(1)(A) of this Code section, then no later than three business days after the records have been retrieved.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(d) |

### Fees

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `GA-0016` | parameter · ceiling | none | **An agency may impose a reasonable charge for search, retrieval, and redaction, but that charge shall not exceed the prorated hourly salary of the lowest paid full-time employee who, in the custodian&#x27;s reasonable discretion, has the necessary skill and training to perform the request.**<br><br>_“In all other instances, the charge for the search, retrieval, or redaction of records shall not exceed the prorated hourly salary of the lowest paid full-time employee who, in the reasonable discretion of the custodian of the records, has the necessary skill and training to perform the request; provided, however, that no charge shall be made for the first quarter hour.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(c)(1) |
| `GA-0017` | parameter · fixed | none | **No labor charge may be made for the first quarter hour of search, retrieval, or redaction.**<br><br>_“provided, however, that no charge shall be made for the first quarter hour.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(c)(1) |
| `GA-0018` | parameter · ceiling | none | **In addition to labor charges, an agency may charge for copying, not to exceed 10 cents per page for letter or legal size documents, or the actual cost of producing the copy for other document types.**<br><br>_“In addition to a charge for the search, retrieval, or redaction of records, an agency may charge a fee for the copying of records or data, not to exceed 10¢ per page for letter or legal size documents or, in the case of other documents, the actual cost of producing the copy.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(c)(2) |
| `GA-0019` | parameter · fixed | none | **For electronic records, the agency may charge the actual cost of the media on which the records or data are produced.**<br><br>_“In the case of electronic records, the agency may charge the actual cost of the media on which the records or data are produced.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(c)(2) |
| `GA-0021` | structural | none | **Where a specific fee for certified copies or other records is authorized or prescribed by law, that specific fee applies instead of the general search/retrieval/copy charges when such records are sought.**<br><br>_“Where fees for certified copies or other copies or records are specifically authorized or otherwise prescribed by law, such specific fee shall apply when certified copies or other records to which a specific fee may apply are sought.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(c)(1) |

| `GA-0022` | structural | sets-deadline · estimate notice due within 3 business-days of receipt of request | **Where an agency will seek costs in excess of $25.00 to respond, it shall notify the requester within three business days and inform the requester of the cost estimate.**<br><br>_“In any instance in which an agency will seek costs in excess of $25.00 for responding to a request, the agency shall notify the requester within a reasonable amount of time not to exceed three business days and inform the requester of the estimate of the costs, and the agency may defer search and retrieval of the records until the requester agrees to pay the estimated costs unless the requester has stated in his or her request a willingness to pay an amount that exceeds the search and retrieval costs.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(d) |
| `GA-0023` | structural | pauses · processing paused until requester agrees to pay the estimated costs | **The agency may defer search and retrieval until the requester agrees to pay the estimated costs, unless the request already stated a willingness to pay an amount exceeding the search and retrieval costs.**<br><br>_“and the agency may defer search and retrieval of the records until the requester agrees to pay the estimated costs unless the requester has stated in his or her request a willingness to pay an amount that exceeds the search and retrieval costs.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(d) |
| `GA-0024` | structural | pauses · processing paused pending prepayment when estimate exceeds $500.00 | **Where the estimated production cost exceeds $500.00, the agency may insist on prepayment of the costs before beginning search, retrieval, review, or production.**<br><br>_“In any instance in which the estimated costs for production of the records exceeds $500.00, an agency may insist on prepayment of the costs prior to beginning search, retrieval, review, or production of the records.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(d) |
| `GA-0025` | structural | pauses · future requests paused pending prepayment until prior costs paid or dispute resolved | **Where a requester has not paid costs lawfully incurred for a prior request, the agency may require prepayment for all future requests from that person until the prior costs are paid or the payment dispute is resolved.**<br><br>_“Whenever any person who has requested to inspect or copy a public record has not paid the cost for search, retrieval, redaction, or copying of such records when such charges have been lawfully incurred, an agency may require prepayment for compliance with all future requests for production of records from that person until the costs for the prior production of records have been paid or the dispute regarding payment resolved.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(d) |
| `GA-0026` | structural | none | **Where charges were lawfully estimated and agreed and the agency has incurred the agreed-upon costs to make records available, the agency may collect those charges (via tax/fee collection methods) regardless of whether the requester inspects or accepts the records.**<br><br>_“Whenever any person has requested to inspect or copy a public record and does not pay the cost for search, retrieval, redaction, or copying of such records when such charges have been lawfully estimated and agreed to pursuant to this article, and the agency has incurred the agreed-upon costs to make the records available, regardless of whether the requester inspects or accepts copies of the records, the agency shall be authorized to collect such charges in any manner authorized by law for the collection of taxes, fees, or assessments by such agency.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-71(c)(3) |

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `GA-0034` | structural | none | **Exemptions are interpreted narrowly to exclude only the portion of a record to which an exclusion is directly applicable, and the agency having custody has a duty to provide all other portions of the record for inspection or copying.**<br><br>_“This Code section shall be interpreted narrowly so as to exclude from disclosure only that portion of a public record to which an exclusion is directly applicable. It shall be the duty of the agency having custody of a record to provide all other portions of a record for public inspection or copying.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-72(b) |
| `GA-0035` | structural | none | **Enumerated personal identifiers (e.g., social security number, financial/bank/credit-card/account data, personal e-mail or cell number, day and month of birth, medical/insurance information, and similar items) shall be redacted prior to disclosure of any requested record, subject to statutory exceptions.**<br><br>_“Records that reveal an individual&#x27;s social security number, mother&#x27;s birth name, credit card information, debit card information, bank account information, account number, utility account number, password used to access his or her account, financial data or information, insurance or medical information in all records, unlisted telephone number if so designated in a public record, personal e-mail address or cellular telephone number, day and month of birth ... Items exempted by this subparagraph shall be redacted prior to disclosure of any record requested pursuant to this article.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-72(a)(20)(A) |
| `GA-0041` | structural | sets-deadline · 10 days notice of intent to disclose to the submitting entity before release | **Where a submitter has attached a trade-secret affidavit to records, the agency shall notify the submitter before producing them; if the agency determines the information is not a trade secret it shall notify the submitter of its intent to disclose within ten days unless barred by court order (the submitter may then sue to prevent disclosure).**<br><br>_“If such entity attaches such an affidavit, before producing such records in response to a request under this article, the agency shall notify the entity of its intention to produce such records as set forth in this paragraph. If the agency makes a determination that the specifically identified information does not in fact constitute a trade secret, it shall notify the entity submitting the affidavit of its intent to disclose the information within ten days unless prohibited from doing so by an appropriate court order.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-72(a)(34) |

Such actions may be brought by any person, firm, corporation, or other entity.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-73(a) |

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |

| `GA-0037` | structural | none | **The Attorney General has discretionary authority to bring enforcement actions to compel compliance with the Open Records Act and to seek civil or criminal penalties or both.**<br><br>_“In addition, the Attorney General shall have authority to bring such actions in his or her discretion as may be appropriate to enforce compliance with this article and to seek either civil or criminal penalties or both.”_ | O.C.G.A. § 50-18-73(a) |

### Material negatives

- `intake.acknowledgment_window` — No separate statutory acknowledgment/receipt deadline distinct from substantive response. Georgia&#x27;s three-business-day period (50-18-71(b)(1)(A)) is a production/response ceiling, not a mere acknowledgment obligation; there is no standalone &#x27;acknowledge within N days&#x27; rule.
- `denials.constructive_denial_clock` — No statutory deemed/constructive-denial trigger (no &#x27;silence for N days = denial&#x27;). Georgia enforcement is judicial and turns on noncompliance/no substantial justification (50-18-73), not an automatic terminal clock. Absence of a found rule is not proof none exists.
- `appeal.administrative.internal_review` — No statutory administrative appeal to a state ombudsman or records authority. Review is judicial (superior court, 50-18-73) plus discretionary Attorney General enforcement; the AG&#x27;s mediation program is informal and non-statutory.
- `clarification.overbroad_request_clock` — No statutory clarification/narrowing clock and no requester-clarification-then-abandonment (nonresponse-abandonment) mechanism in the Open Records Act.
- `tracking.request_log_requirement` — No statewide statutory requirement to maintain a public request log or assign tracking numbers for open records requests.
- `fee.municipal_flat_rate` — No statewide-mandated flat municipal copy or labor rate. The state supplies only ceilings (10 cents/page for letter/legal; lowest-paid qualified employee&#x27;s prorated hourly salary for labor) and a fixed 15-minute free increment; the city fills in the operative values under those ceilings.
- `deadlines.time_calculation_method` — The Act counts in &#x27;business days&#x27; for the core response window but does not codify a general holiday/weekend computation method or a rule for when receipt occurs (e.g., after-hours or weekend submissions); computation details are left unspecified. Manual verification / later AG-opinion pass recommended.

### Structural branches

- `routing.open_records_officer_designation` — Optional open-records-officer regime: agency may designate officer(s) in writing, must publicize (county legal organ + website), and may require directed written requests (GA-0005/GA-0008).
- `deadlines.clock_start_on_written_to_designee` — Conditional clock-start fork: when the agency requires directed requests, the 3-business-day clock does not start until the written request reaches the required designee (GA-0010).
- `deadlines.unavailable_description_and_timeline_notice` — Delayed-records path: description + timeline notice within 3 business days, then production &#x27;as soon as practicable&#x27; (soft standard) (GA-0013/GA-0014).
- `payment.cost_estimate_and_deferral` — Cost-estimate/deferral branch at the fixed $25 threshold (estimate + optional deferral) and prepayment branch at the fixed $500 threshold, plus a delinquent-requester prepayment state (GA-0022/GA-0023/GA-0024/GA-0025).
- `production.trade_secret_third_party_notice` — Reverse-FOIA hold: third-party submitter notice, 10-day intent-to-disclose window, and potential submitter court action before release (GA-0041).
- `routing.civil_litigant_copy_to_counsel` — Civil-litigant routing: written request copied to agency counsel and duplicate record sets delivered to counsel at no cost (GA-0040).
- `appeal.judicial.superior_court_enforcement` — Judicial enforcement path in superior court, available only for written requests, brought by any person or by the Attorney General (GA-0036/GA-0037).
- `deadlines.record_type_extended_window` — Record-type fork: 90-business-day response window for University System of Georgia intercollegiate athletics records, overriding the general 3-day rule (GA-0039) (non-municipal).

## Illinois (IL) — 48 rules

### Intake

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `IL-0001` | structural | none | **A public body must make all public records available to any person for inspection or copying, except records exempt under Sections 7 and 8.5.**<br><br>_“Each public body shall make available to any person for inspection or copying all public records, except as otherwise provided in Sections 7 and 8.5 of this Act.”_ | 5 ILCS 140/3(a) |
| `IL-0002` | structural | none | **A public body may not require a requester to state the purpose of a request, except to determine commercial-purpose status or to rule on a fee-waiver request.**<br><br>_“A public body may not require that a request be submitted on a standard form or require the requester to specify the purpose for a request, except to determine whether the records are requested for a commercial purpose or whether to grant a request for a fee waiver.”_ | 5 ILCS 140/3(c) |
| `IL-0003` | structural | none | **Any person may request public records; the Act imposes no residency or citizenship condition on the right of access.**<br><br>_“Each public body shall make available to any person for inspection or copying all public records, except as otherwise provided in Sections 7 and 8.5 of this Act.”_ | 5 ILCS 140/3(a) |
| `IL-0004` | structural | none | **A public body may not require that a request be submitted on a standard form.**<br><br>_“A public body may not require that a request be submitted on a standard form or require the requester to specify the purpose for a request, except to determine whether the records are requested for a commercial purpose or whether to grant a request for a fee waiver.”_ | 5 ILCS 140/3(c) |
| `IL-0005` | structural | none | **A public body may honor oral requests for inspection or copying.**<br><br>_“A public body may honor oral requests for inspection or copying.”_ | 5 ILCS 140/3(c) |

### Custody/Routing

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `IL-0006` | structural | none | **Each public body must designate one or more officials or employees to act as its Freedom of Information officer(s).**<br><br>_“Each public body shall designate one or more officials or employees to act as its Freedom of Information officer or officers.”_ | 5 ILCS 140/3.5(a) |

### Deadlines

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `IL-0007` | structural | sets-deadline · start event = date written request received; due-date computed per Section 3(d)/(e) | **Upon receiving a request, the FOIA officer must note the date the public body received the written request and compute and record the date the response period expires.**<br><br>_“Upon receiving a request for a public record, the Freedom of Information officer shall: (1) note the date the public body receives the written request; (2) compute the day on which the period for response will expire and make a notation of that date on the written request...”_ | 5 ILCS 140/3.5(b) |
| `IL-0010` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 5 business-days from request receipt | **A public body must comply with or deny a written request within 5 business days after receipt, unless the time is properly extended.**<br><br>_“Each public body shall, promptly, either comply with or deny a request for public records within 5 business days after its receipt of the request, unless the time for response is properly extended under subsection (e) of this Section.”_ | 5 ILCS 140/3(d) |
| `IL-0011` | parameter · ceiling | sets-deadline · ceiling: up to 5 business-days beyond original due date | **A public body may extend the response time by not more than 5 business days beyond the original due date for any of seven enumerated reasons.**<br><br>_“The time for response under this Section may be extended by the public body for not more than 5 business days from the original due date for any of the following reasons: (i) the requested records are stored in whole or in part at other locations than the office having charge of the requested records; (ii) the request requires the collection of a substantial number of specified records; (iii) the request is couched in categorical terms and requires an extensive search for the records responsive to it; (iv) the requested records have not been located in the course of routine search and additional efforts are being made to locate them; (v) the requested records require examination and evaluation by personnel having the necessary competence and discretion to determine if they are exempt from disclosure under Section 7 of this Act or should be revealed only with appropriate deletions; (vi) the request for records cannot be complied with by the public body within the time limits prescribed by subsection (d) of this Section without unduly burdening or interfering with the operations of the public body; (vii) there is a need for consultation, which shall be conducted with all practicable speed, with another public body or among 2 or more components of a public body having a substantial interest in the determination or in the subject matter of the request.”_ | 5 ILCS 140/3(e) |
| `IL-0013` | structural | sets-deadline · period agreed in writing by the parties (no statutory cap) | **The requester and the public body may agree in writing to extend the time for compliance for a period determined by the parties.**<br><br>_“The person making a request and the public body may agree in writing to extend the time for compliance for a period to be determined by the parties.”_ | 5 ILCS 140/3(e) |
| `IL-0017` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 21 working-days from request receipt | **A public body must respond to a request for records to be used for a commercial purpose within 21 working days after receipt.**<br><br>_“A public body shall respond to a request for records to be used for a commercial purpose within 21 working days after receipt.”_ | 5 ILCS 140/3.1(a) |
| `IL-0020` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 21 business-days from request receipt | **A public body must respond to a request from a recurrent requester within 21 business days after receipt.**<br><br>_“a public body shall respond to a request from a recurrent requester...within 21 business days after receipt”_ | 5 ILCS 140/3.2(a) |
| `IL-0024` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 5 business-days from request receipt | **A public body must respond to a voluminous request within 5 business days after receipt (notifying the requester it is being treated as voluminous).**<br><br>_“a public body shall respond to a voluminous request within 5 business days after receipt.”_ | 5 ILCS 140/3.6(a) |
| `IL-0025` | structural | sets-deadline · 10 business-days from the public body&#x27;s response being sent | **After the public body&#x27;s voluminous-request response, the requester must respond within 10 business days; if the requester does not respond (or the request remains voluminous), the public body responds and assesses any fees.**<br><br>_“the requester must respond to the public body within 10 business days after the public body&#x27;s response was sent”_ | 5 ILCS 140/3.6 |
| `IL-0026` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · earlier of 5 business-days from requester&#x27;s response OR 5 business-days from final day for requester to respond | **The public body must give its final voluminous-request response within the earlier of 5 business days after receiving the requester&#x27;s response or 5 business days after the final day for the requester to respond.**<br><br>_“the public body shall respond within the earlier of 5 business days after it receives the response from the requester or 5 business days after the final day for the requester to respond”_ | 5 ILCS 140/3.6(c) |

### Classification

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `IL-0008` | structural | none | **All records in the custody or possession of a public body are presumed open to inspection or copying.**<br><br>_“All records in the custody or possession of a public body are presumed to be open to inspection or copying.”_ | 5 ILCS 140/1.2 |
| `IL-0022` | structural | none | **A requester is a recurrent requester if, in the prior 12 months, it submitted to the same public body at least 50 requests, or at least 15 within a 30-day period, or at least 7 within a 7-day period.**<br><br>_“&quot;Recurrent requester&quot;, as used in Section 3.2 of this Act, means a person that, in the 12 months immediately preceding the request, has submitted to the same public body (i) a minimum of 50 requests for records, (ii) a minimum of 15 requests for records within a 30-day period, or (iii) a minimum of 7 requests for records within a 7-day period.”_ | 5 ILCS 140/2(g) |
| `IL-0023` | structural | none | **A request is voluminous if it includes more than 5 individual requests for more than 5 different categories of records within 20 business days, or requires compiling more than 500 letter/legal-sized pages (unless a single record exceeds 500 pages).**<br><br>_“&quot;Voluminous request&quot; means a request that: (i) includes more than 5 individual requests for more than 5 different categories of records or a combination of individual requests that total requests for more than 5 different categories of records in a period of 20 business days; or (ii) requires the compilation of more than 500 letter or legal-sized pages of public records unless a single requested record exceeds 500 pages.”_ | 5 ILCS 140/2(h) |

Waiver or reduction of the fee is in the public interest if the principal purpose of the request is to access and disseminate information regarding the health, safety and welfare or the legal rights of the general public and is not for the principal purpose of personal or commercial benefit.”_ | 5 ILCS 140/6(c) |
| `IL-0035` | parameter · ceiling | none | **For a commercial request, a public body may charge up to $10 for each hour spent by personnel searching for, retrieving, or examining the record for necessary redactions.**<br><br>_“A public body may charge up to $10 for each hour spent by personnel in searching for and retrieving a requested record or examining the record for necessary redactions.”_ | 5 ILCS 140/6(f) |
| `IL-0036` | parameter · fixed | none | **No fee may be charged for the first 8 hours spent by personnel searching for or retrieving a requested record on a commercial request.**<br><br>_“No fees shall be charged for the first 8 hours spent by personnel in searching for or retrieving a requested record.”_ | 5 ILCS 140/6(f) |
| `IL-0038` | parameter · ceiling | none | **For a voluminous request for electronic records, a public body may charge tiered caps by data size: for non-PDF data up to $20 (&lt;=2 MB), $40 (2-4 MB), or $100 (&gt;4 MB); for PDF data up to $20 (&lt;=80 MB), $40 (80-160 MB), or $100 (&gt;160 MB).**<br><br>_“If a voluminous request is for electronic records and those records are not in a portable document format (PDF), the public body may charge up to $20 for not more than 2 megabytes of data, up to $40 for more than 2 but not more than 4 megabytes of data, and up to $100 for more than 4 megabytes of data. [For PDF:] up to $20 for not more than 80 megabytes of data, up to $40 for more than 80 megabytes but not more than 160 megabytes of data, and up to $100 for more than 160 megabytes of data.”_ | 5 ILCS 140/6(a-5) |

| `IL-0044` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 60 days from date of final denial | **A request for review with the PAC must be filed not later than 60 days after the date of the final denial.**<br><br>_“...may file a request for review with the Public Access Counselor established in the Office of the Attorney General not later than 60 days after the date of the final denial.”_ | 5 ILCS 140/9.5(a) |
| `IL-0045` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 7 business-days from receipt of request for review | **Within 7 business days after receiving the request for review, the public body must provide copies of the requested records to the PAC and otherwise fully cooperate.**<br><br>_“Within 7 business days after receipt of the request for review, the public body shall provide copies of records requested and shall otherwise fully cooperate with the Public Access Counselor.”_ | 5 ILCS 140/9.5(c) |
| `IL-0046` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 60 days from receipt of request for review | **The Attorney General must examine the issues and records, make findings of fact and conclusions of law, and issue a binding opinion within 60 days after receipt of the request for review.**<br><br>_“the Attorney General shall examine the issues and the records, shall make findings of fact and conclusions of law, and shall issue to the requester and the public body an opinion in response to the request for review within 60 days after its receipt. The opinion shall be binding upon both the requester and the public body.”_ | 5 ILCS 140/9.5(f) |
| `IL-0047` | structural | none | **Any person denied access to inspect or copy a public record may file suit for injunctive or declaratory relief in circuit court.**<br><br>_“Any person denied access to inspect or copy any public record by a public body may file suit for injunctive or declaratory relief.”_ | 5 ILCS 140/11(a) |
| `IL-0048` | structural | none | **Suit against a State public body may be filed in the circuit court for the county where the public body has its principal office or where the person denied access resides.**<br><br>_“Where the denial is from a public body of the State, suit may be filed in the circuit court for the county where the public body has its principal office or where the person denied access resides.”_ | 5 ILCS 140/11(b) |

### Material negatives

- `intake.acknowledgment_window` — No separate acknowledgment-of-receipt deadline distinct from the substantive response. Illinois folds acknowledgment into the 5-business-day comply-or-deny window (Section 3(d)); there is no statutory duty to send a bare receipt confirmation on a shorter clock. Absence of a found rule is not proof none exists.
- `clarification.ambiguity_clock` — No general clarification clock that tolls or restarts the response deadline when a request is ambiguous. The only statutory clarification mechanism is the confer-to-narrow duty for an unduly-burdensome categorical request (IL-0016), which does not itself pause the clock.
- `request.tracking_log_public` — No statutory requirement that municipalities maintain or publish a public request-tracking log. Section 3.5 requires the FOIA officer to note the receipt date and compute the due date internally, but this is an internal notation, not a mandated tracking system.
- `appeal.fee` — No filing fee is imposed for a request for review to the Public Access Counselor; the administrative-review path is free to the requester. Stated as an absence of any fee provision in Section 9.5.
- `intake.language_access` — No explicit statutory translation or language-access requirement for processing a request within FOIA (5 ILCS 140). Absence of a found rule is not proof none exists.
- `fee.deposit_threshold` — No general statutory deposit threshold (e.g., prepay when estimate exceeds $X) for ordinary requests. Full-prepayment-before-copying is permitted only on commercial (Section 3.1) and recurrent-requester (Section 3.2) estimates (IL-0019); no analogous deposit rule was found for standard requests.

### Structural branches

- `response.commercial_purpose_window` — Commercial-purpose path: a request classified as commercial runs on a 21-working-day window with a four-branch response menu (estimate/deny/burden-notice/produce) and its own $10/hour search fee — a distinct pipeline from ordinary requests.
- `response.recurrent_requester_window` — Recurrent-requester path: a requester meeting the 50/12mo, 15/30d, or 7/7d thresholds is routed to a 21-business-day window with a mandatory 5-business-day notice and prepayment option.
- `classification.voluminous_request_definition` — Voluminous-request path: a request meeting the category-count or 500-page thresholds enters a multi-step handshake (5-day initial response, 10-business-day requester response, earlier-of final response) with tiered electronic-data fees.
- `search.categorical_unduly_burdensome_denial` — Unduly-burdensome denial path: a categorical request can exit via a three-part burden test, but only after a mandatory confer-to-narrow step.
- `response.extension_by_written_agreement` — Agreed-extension path: requester and public body can extend the compliance deadline by written agreement for a party-determined period with no statutory cap.
- `fee.waiver_public_interest` — Fee-waiver path: a discretionary branch applying a soft public-interest standard (health/safety/welfare/legal-rights dissemination, not personal/commercial benefit) to waive or reduce fees.
- `appeal.administrative.pac_request_for_review` — Administrative-review path: a denial (including a deemed denial) can be taken to the Public Access Counselor within 60 days, triggering a 7-business-day public-body cooperation deadline and a 60-day binding opinion.
- `appeal.judicial.circuit_court_suit` — Judicial-review path: a denied requester may sue for injunctive/declaratory relief in circuit court, with venue rules and a clear-and-convincing burden on the public body — available independently of PAC review.

Code § 5-14-3-6(a) |

The fee for copying documents may not exceed [the statutory ceiling].”_ | Ind. Code § 5-14-3-8(d) |

(definition summarized; full enumeration at IC 5-14-3-2).”_ | Ind. Code § 5-14-3-2 |

### Material negatives

- `deadline.initial_response_window` — Indiana sets NO fixed statutory production/response deadline. The only statutory clocks are the § 5-14-3-9 deemed-denial windows (24 hours / 7 days) and a soft &#x27;reasonable time&#x27; standard for production. Absence of a found fixed window is not proof none exists in a special-record statute.
- `communication.acknowledgment_response_duty` — No statutory requirement that the agency send an acknowledgment or an estimated completion date. (The deemed-denial windows substitute for an affirmative-acknowledgment duty.)
- `clarification.request_permitted` — No explicit statutory clarification procedure or clarification clock; &#x27;reasonable particularity&#x27; gates the duty but the Act does not codify a confer-to-narrow step or a nonresponse-abandonment clock.
- `deadline.determination_extension` — No statutory extension/unusual-circumstances mechanism to lengthen a response window (there is no fixed window to extend).
- `custody.foia_officer_designation` — APRA does not require agencies to designate or publicly identify a public-records/FOIA officer; it only requires a written denial to name the person responsible for the denial (IN-0020).
- `withdrawal.abandonment_fail_inspect_or_pay` — No statutory abandonment/withdrawal rule for a requester who fails to inspect, pay, or respond to a cost estimate.
- `payment.advance_deposit_before_production` — No explicit statutory authorization to require a deposit or advance full payment before producing standard copies (agencies may set fee schedules; advance-payment practice was not found in statute).
- `fee.no_search_review_labor_overhead` — Statute caps copy charges at per-page/actual cost and bars any inspection fee, but does not expressly address (bar or allow) search/review/labor charges for standard copies; treat as unresolved rather than assume either way.
- `production.no_duty_create_new_record` — No express statutory &#x27;no duty to create a new record&#x27; provision found (contrast the reprogramming provisions in § 5-14-3-6, which address separation, not creation).
- `deadline.tolling_pending_requester_response` — No statutory tolling of a response clock while awaiting a requester&#x27;s answer to clarification/estimate (consistent with there being no fixed response clock). Any tolling of the 30-day PAC-complaint window during an informal inquiry (IC 5-14-5) was not captured verbatim and is flagged for a later pass.

### Structural branches

- `appeal.administrative.pac_complaint_right` — Public Access Counselor formal-complaint path (IC 5-14-5): requester files a complaint within 30 days; PAC issues a non-binding advisory opinion within 30 days. A distinct administrative-review stage the engine must express.
- `appeal.judicial.enforcement_right` — Court action under IC 5-14-3-9(e) in the circuit/superior court of the county of denial to compel access; reviewed de novo with the burden on the agency.
- `denial.deemed_denial_on_nonresponse` — Constructive denial: request moves to a denied state when the applicable 24-hour (in-person/phone/enhanced-access) or 7-day (mail/fax) window elapses - a channel-dependent terminal branch.
- `redaction.segregability` — When a record is mixed, the agency must separate disclosable material and produce it; may branch to a reprogramming-cost charge for electronic separation (IC 5-14-3-6).
- `production.enhanced_access_permission` — Optional enhanced-access path via a section-3.5 contract with its own contract-set fee - a fork the engine must support but need not require.
- `classification.presumption_of_openness` — Liberal-construction / burden-on-agency default (IC 5-14-3-1) that governs how ambiguous classification and denial decisions resolve.

## Kansas (KS) — 50 rules

### Eligibility

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `KS-0001` | structural | none | **All public records shall be open for inspection by any person, except as otherwise provided by the act; each public agency must make suitable facilities available for inspection.**<br><br>_“All public records shall be open for inspection by any person, except as otherwise provided by this act, and suitable facilities shall be made available by each public agency for this purpose.”_ | K.S.A. 45-218(a) |
| `KS-0002` | structural | none | **No residency or citizenship qualification is imposed on the right to request; the right runs to &#x27;any person.&#x27;**<br><br>_“All public records shall be open for inspection by any person, except as otherwise provided by this act, and suitable facilities shall be made available by each public agency for this purpose.”_ | K.S.A. 45-218(a) |
| `KS-0003` | structural | none | **A public agency shall not require a request to contain more than the requester&#x27;s name and address and the information necessary to ascertain the records and the requester&#x27;s right of access; no general statement of purpose is required.**<br><br>_“Except as otherwise provided by subsection (c), a public agency shall not require that a request contain more information than the requester&#x27;s name and address and the information necessary to ascertain the records to which the requester desires access and the requester&#x27;s right of access to the records.”_ | K.S.A. 45-220(b) |

### Intake

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `KS-0004` | structural | none | **A public agency may require a written request for inspection of public records but shall not otherwise require a request in any particular form.**<br><br>_“A public agency may require a written request for inspection of public records but shall not otherwise require a request to be made in any particular form.”_ | K.S.A. 45-220(b) |
| `KS-0005` | structural | none | **A public agency shall not require a request to be made in any particular form.**<br><br>_“A public agency may require a written request for inspection of public records but shall not otherwise require a request to be made in any particular form.”_ | K.S.A. 45-220(b) |
| `KS-0006` | structural | none | **A public agency shall not require that a request contain more than the requester&#x27;s name and address, information necessary to ascertain the records desired, and the requester&#x27;s right of access.**<br><br>_“Except as otherwise provided by subsection (c), a public agency shall not require that a request contain more information than the requester&#x27;s name and address and the information necessary to ascertain the records to which the requester desires access and the requester&#x27;s right of access to the records.”_ | K.S.A. 45-220(b) |
| `KS-0007` | structural | none | **A public agency may require proof of identity of any person requesting access to a public record.**<br><br>_“A public agency may require proof of identity of any person requesting access to a public record.”_ | K.S.A. 45-220(b) |
| `KS-0008` | structural | none | **No request shall be returned, delayed, or denied because of any technicality unless it is impossible to determine the records to which the requester desires access.**<br><br>_“No request shall be returned, delayed or denied because of any technicality unless it is impossible to determine the records to which the requester desires access.”_ | K.S.A. 45-220(b) |
| `KS-0009` | structural | none | **If copies are requested, the public agency may require a written request and advance payment of the prescribed fee.**<br><br>_“If copies are requested, the public agency may require a written request and advance payment of the prescribed fee.”_ | K.S.A. 45-219(a) |
| `KS-0010` | structural | none | **Where access to records or their permitted use is limited under K.S.A. 45-221 or 45-230, the agency may require the requester to certify in writing either a right of access or that the requester will not use/sell any list of names and addresses to sell property or services.**<br><br>_“If access to public records of an agency or the purpose for which the records may be used is limited pursuant to K.S.A. 45-221 or 45-230, and amendments thereto, the agency may require a person requesting the records or information therein to provide written certification that: (1) The requester has a right of access to the records and the basis of that right; or (2) the requester does not intend to, and will not: (A) Use any list of names or addresses contained in or derived from the records or information for the purpose of selling or offering for sale any property or service to any person listed or to any person who resides at any address listed; or (B) sell, give or otherwise make available to any person any list of names or addresses contained in or derived from the records or information for the purpose of allowing that person to sell or offer for sale any property or service to any person listed or to any person who resides at any address listed.”_ | K.S.A. 45-220(c) |
| `KS-0011` | structural | none | **Each public agency shall adopt procedures that provide full access, protect records, prevent excessive disruption, provide assistance and information, and ensure efficient and timely action on requests.**<br><br>_“Each public agency shall adopt procedures to be followed in requesting access to and obtaining copies of public records, which procedures shall provide full access to public records, protect public records from damage and disorganization, prevent excessive disruption of the agency&#x27;s essential functions, provide assistance and information upon request and ensure efficient and timely action in response to applications for inspection of public records.”_ | K.S.A. 45-220(a) |

| `KS-0014` | structural | none | **Each official custodian shall designate the persons necessary to carry out custodian duties and shall ensure a custodian is available during regular business hours.**<br><br>_“Each official custodian of public records shall designate such persons as necessary to carry out the duties of custodian under this act and shall ensure that a custodian is available during regular business hours of the public agency to carry out such duties.”_ | K.S.A. 45-220(e) |

| `KS-0017` | structural | none | **Except for multi-agency summary data or as otherwise provided by law, requests for records in the KBI-maintained Kansas asset seizure and forfeiture repository shall be directed to the law enforcement agency from which the records originated.**<br><br>_“Except for requests of summary data compiled from information submitted by multiple law enforcement agencies or as otherwise provided by law, requests for records submitted to the Kansas asset seizure and forfeiture repository that are maintained by the Kansas bureau of investigation pursuant to K.S.A. 60-4127, and amendments thereto, shall be directed to the law enforcement agency from which the records originated.”_ | K.S.A. 45-220(h) |
| `KS-0018` | structural | none | **Requests for records defined as &#x27;files&#x27; under K.S.A. 75-4379 submitted to a state or local law enforcement or governmental agency shall be directed to the agency that made, maintained, or kept such files.**<br><br>_“Requests for records defined as &quot;files&quot; pursuant to K.S.A. 75-4379, and amendments thereto, submitted to a state or local law enforcement agency or governmental agency shall be directed to the state or local law enforcement agency or governmental agency that made, maintained or kept such files, as required by K.S.A. 75-4379, and amendments thereto.”_ | K.S.A. 45-220(i) |
| `KS-0019` | structural | none | **Requests for records containing captured license plate data or pertaining to the location of an automated license plate recognition system shall be directed to the agency that owns, leases, or contracts for the system.**<br><br>_“Requests for records that contain captured license plate data or that pertain to the location of an automated license plate recognition system submitted to a state or local law enforcement agency or governmental agency shall be directed to the state or local law enforcement agency or governmental agency that owns, leases or contracts for the automated license plate recognition system.”_ | K.S.A. 45-220(j) |
