| `KS-0032` | structural | none | **If a record contains material not subject to disclosure, the agency shall separate or delete that material and make the disclosable remainder available; identifying portions of records pertaining to an identifiable individual shall be deleted and remaining disclosable portions produced.**<br><br>_“If a public record contains material that is not subject to disclosure pursuant to this act, the public agency shall separate or delete such material and make available to the requester that material in the public record that is subject to disclosure pursuant to this act. If a public record is not subject to disclosure because it pertains to an identifiable individual, the public agency shall delete the identifying portions of the record and make available to the requester any remaining portions that are subject to disclosure pursuant to this act, unless the request is for a record pertaining to a specific individual or to such a limited group of individuals that the individuals&#x27; identities are reasonably ascertainable, the public agency shall not be required to disclose those portions of the record that pertain to such individual or individuals.”_ | K.S.A. 45-221(d) |

### Fees

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `KS-0033` | parameter · soft-standard | none | **Except where fees are prescribed by statute or subsection (h) applies, each public agency may prescribe reasonable fees for providing access to or furnishing copies of public records, subject to enumerated limits.**<br><br>_“Except as provided by subsection (h) or where fees for inspection or for copies of a public record are prescribed by statute, each public agency may prescribe reasonable fees for providing access to or furnishing copies of public records, subject to the following:”_ | K.S.A. 45-219(c) |
| `KS-0034` | parameter · ceiling | none | **Fees for copies shall not exceed the actual cost of furnishing the requested records, including the cost of staff time required to make the information available.**<br><br>_“In the case of fees for copies of records, the fees shall not exceed the actual cost of furnishing the requested records, including the cost of staff time required to make the information available. Actual costs may include the cost to review and redact the requested records but shall not include incidental costs incurred by the public agency that are not attributable to furnishing the requested records.”_ | K.S.A. 45-219(c)(1) |
| `KS-0035` | parameter · ceiling | none | **Actual costs may include the cost to review and redact the requested records but shall not include incidental costs not attributable to furnishing the requested records.**<br><br>_“Actual costs may include the cost to review and redact the requested records but shall not include incidental costs incurred by the public agency that are not attributable to furnishing the requested records.”_ | K.S.A. 45-219(c)(1) |
| `KS-0036` | parameter · ceiling | none | **Fees for providing access to records maintained on computer facilities shall include only the cost of any computer services, including staff time required.**<br><br>_“In the case of fees for providing access to records maintained on computer facilities, the fees shall include only the cost of any computer services, including staff time required.”_ | K.S.A. 45-219(c)(2) |
| `KS-0037` | structural | none | **If the agency incurs staff-time costs, it shall use in good faith the lowest-cost category of staff reasonably necessary to provide access or furnish copies.**<br><br>_“If the public agency incurs costs for staff time to provide access to or furnish copies of public records, the agency shall use in good faith the lowest-cost category of staff reasonably necessary to provide access to or furnish copies of public records.”_ | K.S.A. 45-219(c)(3) |
| `KS-0038` | parameter · ceiling | none | **Charges for staff time shall be based on the employee&#x27;s salary or hourly wage and shall not include the costs of employee benefits.**<br><br>_“Charges for staff time shall be based on the employee&#x27;s salary or hourly wage. Charges for staff time shall not include the costs of employee benefits.”_ | K.S.A. 45-219(c)(3) |
| `KS-0039` | structural | none | **Upon request, the agency shall provide an itemized statement of costs charged to the requester, including the hourly rates for each employee involved and an itemized list of any other fees.**<br><br>_“Upon request, a public agency shall provide to the person requesting access to or copies of public records pursuant to this section an itemized statement of costs incurred by the public agency and charged to such requester. Such itemized statement shall include, but not be limited to, the hourly rates charged for each employee involved in making the requested records available and an itemized list of any other fees charged to provide access to or furnish copies of the requested records.”_ | K.S.A. 45-219(c)(4) |
| `KS-0040` | parameter · fixed | none | **If it is necessary to use other facilities for copying, the requester shall pay that cost; the agency may also charge the same supervision fee as for furnishing copies under subsection (c).**<br><br>_“If it is necessary to use other facilities for copying, the cost thereof shall be paid by the person desiring a copy of the records. In addition, the public agency may charge the same fee for the services rendered in supervising the copying as for furnishing copies under subsection (c) and may establish a reasonable schedule of times for making copies at other facilities.”_ | K.S.A. 45-219(b) |
| `KS-0041` | parameter · fixed | none | **When staff time to respond will exceed five hours, or estimated actual staff-time cost exceeds $200, the agency shall make reasonable efforts to contact the requester and engage in interactive communication about mitigating costs; the requester is not obligated to mitigate.**<br><br>_“When the staff time needed to respond to a records request will exceed five hours or the estimated actual cost for staff time needed to fill the request exceeds $200, the public agency shall make reasonable efforts to contact the requester and engage in interactive communication about mitigating costs to fill the request. The requester is not obligated to mitigate costs.”_ | K.S.A. 45-219(e)(1) |

### Payment

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `KS-0043` | structural | none | **A public agency may charge and require advance payment of a fee for providing access to or furnishing copies of public records, subject to K.S.A. 45-219.**<br><br>_“A public agency may charge and require advance payment of a fee for providing access to or furnishing copies of public records, subject to K.S.A. 45-219.”_ | K.S.A. 45-218(f) |

### Production

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `KS-0044` | structural | none | **A public agency is not required to provide copies of tapes, discs, video, film, pictures, slides, graphics, illustrations, or similar audio/visual items unless they were shown/played to a public meeting of the governing body; and need not provide items copyrighted by another.**<br><br>_“A public agency shall not be required to provide copies of radio or recording tapes or discs, video tapes or films, pictures, slides, graphics, illustrations or similar audio or visual items or devices, unless such items or devices were shown or played to a public meeting of the governing body thereof, except that the public agency shall not be required to provide such items or devices that are copyrighted by a person other than the public agency.”_ | K.S.A. 45-219(a) |
| `KS-0045` | structural | none | **Copies shall be made while records are in the custodian&#x27;s possession/custody/control and under the custodian&#x27;s supervision; whenever practicable, copies shall be made where the records are kept, and if not practicable the custodian shall allow arrangements for other facilities.**<br><br>_“Copies of public records shall be made while the records are in the possession, custody and control of the custodian or a person designated by the custodian and shall be made under the supervision of such custodian or person. Whenever practicable, copies shall be made in the place where the records are kept. If it is not practicable to do so, the custodian shall allow arrangements to be made for use of other facilities.”_ | K.S.A. 45-219(b) |
| `KS-0046` | structural | none | **K.S.A. 45-219(a) requires a public agency, upon request, to provide a copy of a public record in the format in which the agency maintains that record.**<br><br>_“The plain language of K.S.A. 45-219(a) requires a public agency, upon request, to provide a copy of a public record in the format in which the agency maintains that record.”_ | Roe v. Phillips Cnty. Hosp., 317 Kan. 1, 522 P.3d 277 (2023) |
| `KS-0047` | structural | none | **Nothing in the act requires an agency to make electronic copies by allowing a person to attach an electronic device provided by that person to the agency&#x27;s computer or other electronic device.**<br><br>_“Nothing in the open records act shall require a public agency to electronically make copies of public records by allowing a person to obtain copies of a public record by inserting, connecting or otherwise attaching an electronic device provided by such person to the computer or other electronic device of the public agency.”_ | K.S.A. 45-219(i) |

### Appeals

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `KS-0048` | structural | none | **The district court of any county where the records are located has jurisdiction to enforce the act by injunction, mandamus, declaratory judgment, or other appropriate order, in an action brought by any person, the attorney general, or a county or district attorney.**<br><br>_“The district court of any county in which public records are located shall have jurisdiction to enforce the purposes of this act with respect to such records, by injunction, mandamus, declaratory judgment or other appropriate order, in an action brought by any person, the attorney general or a county or district attorney.”_ | K.S.A. 45-222(a) |
| `KS-0049` | structural | none | **In any enforcement action the court shall determine the matter de novo and may, on its own or a party&#x27;s motion, view the records in controversy in camera before deciding.**<br><br>_“In any action hereunder, the court shall determine the matter de novo. The court on its own motion, or on motion of either party, may view the records in controversy in camera before reaching a decision.”_ | K.S.A. 45-222(b) |
| `KS-0050` | structural | none | **In any enforcement action (or action under K.S.A. 45-251), the burden of proof is on the public agency to sustain its action.**<br><br>_“In any action hereunder, or under K.S.A. 45-251, and amendments thereto, the burden of proof shall be on the public agency to sustain its action.”_ | K.S.A. 45-222(c) |

### Material negatives

- `deadline.determination_extension` — KORA sets no fixed extension mechanism. Beyond the three-business-day action deadline (KS-0026), the only &#x27;extension&#x27; path is the custodian&#x27;s &#x27;detailed explanation of the cause for further delay&#x27; with an earliest date of availability (KS-0027) — a soft standard, not a numeric extension with enumerated grounds.
- `denial.deemed_denial_on_nonresponse` — No explicit statutory &#x27;silence = denial / deemed denial&#x27; trigger. KORA imposes the three-business-day action deadline and provides judicial enforcement, but does not state that non-response after N days constitutes a denial. (The 2025 fee-mitigation provision creates a deemed WITHDRAWAL for requester non-response — KS-0042 — which is not a deemed denial.)
- `appeal.administrative.review_right` — No general internal/administrative appeal stage for municipal requests. The only administrative-review path in KORA is a fee-reasonableness appeal to the Secretary of Administration under K.S.A. 45-219(d), and that is limited to records within the state EXECUTIVE BRANCH — inapplicable to municipal requests. Municipal recourse is judicial (K.S.A. 45-222).
- `fee.per_page_rate_ceiling` — No statewide fixed per-page copy rate for general municipal records. Copy fees are governed by &#x27;reasonable&#x27; and &#x27;actual cost&#x27; standards (KS-0033, KS-0034), with the city supplying the operative value; there is no statutory cents-per-page cap in the open records act.
- `fee.waiver_public_interest` — KORA contains no public-interest or indigency fee-waiver provision.
- `fee.commercial_purpose_rate` — No separate commercial-purpose fee tier. KORA does not define &#x27;commercial purpose&#x27; or set a higher rate for commercial requesters; it instead restricts sale/use of name-and-address lists (K.S.A. 45-230) and permits a non-sale certification (KS-0010).
- `fee.inspection_no_charge` — No explicit statutory rule that inspection is free of charge. K.S.A. 45-218(f) permits charging a fee for &#x27;providing access to&#x27; records; inspection during office hours is provided but the act does not expressly bar an access fee for inspection.
- `preservation.hold_pending_request` — KORA contains no request-triggered preservation or litigation-hold provision; records retention/destruction is governed outside the open records act.
- `clarification.request_permitted` — No express clarification-request mechanism or clarification clock. The nearest provisions are the requirement that a request contain enough information to ascertain the records sought and the &#x27;impossible to determine the records&#x27; technicality exception (KS-0008), plus the 2025 cost-mitigation interactive communication (KS-0041).
- `intake.oral_request_permitted` — Oral requests are not guaranteed; the agency &#x27;may require a written request&#x27; (KS-0004). No statutory duty to accept oral requests.
- `production.electronic_format_choice` — No general statutory requester right to choose the electronic format/medium. The duty established judicially (KS-0046) is to provide a copy in the format the agency MAINTAINS the record, and the agency need not allow requester devices to be attached (KS-0047).

### Structural branches

- `withdrawal.fee_mitigation_nonresponse` — Deemed-withdrawal state (KS-0042): after a triggered cost-mitigation contact (KS-0041), requester silence for three business days moves the request into a &#x27;deemed withdrawn&#x27; state, reversible only on subsequent requester contact. A Kansas-specific path added by L. 2025, ch. 89.
- `fee.cost_mitigation_conference_threshold` — Interactive cost-mitigation conference stage (KS-0041): crossing the 5-hour / $200 staff-time threshold forces a mandatory contact-and-confer step before the request proceeds.
- `denial.written_statement_on_request` — Two-step denial path (KS-0028 to KS-0030): denial itself is communicated within the general 3-business-day window, but the written, provision-citing statement of grounds is owed only &#x27;upon request,&#x27; and its own separate 3-business-day clock runs from receipt of that follow-up request.
- `certification.access_or_nonsale_when_restricted` — Restricted-access certification gate (KS-0010): where access or use is limited under K.S.A. 45-221 or 45-230, the request branches to require a written right-of-access or non-sale certification before production.
- `routing.record_type_originating_agency` — Record-type routing forks (KS-0016 to KS-0019): criminal-justice-repository records, asset-seizure-repository records, &#x27;files&#x27; under K.S.A. 75-4379, and ALPR data are each statutorily routed to the originating/owning agency rather than the receiving agency.
- `custody.wrong_recipient_routing` — Non-custodian redirect (KS-0013): a non-custodian recipient must route the requester to the named custodian&#x27;s location.
- `appeal.judicial.enforcement_right` — Judicial enforcement path (KS-0048 to KS-0050): the sole review route for municipal requests is a de novo district-court action with in camera inspection and the burden of proof on the agency; there is no municipal administrative-appeal stage.

---

## Massachusetts (MA) — 69 rules

### Eligibility

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MA-0001` | structural | none | **A records access officer must permit inspection or furnish a copy of any public record to any requestor, without limiting access to a class of persons.**<br><br>_“A records access officer appointed pursuant to section 6A, or a designee, shall at reasonable times and without unreasonable delay permit inspection or furnish a copy of any public record as defined in clause twenty-sixth of section 7 of chapter 4, or any segregable portion of a public record, not later than 10 business days following the receipt of the request”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(a) |
| `MA-0002` | structural | none | **Access to public records is not conditioned on the requestor&#x27;s residency or citizenship; any person may request regardless of state of residence.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“A records access officer appointed pursuant to section 6A, or a designee, shall at reasonable times and without unreasonable delay permit inspection or furnish a copy of any public record ... not later than 10 business days following the receipt of the request, provided that: (i) the request reasonably describes the public record sought; (ii) the public record is within the possession, custody or control of the agency or municipality ...; and (iii) the records access officer receives payment of a reasonable fee”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(a) |
| `MA-0003` | structural | none | **A records access officer may not require the requester to state the purpose of a request, except to determine a commercial purpose or to rule on a fee-waiver request.**<br><br>_“a records access officer may not require the requester to specify the purpose for a request, except to determine whether the records are requested for a commercial purpose or whether to grant a request for a fee waiver”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(d)(viii) |

### Fees

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MA-0004` | structural | none | **&#x27;Commercial purpose&#x27; means the sale/resale of a record or use of its information to advance the requester&#x27;s strategic business interests for profit, excluding news, citizen oversight, and academic/scientific/journalistic/public research.**<br><br>_“as used in this section &#x27;&#x27;commercial purpose&#x27;&#x27; shall mean the sale or resale of any portion of the public record or the use of information from the public record to advance the requester&#x27;s strategic business interests in a manner that the requester can reasonably expect to make a profit, and shall not include gathering or reporting news or gathering information to promote citizen oversight or further the understanding of the operation or activities of government or for academic, scientific, journalistic or public research or education”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(d)(ix) |
| `MA-0029` | parameter · ceiling | none | **A records access officer may assess a reasonable fee for production (except freely-inspectable records), and the fee shall not exceed the actual cost of reproducing the record.**<br><br>_“A records access officer may assess a reasonable fee for the production of a public record except those records that are freely available for public inspection. The reasonable fee shall not exceed the actual cost of reproducing the record.”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(d) |
| `MA-0030` | parameter · ceiling | none | **The fee for standard black-and-white paper copies or printouts shall not exceed 5 cents per page, for both single- and double-sided copies.**<br><br>_“the fee assessed for standard black and white paper copies or printouts of records shall not exceed 5 cents per page, for both single and double-sided black and white copies or printouts”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(d)(i) |
| `MA-0031` | parameter · ceiling | none | **The actual cost of any storage device or material provided in response to a request may be included as part of the fee.**<br><br>_“the actual cost of any storage device or material provided to a person in response to a request for public records under subsection (a) may be included as part of the fee”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(d)(i) |
| `MA-0032` | parameter · ceiling | none | **For a municipality, the hourly labor rate must equal or be less than the lowest-paid capable employee&#x27;s rate and shall not exceed $25 per hour, unless a higher rate is approved by the Supervisor.**<br><br>_“if a municipality is required to devote more than 2 hours of employee time to search for, compile, segregate, redact or reproduce a record requested, the records access officer may include as part of the fee an hourly rate equal to or less than the hourly rate attributed to the lowest paid employee who has the necessary skill required to search for, compile, segregate, redact or reproduce the record requested but the fee (A) shall not be more than $25 per hour unless such rate is approved by the supervisor of records under clause (iv)”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(d)(iii) |
| `MA-0033` | parameter · ceiling | none | **For a (state) agency, the hourly labor rate must equal or be less than the lowest-paid capable employee&#x27;s rate and shall not be more than $25 per hour.**<br><br>_“if an agency is required to devote more than 4 hours of employee time ... the records access officer may also include as part of the fee an hourly rate equal to or less than the hourly rate attributed to the lowest paid employee who has the necessary skill ... but the fee (A) shall not be more than $25 per hour”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(d)(ii) |
| `MA-0034` | parameter · fixed | none | **For a municipality with a population over 20,000, no labor fee may be assessed for the first 2 hours of work performed.**<br><br>_“(B) shall not be assessed for the first 2 hours of work performed where the responding municipality has a population of over 20,000 people”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(d)(iii)(B) |
| `MA-0035` | parameter · fixed | none | **For a (state) agency, no labor fee may be assessed for the first 4 hours of work performed.**<br><br>_“(B) shall not be assessed for the first 4 hours of work performed”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(d)(ii)(B) |
| `MA-0036` | structural | none | **A fee shall not be assessed for time spent segregating or redacting records unless such segregation/redaction is required by law or approved by the Supervisor.**<br><br>_“(C) shall not be assessed for time spent segregating or redacting records unless such segregation or redaction is required by law or approved by the supervisor of records under clause (iv)”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(d)(ii)(C), (iii)(C) |
| `MA-0037` | structural | sets-deadline · Supervisor written determination within 5 business-days of receipt of the petition | **The Supervisor may approve a petition to charge for segregation/redaction time, or a municipal petition to charge over $25/hour, if the request is for a commercial purpose or the fee is a necessary, reasonable good-faith cost not designed to deter access; the entity must furnish the petition to the requestor and the Supervisor decides within 5 business days.**<br><br>_“the supervisor of records may approve a petition from an agency or municipality to charge for time spent segregating or redacting, or a petition from a municipality to charge in excess of $25 per hour, if the supervisor of records determines that (A) the request is for a commercial purpose; or (B) the fee represents an actual and good faith representation by the agency or municipality to comply with the request, the fee is necessary such that the request could not have been prudently completed without the redaction, segregation or fee in excess of $25 per hour and the amount of the fee is reasonable and the fee is not designed to limit, deter or prevent access”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(d)(iv) |
| `MA-0038` | structural | none | **The RAO may waive or reduce a fee upon a showing that disclosure is in the public interest (likely to contribute significantly to public understanding of government and not primarily in the requestor&#x27;s commercial interest), or that the requestor lacks the financial ability to pay the full reasonable fee.**<br><br>_“the records access officer may waive or reduce the amount of any fee charged under this subsection upon a showing that disclosure of a requested record is in the public interest because it is likely to contribute significantly to public understanding of the operations or activities of the government and is not primarily in the commercial interest of the requestor, or upon a showing that the requestor lacks the financial ability to pay the full amount of the reasonable fee”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(d)(v) |
| `MA-0039` | structural | none | **If fees are to be assessed, the RAO must provide a written, itemized, good-faith estimate of fees before complying, within the 10-business-day response.**<br><br>_“if fees are being assessed, a records access officer shall provide a written, itemized, good faith estimate of any fees that may be charged to produce the records prior to complying with a public records request within ten business days.”_ | 950 CMR 32.07(2)(b) |
| `MA-0040` | structural | terminal · terminal: fee right is forfeited if no § 10(b) response within 10 business-days | **A records access officer shall not charge a fee for a public record unless the RAO responded to the requestor within 10 business days under subsection (b).**<br><br>_“A records access officer shall not charge a fee for a public record unless the records access officer responded to the requestor within 10 business days under subsection (b).”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(e) |
| `MA-0041` | structural | none | **The RAO may deny public-records requests from a requester who has failed to compensate the entity for previously produced public records.**<br><br>_“the records access officer may deny public records requests from a requester who has failed to compensate the agency or municipality for previously produced public records”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(d)(vi) |

### Intake

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MA-0005` | structural | none | **A valid request must reasonably describe the public record sought so the RAO can identify and locate it.**<br><br>_“provided that: (i) the request reasonably describes the public record sought”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(a)(i) |
| `MA-0007` | structural | none | **A request may be delivered by hand, by first class mail to the RAO&#x27;s business address, or by electronic mail to the posted address (regulations add facsimile if the custodian has fax access).**<br><br>_“A request for public records may be delivered to the records access officer by hand or via first class mail at the record officer&#x27;s business address, or via electronic mail to the address posted by the agency or municipality that the records access officer serves.”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(a) |

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |

| `MA-0015` | structural | none | **Calculation of time commences only for requests made in accordance with the request requirements (e.g., reasonable description, proper delivery).**<br><br>_“calculation of time will commence only for requests that are made in accordance with 950 CMR 32.06(1).”_ | 950 CMR 32.06(1)(f) |
| `MA-0016` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 10 business-days from receipt of request | **The RAO must permit inspection or furnish a copy (or any segregable portion) not later than 10 business days following receipt of the request.**<br><br>_“shall at reasonable times and without unreasonable delay permit inspection or furnish a copy of any public record ... or any segregable portion of a public record, not later than 10 business days following the receipt of the request”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(a) |
| `MA-0017` | structural | sets-deadline · undefined-soft: &quot;at reasonable times and without unreasonable delay&quot; from request receipt | **Independent of the 10-day cap, production or inspection must occur at reasonable times and without unreasonable delay.**<br><br>_“shall at reasonable times and without unreasonable delay permit inspection or furnish a copy of any public record”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(a) |
| `MA-0020` | parameter · ceiling | sets-deadline · ceiling: 25 business-days from initial receipt (municipality) | **For a municipality, the reasonable production timeframe stated in the written response shall not exceed 25 business days following initial receipt of the request.**<br><br>_“provided, that for an agency, the timeframe shall not exceed 15 business days following the initial receipt of the request for public records and for a municipality the timeframe shall not exceed 25 business days following the initial receipt of the request for public records”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(b)(vi) |
| `MA-0021` | parameter · ceiling | sets-deadline · ceiling: 15 business-days from initial receipt (agency) | **For a (state) agency, the reasonable production timeframe stated in the written response shall not exceed 15 business days following initial receipt of the request.**<br><br>_“provided, that for an agency, the timeframe shall not exceed 15 business days following the initial receipt of the request for public records”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(b)(vi) |
| `MA-0022` | structural | none | **The requestor may voluntarily agree to a response date beyond the statutory extended timeframes.**<br><br>_“provided further, that the requestor may voluntarily agree to a response date beyond the timeframes set forth herein”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(b)(vi) |
| `MA-0023` | parameter · ceiling | sets-deadline · within 20 business-days from initial receipt (or 10 business-days from Supervisor determination) | **A records access officer may petition the Supervisor of Records for an extension of time as soon as practical and within 20 business days after initial receipt (or within 10 business days after a Supervisor determination that the record is public).**<br><br>_“a records access officer may, as soon as practical and within 20 business days after initial receipt of the request, or within 10 business days after receipt of a determination by the supervisor of public records that the requested record constitutes a public record, petition the supervisor of records for an extension of the time”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(c) |
| `MA-0025` | parameter · ceiling | sets-deadline · ceiling: single extension up to 30 business-days (municipality) | **Upon a showing of good cause, the Supervisor may grant a single extension to a municipality not to exceed 30 business days.**<br><br>_“Upon a showing of good cause, the supervisor of records may grant a single extension to an agency not to exceed 20 business days and a single extension to a municipality not to exceed 30 business days.”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(c) |
| `MA-0026` | parameter · ceiling | sets-deadline · ceiling: single extension up to 20 business-days (agency) | **Upon a showing of good cause, the Supervisor may grant a single extension to a (state) agency not to exceed 20 business days.**<br><br>_“the supervisor of records may grant a single extension to an agency not to exceed 20 business days”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(c) |
| `MA-0027` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 5 business-days from Supervisor&#x27;s receipt of the petition | **The Supervisor must issue a written decision on an RAO extension petition within 5 business days following receipt of the petition.**<br><br>_“The supervisor of records shall issue a written decision regarding a petition submitted by a records access officer under this subsection within 5 business days following receipt of the petition.”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(c) |
| `MA-0028` | structural | terminal · terminal: obligation to provide copies may be relieved (or a longer extension granted) | **If the Supervisor finds a request is part of a series of contemporaneous requests that are frivolous or designed to intimidate/harass and not for broad public dissemination, the Supervisor may grant a longer extension or relieve the entity of its obligation to provide copies.**<br><br>_“If the supervisor of records determines that the request is part of a series of contemporaneous requests that are frivolous or designed to intimidate or harass, and the requests are not intended for the broad dissemination of information to the public about actual or alleged government activity, the supervisor of records may grant a longer extension or relieve the agency or municipality of its obligation to provide copies of the records sought.”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(c) |

### Communications

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MA-0018` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 10 business-days from initial receipt of request | **If the entity will not fully produce within the initial timeframe (or will withhold), it must inform the requestor in writing not later than 10 business days after initial receipt.**<br><br>_“the agency or municipality shall inform the requestor in writing not later than 10 business days after the initial receipt of the request for public records. The written response shall be made via first class or electronic mail”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(b) |
| `MA-0019` | structural | none | **The § 10(b) written response must contain nine enumerated elements: receipt confirmation; records not in custody; other custodian; records withheld with specific exemptions; records to be produced with a burden statement; a reasonable production timeframe; a suggested scope modification; an itemized good-faith fee estimate; and a statement of appeal and judicial-review rights.**<br><br>_“The written response shall ... (i) confirm receipt of the request; ... (iv) identify any records, categories of records or portions of records that the agency or municipality intends to withhold, and provide the specific reasons for such withholding, including the specific exemption or exemptions upon which the withholding is based ... (vi) identify a reasonable timeframe in which the agency or municipality shall produce the public records sought ... (vii) suggest a reasonable modification of the scope of the request ... (viii) include an itemized, good faith estimate of any fees that may be charged to produce the records; and (ix) include a statement informing the requestor of the right of appeal to the supervisor of records under subsection (a) of section 10A and the right to seek judicial review”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(b)(i)-(ix) |
| `MA-0024` | structural | none | **Upon submitting the extension petition to the Supervisor, the RAO must furnish a copy of the petition to the requestor.**<br><br>_“The records access officer shall, upon submitting the petition to the supervisor of records, furnish a copy of the petition to the requestor.”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10(c) |

| `MA-0062` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 10 business-days from Supervisor&#x27;s receipt of the petition | **The Supervisor of Records must issue a written determination on any properly submitted appeal petition not later than 10 business days following receipt of the petition.**<br><br>_“The supervisor of records shall issue a written determination regarding any petition submitted in accordance with this section not later than 10 business days following receipt of the petition by the supervisor of records.”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10A(a) |
| `MA-0063` | structural | none | **Upon determining that a violation has occurred, the Supervisor shall order timely and appropriate relief.**<br><br>_“Upon a determination by the supervisor of records that a violation has occurred, the supervisor of records shall order timely and appropriate relief.”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10A(a) |
| `MA-0064` | structural | none | **An oral request, while valid as a public-records request, cannot be the basis of an administrative appeal to the Supervisor.**<br><br>_“an oral request, while valid as a public record request, shall not be the basis of an appeal under 950 CMR 32.08.”_ | 950 CMR 32.08(1)(c) |
| `MA-0065` | structural | none | **The Supervisor may deny an appeal for reasons including that the records are the subject of active litigation/administrative hearing/mediation, the request is designed to harass/intimidate or assist a crime, the request is made solely for a commercial purpose, or the requester failed to comply with request requirements.**<br><br>_“the Supervisor may deny an appeal for, among other reasons if, in the opinion of the Supervisor: 1. the public records in question are the subjects of disputes in active litigation, administrative hearings or mediation; 2. the request is designed or intended to harass, intimidate, or assist in the commission of a crime; 3. the public records request is made solely for a commercial purpose;”_ | 950 CMR 32.08(2)(b) |
| `MA-0066` | structural | none | **A requestor aggrieved by a Supervisor order, or by the Supervisor&#x27;s failure to issue a timely determination, may obtain judicial review only through a superior court action in the nature of certiorari under c. 249, § 4.**<br><br>_“A requestor, aggrieved by an order issued by the supervisor of records or upon the failure of the supervisor of records to issue a timely determination, may obtain judicial review only through an action in superior court seeking relief in the nature of certiorari under section 4 of chapter 249 and as prescribed in subsection (d).”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10A(a) |
| `MA-0067` | structural | none | **Notwithstanding the Supervisor and AG procedures, a requestor may initiate a civil action to enforce the chapter, filed for municipalities in the superior court of the county where the municipality is located.**<br><br>_“Notwithstanding the procedure in subsections (a) or (b), a requestor may initiate a civil action to enforce the requirements of this chapter. Any action under this subsection shall be filed in Suffolk superior court with respect to agencies and, with respect to municipalities, in the superior court in the county in which the municipality is located.”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10A(c) |
| `MA-0068` | structural | none | **In a requestor&#x27;s civil action, the superior court determines the propriety of the withholding de novo, a presumption exists that each record is public, and the burden is on the defendant entity to prove by a preponderance that the record may be withheld under state or federal law.**<br><br>_“a presumption shall exist that each record sought is public and the burden shall be on the defendant agency or municipality to prove, by a preponderance of the evidence, that such record or portion of the record may be withheld in accordance with state or federal law.”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10A(d)(1)(iv) |

### Enforcement

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MA-0069` | structural | none | **If an entity refuses or fails to comply with a Supervisor order, the Supervisor may notify the Attorney General, who may take measures necessary to ensure compliance.**<br><br>_“If an agency or municipality refuses or fails to comply with an order issued by the supervisor of records, the supervisor of records may notify the attorney general who, after consultation with the supervisor of records, may take whatever measures the attorney general considers necessary to ensure compliance.”_ | M.G.L. c. 66, § 10A(b) |

### Material negatives

- `classification.deemed_denial` — MA has no automatic &#x27;silence = denial&#x27; or &#x27;silence = grant&#x27; deeming provision for a municipality&#x27;s non-response. Instead, failure to respond within the required timeframe opens the administrative-appeal path (petition to the Supervisor within 90 calendar days of the request, 950 CMR 32.08(1)(e)) and enables judicial review upon the Supervisor&#x27;s failure to issue a timely determination (M.G.L. c. 66, s.10A(a)). Absence of a found deeming rule is not proof none exists.
- `preservation.litigation_hold_trigger` — No explicit statutory preservation/litigation-hold triggered by the request or by an appeal on that request was found in c. 66 s.10/s.10A or 950 CMR 32.00. Records retention is governed separately (records-retention schedules, out of scope under the inclusion test).
- `intake.acknowledgment_window` — MA provides no separate &#x27;acknowledgment&#x27; step distinct from substantive action; the s.10(b) written response (which confirms receipt) and the s.10(a) production duty both run on the same 10-business-day clock. Acknowledgment is folded into MA-0016/MA-0018 rather than being an independent deadline.
- `fee.electronic_record_rate` — No fixed statewide per-unit fee for electronic records; electronic production is governed by the general &#x27;actual cost of reproducing&#x27; ceiling (MA-0029) plus the labor-rate structure. No separate electronic-record rate parameter exists to configure.
- `request.tracking_number_requirement` — No statute or regulation found requiring municipalities to assign or maintain a tracking/reference number for individual requests. (The Supervisor&#x27;s own appeal intake is time-stamped under 950 CMR 32.08(1)(j), but that governs the Division, not the municipal RAO.)
- `fee.deposit_or_prepayment_threshold` — MA sets no numeric deposit threshold triggering mandatory prepayment; the RAO &#x27;may delay provision of records until all fees ... are paid in full&#x27; (MA-0044) but no dollar threshold or advance-deposit percentage is specified to configure.

### Structural branches

- `response.written_response_contents` — The M.G.L. c. 66 s.10(b) written response is a mandatory branch triggered when the entity cannot fully produce within the initial timeframe or intends to withhold; it carries nine required content elements (MA-0019).
- `extension.petition_deadline` — RAO petition to the Supervisor of Records for a single time extension - a bespoke MA stage inserted between the initial response and production, gated by a 20-business-day filing window and a 5-business-day Supervisor decision (MA-0023, MA-0025/26, MA-0027).
- `extension.frivolous_series_relief` — Supervisor may relieve the entity of its obligation to provide copies where a contemporaneous request series is frivolous/harassing - a terminal path ending the request (MA-0028).
- `fee.supervisor_petition_excess` — Entity petition to the Supervisor to charge for redaction time or (municipality) to exceed $25/hr - a MA-specific fee-approval branch with its own 5-business-day decision clock (MA-0037).
- `appeal.administrative.petition_to_supervisor` — Administrative appeal to the Supervisor of Records (Division of Public Records) - MA&#x27;s internal review stage before court, with 90-day filing windows and a 10-business-day determination (MA-0057 through MA-0065).
- `appeal.judicial.certiorari_review` — Certiorari review in superior court under c. 249 s.4 of a Supervisor order or untimely determination - MA&#x27;s designated judicial-review route (MA-0066).
- `appeal.judicial.civil_enforcement_action` — Direct de novo civil enforcement action in superior court, available without exhausting the Supervisor appeal (MA-0067, MA-0068).
- `enforcement.ag_referral_trigger` — Supervisor referral to the Attorney General on non-compliance with a Supervisor order (MA-0069).
- `custody.records_access_officer_designation` — Designated records access officer (municipal clerk by default) as the routing/response locus for all municipal requests (MA-0010, MA-0011, MA-0012).
