| `NJ-M28` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 45 calendar-days from date of denial | **A requestor accurately identified by name who is denied access may, within 45 days of the date of denial, institute a proceeding in Superior Court challenging the custodian&#x27;s decision, heard by a designated Superior Court judge.**<br><br>_“A person who is denied access to a government record by the custodian of the record, at the option of the requestor who is accurately identified by name, may, within 45 days of the date of denial: institute a proceeding to challenge the custodian&#x27;s decision by filing an action in Superior Court which shall be heard in the vicinage where it is filed by a Superior Court Judge who has been designated to hear such cases because of that judge&#x27;s knowledge and expertise in matters relating to access to government records;”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-6; P.L.2024, c.16, §3 |
| `NJ-M29` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 45 calendar-days from date of denial | **In lieu of a Superior Court action, a denied requestor may, within 45 days of the date of denial, file a complaint with the Government Records Council.**<br><br>_“in lieu of filing an action in Superior Court, file a complaint with the Government Records Council established pursuant to section 8 of P.L.2001, c.404 (C.47:1A-7).”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-6; P.L.2024, c.16, §3 |
| `NJ-M30` | structural | none | **The right to institute a proceeding challenging a denial is solely that of the requestor, who must be accurately identified by name.**<br><br>_“The right to institute any proceeding under this section shall be solely that of the requestor.”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-6; P.L.2024, c.16, §3 |
| `NJ-M31` | structural | none | **An anonymous requestor is not permitted to institute proceedings under N.J.S.A. 47:1A-6.**<br><br>_“anonymous requestors shall not be permitted to institute proceedings pursuant to section 7 of P.L.2001, c.404 (C.47:1A-6).”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-5(f); P.L.2024, c.16, §2 |
| `NJ-M32` | structural | none | **Any proceeding challenging a denial shall proceed in a summary or expedited manner.**<br><br>_“Any such proceeding shall proceed in a summary or expedited manner.”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-6; P.L.2024, c.16, §3 |
| `NJ-M33` | structural | none | **In a denial-challenge proceeding, the public agency has the burden of proving that the denial of access is authorized by law.**<br><br>_“The public agency shall have the burden of proving that the denial of access is authorized by law.”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-6; P.L.2024, c.16, §3 |
| `NJ-M34` | structural | none | **If it is determined that access was improperly denied, the court or the Government Records Council shall order that access be allowed.**<br><br>_“If it is determined that access has been improperly denied, the court or Government Records Council shall order that access be allowed.”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-6; P.L.2024, c.16, §3 |
| `NJ-M35` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 45 calendar-days from date GRC renders decision | **A decision of the Government Records Council may be appealed to the Appellate Division of the Superior Court, filed within 45 days from the date the council renders its decision.**<br><br>_“A decision of the council may be appealed to the Appellate Division of the Superior Court. Such appeals shall be filed within 45 days from the date the council renders a decision.”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-7(e); P.L.2024, c.16, §4 |
| `NJ-M36` | structural | none | **A decision of the Government Records Council has no value as precedent for any case initiated in Superior Court under N.J.S.A. 47:1A-6.**<br><br>_“A decision of the council shall not have value as a precedent for any case initiated in Superior Court pursuant to section 7 of P.L.2001, c.404 (C.47:1A-6).”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-7(e); P.L.2024, c.16, §4 |

### Review

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `NJ-M37` | structural | none | **The Government Records Council shall receive, hear, review, and adjudicate a complaint filed by any person concerning a denial of access to a government record by a records custodian.**<br><br>_“receive, hear, review and adjudicate a complaint filed by any person concerning a denial of access to a government record by a records custodian;”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-7(b); P.L.2024, c.16, §4 |
| `NJ-M38` | structural | none | **The Government Records Council shall establish an informal mediation program to facilitate resolution of disputes regarding access to government records.**<br><br>_“establish an informal mediation program to facilitate the resolution of disputes regarding access to government records;”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-7(b); P.L.2024, c.16, §4 |
| `NJ-M39` | structural | none | **Upon receipt of a written complaint alleging improper denial, the council shall offer the parties the opportunity to resolve the dispute through mediation by a neutral mediator selected by the council.**<br><br>_“Upon receipt of a written complaint signed by any person alleging that a custodian of a government record has improperly denied that person access to a government record, the council shall offer the parties the opportunity to resolve the dispute through mediation.”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-7(d); P.L.2024, c.16, §4 |
| `NJ-M40` | structural | none | **If any party declines mediation or mediation fails to resolve the matter to all parties&#x27; satisfaction, the council shall initiate an investigation of the facts and circumstances in the complaint.**<br><br>_“If any party declines mediation or if mediation fails to resolve the matter to the satisfaction of all parties, the council shall initiate an investigation concerning the facts and circumstances set forth in the complaint.”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-7(e); P.L.2024, c.16, §4 |
| `NJ-M41` | structural | none | **The council shall determine whether the complaint is within its jurisdiction or is frivolous or without any reasonable factual basis; if outside jurisdiction, frivolous, or without factual basis, it shall reduce that conclusion to writing and transmit copies to the complainant and the agency.**<br><br>_“The council shall make a determination as to whether the complaint is within its jurisdiction or frivolous or without any reasonable factual basis. ... If the council shall conclude that the complaint is outside its jurisdiction, frivolous, or without factual basis, it shall reduce that conclusion to writing and transmit a copy thereof to the complainant and to the public agency that employs the records custodian against whom the complaint was filed.”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-7(e); P.L.2024, c.16, §4 |
| `NJ-M42` | structural | none | **The public agency employing the custodian shall have the opportunity to present the council with any statement or information concerning the complaint.**<br><br>_“The public agency that employs the records custodian shall have the opportunity to present the board with any statement or information concerning the complaint which the agency wishes.”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-7(e); P.L.2024, c.16, §4 |
| `NJ-M43` | structural | none | **If the council cannot determine a record&#x27;s accessibility from the complaint and the agency&#x27;s response, it shall conduct a hearing in conformity with the rules for State-agency contested cases under the Administrative Procedure Act.**<br><br>_“If the council is unable to make a determination as to a record&#x27;s accessibility based upon the complaint and the agency&#x27;s response thereto, the council shall conduct a hearing on the matter in conformity with the rules and regulations provided for hearings by a State agency in contested cases under the &quot;Administrative Procedure Act,&quot; P.L.1968, c.410 (C.52:14B-1 et seq.), insofar as they may be applicable and practicable.”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-7(e); P.L.2024, c.16, §4 |
| `NJ-M44` | structural | none | **The council shall, by majority vote of its members, render a decision as to whether the record at issue must be made available for public access.**<br><br>_“The council shall, by a majority vote of its members, render a decision as to whether the record which is the subject of the complaint is a government record which must be made available for public access pursuant to P.L.1963, c.73 (C.47:1A-1 et seq.) as amended and supplemented.”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-7(e); P.L.2024, c.16, §4 |
| `NJ-M45` | parameter · fixed | none | **The Government Records Council shall not charge any party a fee in regard to actions filed with the council.**<br><br>_“The council shall not charge any party a fee in regard to actions filed with the council.”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-7(f); P.L.2024, c.16, §4 |
| `NJ-M46` | structural | none | **The council has no jurisdiction over the Judicial or Legislative Branches of State Government or any agency, officer, or employee of those branches.**<br><br>_“The council shall not have jurisdiction over the Judicial or Legislative Branches of State Government or any agency, officer, or employee of those branches.”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-7(g); P.L.2024, c.16, §4 |
| `NJ-M47` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 90 calendar-days from complaint filing (+45-day good-cause extension), excluding mediation periods | **Beginning 18 months after the effective date of P.L.2024, c.16, the council shall adjudicate all complaints within 90 days of filing, extendable 45 days for good cause, exclusive of time during which parties are engaged in mediation.**<br><br>_“Beginning 18 months following the effective date of P.L.2024, c.16 (C.47:1A-5.1 et al.), the council shall adjudicate all complaints that come before it within 90 days of the complaint&#x27;s filing, with the ability to extend for 45 days for good cause, exclusive of any time period during which the parties are engaged in a mediation process pursuant to this section.”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-7(e); P.L.2024, c.16, §4 |
| `NJ-M48` | structural | none | **At the council&#x27;s request, a public agency shall produce documents and ensure the attendance of witnesses for the council&#x27;s investigation of any complaint or the holding of any hearing.**<br><br>_“At the request of the council, a public agency shall produce documents and ensure the attendance of witnesses with respect to the council&#x27;s investigation of any complaint or the holding of any hearing.”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-7(c); P.L.2024, c.16, §4 |
| `NJ-M49` | structural | none | **The council may go into closed session during the portion of any proceeding in which the contents of a contested record would be disclosed.**<br><br>_“The council shall be subject to the provisions of the &quot;Open Public Meetings Act,&quot; P.L.1975, c.231 (C.10:4-6), except that the council may go into closed session during that portion of any proceeding during which the contents of a contested record would be disclosed.”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-7(f); P.L.2024, c.16, §4 |
| `NJ-M50` | structural | sets-deadline · amendment within 90 calendar-days of the Sept. 3, 2024 effective date (window closed ~Dec. 2, 2024) | **Complaints/appeals pending before the GRC or Superior Court that were filed anonymously or under a fictitious identity before the Act&#x27;s effective date may be dismissed with prejudice on the agency&#x27;s motion unless the complainant amends to accurately identify name and mailing address within 90 days of the effective date.**<br><br>_“All complaints and appeals pending before the Government Records Council or the Superior Court filed prior to the effective date of P.L.2024, c.16 (C.47:1A-5.1 et al.), either anonymously or using a fictitious name or identity, may be dismissed with prejudice upon a motion by the public agency, unless the complainant files an amendment to their complaint that accurately identifies their name and mailing address within 90 days of the effective date of P.L.2024, c.16 (C.47:1A-5.1 et al.).”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-7.1(a); P.L.2024, c.16, §9 |

### Enforcement

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `NJ-M51` | structural | none | **A requestor who prevails in any proceeding may be entitled to a reasonable attorney&#x27;s fee (discretionary).**<br><br>_“A requestor who prevails in any proceeding may be entitled to a reasonable attorney&#x27;s fee. While the court or Government Records Council may award a reasonable attorney&#x27;s fee to a prevailing party in any proceeding,”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-6; P.L.2024, c.16, §3 |
| `NJ-M52` | structural | none | **If the agency is determined to have unreasonably denied access, acted in bad faith, or knowingly and willfully violated OPRA, the court or GRC shall award a reasonable attorney&#x27;s fee.**<br><br>_“if the public agency has been determined to have unreasonably denied access, acted in bad faith, or knowingly and willfully violated P.L.1963, c.73 (C.47:1A-1 et seq.), then the court or Government Records Council shall award a reasonable attorney&#x27;s fee.”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-6; P.L.2024, c.16, §3 |
| `NJ-M53` | structural | terminal · 7 business-days from service of the Superior Court action or GRC complaint | **If the agency produces the records within seven business days of service of a Superior Court action or GRC complaint, upon notification the matter shall be dismissed without prejudice; the requestor may still be entitled to a reasonable attorney&#x27;s fee if the custodian knew or should have known the denial violated OPRA.**<br><br>_“If the records sought are produced by the public agency within seven business days of service of an action in Superior Court or a complaint before the Government Records Council, upon notification to the Superior Court or the Government Records Council, the matter shall be dismissed without prejudice and the requestor may be entitled to a reasonable attorney&#x27;s fee if the custodian knew or should have known that the denial of access violated P.L.1963, c.73 (C.47:1A-1 et seq.).”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-6; P.L.2024, c.16, §3 |
| `NJ-M54` | parameter · fixed | none · amounts: $1,000 initial / $2,500 second (within 10 yrs) / $5,000 third (within 10 yrs) | **Where a public official/officer/employee/custodian is found to have knowingly and willfully violated OPRA and to have unreasonably denied access under the totality of the circumstances, the employing agency is subject to a civil penalty of $1,000 (initial), $2,500 (second within 10 years), and $5,000 (third within 10 years); penalties may be imposed by the courts or the GRC.**<br><br>_“If a public official, officer, employee, or custodian is found to have knowingly and willfully violated P.L.1963, c.73 (C.47:1A-1 et seq.), as amended and supplemented, and to have unreasonably denied access under the totality of the circumstances, the public agency that employs the custodian, officer, employee, or official shall be subject to a civil penalty of $1,000 for an initial violation, $2,500 for a second violation that occurs within 10 years of an initial violation, and $5,000 for a third violation that occurs within 10 years of an initial violation. The penalties authorized pursuant to this subsection may be imposed by the courts or by the Government Records Council.”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-11(a); P.L.2024, c.16, §5 |

The court may issue the protective order if it finds by clear and convincing evidence that the requestor has sought records under P.L.1963, c.73 (C.47:1A-1 et seq.) with the intent to substantially interrupt the performance of government function.”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-5.1(a); P.L.2024, c.16, §7 |

| `NJ-M61` | structural | none | **Requests by a labor organization or CBA-signatory contractor seeking information material to enforcing State/federal wage-hour, workplace-safety, or public-procurement/bidding statutes (not sought in furtherance of litigation discovery) shall not be considered intended to interrupt government functions and cannot form the basis of a protective-order complaint.**<br><br>_“Requests for government records filed by a labor organization or by a contractor signatory to a collective bargaining agreement seeking information material to the enforcement of State or federal statutes or regulations regarding, but not limited to, wage and hour protections, workplace safety, or public procurement and public bidding, including, but not limited to, requests for certified payrolls or information about all bids submitted in response to a public procurement process subsequent to the deadline for the submission of all bids for that solicitation, when the request by the labor organization or contractor signatory is not sought in connection to or in furtherance of discovery requests in a court proceeding, shall not be considered to be intended to interrupt government functions, and shall not form the basis for the filing of a complaint under this section.”_ | N.J.S.A. 47:1A-5.1(c); P.L.2024, c.16, §7 |

### Material negatives

- `clarification.deficiency_cure_clock` — OPRA contains no mandatory clarification/deficiency-cure step or nonresponse-abandonment clock. When a request is incomplete, over-broad, or requires more than reasonable effort to clarify, the statute PERMITS denial (N.J.S.A. 47:1A-5(f), NJ-A14/NJ-A15) rather than requiring the custodian to seek clarification and toll a clock. The only affirmative negotiation duty is the &#x27;reasonable solution&#x27; attempt for requests that would substantially disrupt operations (NJ-A24). No abandonment deadline for an unclarified request was found.
- `intake.acknowledgment_window` — OPRA has no separate acknowledgment-of-receipt step distinct from the substantive grant/deny response deadline. There is no statutory duty to send an interim acknowledgment; the first required communication is the grant/deny (or storage/archive advisory) within the response window (Deadlines sibling).
- `eligibility.identity_verification` — No requestor identity-verification or proof-of-identity requirement at intake. Requests may be anonymous (NJ-A04); the only consequence of not accurately identifying is loss of standing to sue and suspension of the response duty until the requestor makes contact (NJ-A05). No government-ID or notarization requirement found.
- `search.burden_hour_threshold` — No fixed numeric search-burden threshold (e.g., a maximum number of search hours or a defined &#x27;unduly burdensome&#x27; cap) exists. Burden limits are expressed as soft standards - &#x27;substantially disrupt agency operations&#x27; (NJ-A24) and &#x27;reasonable time period&#x27; for correspondence (NJ-A26) - with no day/hour count.
- `coverage.exemptions_out_of_scope` — The confidentiality/exemption catalogue within N.J.S.A. 47:1A-1.1 (deliberative/ACD material, criminal investigatory records, victims&#x27; records, legislative constituent records, etc.), the enacted &#x27;personal identifying information&#x27; redaction definition, and the judicial-officer home-address protection (N.J.S.A. 47:1B-1/-2) are Denials/Redaction scope and are deferred to the sibling agent; only the core government-record and public-agency coverage definitions are captured here.
- `response.acknowledgment_window` — OPRA has no separate receipt-acknowledgment step or deadline distinct from the substantive grant/deny response. The seven-business-day (or 14) response under 47:1A-5(i)(1) is the sole required response; there is no statutory &#x27;we received your request&#x27; acknowledgment clock.
- `clarification.cure_clock` — Within my scope areas, no statutory clock or mandatory back-and-forth to cure a deficient request appears. The custodian may deny incomplete/overbroad requests (47:1A-5(f),(g)) but no fixed cure window is prescribed. Intake/clarification is primarily a sibling area; noted here only to record the absence of a cure deadline.
- `redaction.broad_pii` — A broad mandatory redaction of email, medical, financial, birthdate, or general &#x27;personal information&#x27; was proposed in earlier reprints of S2930 but was DELETED before enactment. Enacted 47:1A-5(a) mandates redaction only of SSN, credit card number, personal telephone number, driver license number, and covered-officer home addresses. Do not treat the broad PII list as an operative redaction rule.
- `intake.data_broker_denial` — A provision requiring denial of all requests by or for data brokers (and a data-broker registration/fee scheme) was in earlier reprints but was DELETED and NOT enacted. No data-broker processing rule exists in current OPRA.
- `special_records.indecent_image_nondisclosure` — P.L.2024, c.16, s.10 bars a non-subject who obtained a photo/video via OPRA from disclosing indecent/graphic images of intimate parts without consent (disorderly persons offense). Excluded from rules: it is a downstream use-restriction with a criminal penalty, not something the platform does differently while processing a request.
- `production.format_priority` — Beyond &#x27;provide in the medium/format maintained&#x27; and &#x27;convert if available without substantial IT manipulation&#x27; (47:1A-5(d)), OPRA states no ranked hierarchy of production formats the platform must apply.
- `fee.indigent_or_public_interest_waiver` — OPRA creates no general fee waiver for indigent, non-profit, or public-interest requestors. The only statutory zero-fee cases are crime victims (NJ-M05) and records available on a public agency website (NJ-M04). Absence of a found rule is not proof none exists.
- `fee.inspection_no_charge` — OPRA attaches fees only to copies/duplication; no fee is prescribed for mere inspection or examination during business hours (47:1A-5(a),(b)). No affirmative &#x27;inspection is free&#x27; clause exists, so this is stated cautiously as an absence rather than an express rule.
- `payment.late_payment_interest` — No statutory provision imposes interest, late fees, or penalties on a requestor for unpaid or delayed OPRA fee payments; the only leverage is prepayment/deposit (NJ-M14, NJ-M15) and the 14-business-day record-retrieval window.
- `fee.special_service_charge_cap` — No numeric cap on cumulative special service charges (47:1A-5(c),(d)); charges are bounded only by &#x27;reasonable / actual direct cost&#x27; with a rebuttable presumption of reasonableness (NJ-M06, NJ-M09, NJ-M10).
- `review.mediation_time_limit` — No statutory deadline governs completion of GRC mediation; mediation time is expressly excluded from the 90-day adjudication clock (NJ-M47), leaving mediation duration open-ended.
- `appeal.grc_bond_requirement` — No bond or filing fee is required to appeal to the GRC (the statute affirmatively bars GRC fees, NJ-M45). No provision addresses a bond for Superior Court OPRA actions within OPRA itself.

### Structural branches

- `custody.custodian_designation` — NJ-A06: custodian identity forks by entity type - municipal clerk for a municipality vs. a formally designated officer for any other public agency - determining who must receive and answer the request.
- `custody.forward_to_custodian` — NJ-A30/NJ-A31: a request landing on a non-custodian must be forwarded or redirected to the custodian, and is not deemed submitted (clock does not start) until the custodian receives it - a routing stage the engine must model before intake completes.
- `custody.referral_to_originating_agency` — NJ-A32/NJ-A33: for records held only via remote access or courtesy copy from another (non-judicial-branch) agency, the request branches to a referral path - custodian directs the requestor to the originating agency within seven business days and the request is deemed completed.
- `custody.website_redirect` — NJ-A34: for records readily available in complete form on the agency website, the request branches to a self-service path - custodian may require retrieval from the website while providing locating directions - with downstream fulfillment/assist/physical-copy sub-branches (deferred to Deadlines/Fees).
- `intake.legal_proceeding_court_order_bar` — NJ-A22/NJ-A23: requests branch on a mandatory legal-proceeding certification; a party to a proceeding is barred from OPRA for records subject to a court order/pending discovery, with a labor-organization/CBA-contractor carve-out - a gate the engine must evaluate before processing.
- `clarification.substantial_disruption_resolution` — NJ-A24: requests that would substantially disrupt operations branch into a mandatory notify-and-negotiate-a-reasonable-solution stage before any denial is permitted.
- `deadlines.storage_production_path` — Storage/archived records follow a distinct path: advise within 7/14 business days (NJ-T11), make available within 21 business days of advisement (NJ-T12), else deemed denied (NJ-T13).
- `production.website_fulfillment_path` — Website-redirect fulfillment fork: redirect with directions (NJ-T31) -&gt; deemed fulfilled after 7 business days or completeness carve-back (NJ-T32) -&gt; good-faith assistance within 7 business days (NJ-T33) -&gt; physical copy within 7 business days (NJ-T34).
- `routing.redirect_originating_agency` — Not-in-possession/courtesy-copy fork: custodian may decline and must redirect the requestor within 7 business days to the originating (non-judicial-branch) agency, completing the request (NJ-T16).
- `deadlines.emergency_suspension` — Declared-emergency fork suspending the (i)(1) and subsection-e deadlines, replaced by a reasonable-effort soft standard (NJ-T15).
- `special_records.election_agency_path` — Election-agency-specific processing: limited redaction (NJ-T41), 2-business-day immediate access (NJ-T42), election-eve noon deadlines (NJ-T43), website posting satisfies deadlines (NJ-T44).
- `special_records.harassment_protective_order` — Court protective-order path that can toll, limit, or eliminate the agency&#x27;s duty to respond to a vexatious requestor (NJ-T46).
- `redaction.covered_person_address_path` — Daniel&#x27;s Law path: OIP-approved covered-person home-address redaction (NJ-T36) with a 30-day agency compliance deadline (NJ-T38) and a triggered 14-business-day review window (NJ-T03).
- `production.commercial_expedite_option` — Commercial requestor may elect 7-business-day delivery instead of the default 14, for up to 2x production cost (NJ-T06).
- `appeal.forum_election` — A denied requestor must elect one of two mutually exclusive review paths within 45 days: an action in Superior Court before a designated judge, OR a complaint to the Government Records Council (&#x27;in lieu of&#x27; filing in Superior Court). NJ-M28 / NJ-M29.
- `fee.commercial_purpose_classification` — Certification of &#x27;commercial purpose&#x27; forks the request into a distinct track: a 14-business-day response window (vs 7), an optional 2x expedite fee, and miscertification-penalty exposure. NJ-M13 / NJ-M12 / NJ-M55.
- `denial.deemed_denial_terminal` — Custodian silence past the 7/14-business-day window converts the request to a constructive denial (terminal state opening the 45-day appeal clock), unless the requestor withheld accurate identity/contact info, which pauses the obligation. NJ-M25 / NJ-M26.
- `denial.storage_archived_path` — A record in storage/archive routes to a separate timeline: advise within 7/14 days, produce within no more than 21 business days, else deemed denied. NJ-M27.
- `review.grc_mediation_to_hearing_pipeline` — GRC processing is a staged pipeline: mediation offer -&gt; (on decline/failure) investigation and frivolous/jurisdiction screen -&gt; agency response -&gt; APA contested-case hearing if accessibility cannot be decided on the papers -&gt; majority-vote decision. NJ-M39 through NJ-M44.
- `enforcement.protective_order_path` — An agency may open a separate Superior Court track (verified complaint, clear-and-convincing standard) to obtain a protective order that limits or eliminates its future duty to respond to a requestor found to intend substantial interruption of government function, subject to a labor-organization carve-out. NJ-M58 / NJ-M59 / NJ-M60 / NJ-M61.
- `denial.substantial_disruption_negotiation` — A request that would substantially disrupt agency operations routes to a mandatory notice-and-negotiation step before the custodian may deny access. NJ-M21.

---

Rev. Stat. § 239.010(1) |
| `NV-0002` | structural | none | **Access runs to &#x27;any person&#x27;; the statute imposes no residency or citizenship condition on who may request public records.**<br><br>_“all public books and public records of a governmental entity must be open at all times during office hours to inspection by any person”_ | Nev. Rev. Stat. § 239.010(1) |
| `NV-0003` | structural | none | **Access runs to &#x27;any person&#x27;; the statute requires no statement of purpose or justification for a request.**<br><br>_“all public books and public records of a governmental entity must be open at all times during office hours to inspection by any person”_ | Nev. Rev. Stat. § 239.010(1) |

Rev. Stat. § 239.0107(1) |

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |

Rev. Stat. § 239.005(5) |

Rev. Stat. § 239.0107(1)(b) |

### Deadlines

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `NV-0006` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 5 business-days from custodian&#x27;s receipt of request | **Not later than the end of the fifth business day after receipt of a request, the entity must allow inspection/copying or provide the copy (or give one of the alternative statutory responses).**<br><br>_“Not later than the end of the fifth business day after the date on which the person who has legal custody or control of a public book or record of a governmental entity receives a written or oral request from a person to inspect, copy or receive a copy of the public book or record, a governmental entity shall do one of the following, as applicable: (a) Except as otherwise provided in subsection 2, allow the person to inspect or copy the public book or record or, if the request is for the person to receive a copy of the public book or record, provide such a copy to the person.”_ | Nev. ### Production

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `NV-0007` | structural | sets-deadline · undefined-soft: &quot;as expeditiously as practicable&quot; from receipt of request | **If a record is readily available for inspection or copying, the custodian must allow the requester to inspect, copy or receive a copy as expeditiously as practicable.**<br><br>_“If a public book or record of a governmental entity is readily available for inspection or copying, the person who has legal custody or control of the public book or record shall allow a person who has submitted a request to inspect, copy or receive a copy of a public book or record as expeditiously as practicable.”_ | Nev. Rev. Stat. § 239.0107(2) |
| `NV-0014` | structural | none | **If requested, the entity must provide a copy in an electronic format by electronic medium, unless the record was neither created nor available electronically, or doing so would give access to proprietary software or require producing non-separable confidential information.**<br><br>_“If requested, a governmental entity shall provide a copy of a public record in an electronic format by means of an electronic medium. Nothing in this subsection requires a governmental entity to provide a copy of a public record in an electronic format or by means of an electronic medium if: (a) The public record: (1) Was not created or prepared in an electronic format; and (2) Is not available in an electronic format; or (b) Providing the public record in an electronic format or by means of an electronic medium would: (1) Give access to proprietary software; or (2) Require the production of information that is confidential and that cannot be redacted, deleted, concealed or separated from information that is not otherwise confidential.”_ | Nev. Rev. Stat. § 239.010(4) |

Rev. Stat. § 239.010(5)(a) |
| `NV-0016` | structural | none | **On request, the custodian must prepare the copy of the record and must not require the requester to prepare the copy himself or herself.**<br><br>_“(b) Except as otherwise provided in NRS 239.030, shall, upon request, prepare the copy of the public record and shall not require the person who has requested the copy to prepare the copy himself or herself.”_ | Nev. | `NV-0017` | structural | none | **An officer having custody of non-confidential public records must furnish certified copies to any person who requests them and pays or tenders the prescribed copying/certifying fees.**<br><br>_“Every officer having custody of public records, the contents of which are not declared by law to be confidential, shall furnish copies certified to be correct to any person who requests them and pays or tenders such fees as may be prescribed for the service of copying and certifying.”_ | Nev. Rev. Stat. § 239.030 |
| `NV-0019` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 3 working-days from county recorder&#x27;s receipt of request | **When a county recorder receives a request for a particular item that has been removed to storage, the recorder must produce a microfilmed copy or the original within three working days.**<br><br>_“If a county recorder receives a request for a particular item which has been stored pursuant to subsection 1, the county recorder shall produce a microfilmed copy of such item or the original within 3 working days.”_ | Nev. Rev. Stat. § 239.015(2) |

### Redaction

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `NV-0013` | structural | none | **The entity must not deny a request on the basis that the record contains confidential information if it can redact, delete, conceal or separate (including electronically) the confidential information from the non-confidential information.**<br><br>_“A governmental entity that has legal custody or control of a public book or record shall not deny a request made pursuant to subsection 1 to inspect or copy or receive a copy of a public book or record on the basis that the requested public book or record contains information that is confidential if the governmental entity can redact, delete, conceal or separate, including, without limitation, electronically, the confidential information from the information included in the public book or record that is not otherwise confidential.”_ | Nev. Rev. Stat. § 239.010(3) |

Rev. Stat. § 239.030 |

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |

| `NV-0020` | parameter · ceiling | none · ceiling: actual cost of providing the copy | **A governmental entity may charge a fee for a copy, but the fee must not exceed the actual cost to provide the copy unless a specific statute or regulation sets the fee.**<br><br>_“Except as otherwise provided in this subsection, a governmental entity may charge a fee for providing a copy of a public record. Such a fee must not exceed the actual cost to the governmental entity to provide the copy of the public record unless a specific statute or regulation sets a fee that the governmental entity must charge for the copy.”_ | Nev. Rev. Stat. § 239.052(1) |
| `NV-0021` | structural | none | **&#x27;Actual cost&#x27; means direct cost of providing the copy (ink, toner, paper, media, postage) and excludes any cost the entity incurs regardless of whether a copy is requested.**<br><br>_“‘Actual cost’ means the direct cost incurred by a governmental entity in the provision of a public record, including, without limitation, the cost of ink, toner, paper, media and postage. The term does not include a cost that a governmental entity incurs regardless of whether or not a person requests a copy of a particular public record.”_ | Nev. Rev. Stat. § 239.005(1) |
| `NV-0022` | structural | none | **A governmental entity must not charge a fee for a copy if a specific statute or regulation requires it to provide the copy without charge.**<br><br>_“A governmental entity shall not charge a fee for providing a copy of a public record if a specific statute or regulation requires the governmental entity to provide the copy without charge.”_ | Nev. Rev. Stat. § 239.052(1) |
| `NV-0023` | structural | none | **A governmental entity may waive all or part of a copy fee if it adopts a written waiver policy and posts a legible sign or notice of the policy&#x27;s terms at each office providing copies.**<br><br>_“A governmental entity may waive all or a portion of a charge or fee for a copy of a public record if the governmental entity: (a) Adopts a written policy to waive all or a portion of a charge or fee for a copy of a public record; and (b) Posts, in a conspicuous place at each office in which the governmental entity provides copies of public records, a legible sign or notice that states the terms of the policy.”_ | Nev. Rev. Stat. § 239.052(2) |
| `NV-0024` | parameter · ceiling | none · ceiling: $0.50/page | **The fee for a copy of a record in the custody of a governmental law library must not exceed 50 cents per page.**<br><br>_“The fee for providing a copy of a public book or record in the custody of a law library operated by a governmental entity must not exceed 50 cents per page.”_ | Nev. Rev. Stat. § 239.052(4) |
| `NV-0025` | parameter · fixed | none · fixed: court reporter&#x27;s contract per-page fee + actual medium cost | **For a copy of a certified-court-reporter transcript of an administrative proceeding, the entity must charge, on top of actual medium cost, a per-page fee equal to the court reporter&#x27;s contract per-page fee, and remit that amount to the reporter.**<br><br>_“If a person requests a copy of a transcript of an administrative proceeding that has been transcribed by a certified court reporter, a governmental entity shall charge, in addition to the actual cost of the medium in which the copy of the transcript is provided, a fee for each page provided which is equal in amount to the fee per page charged by the court reporter for the copy of the transcript, as set forth in the contract between the governmental entity and the court reporter.”_ | Nev. Rev. Stat. § 239.053(1) |
| `NV-0026` | parameter · soft-standard | none | **A fee for information from a geographic information system may include, in addition to actual medium cost, the reasonable costs of data gathering/entry, database maintenance, hardware, software, quality control, and consultation with personnel.**<br><br>_“A fee for the provision of information from a geographic information system may include, in addition to the actual cost of the medium in which the information is provided, the reasonable costs related to: (a) The gathering and entry of data into the system; (b) Maintenance and updating of the database of the system; (c) Hardware; (d) Software; (e) Quality control; and (f) Consultation with personnel of the governmental entity.”_ | Nev. Rev. Stat. § 239.054(1) |
| `NV-0027` | structural | none | **When a public record copy is required by the federal Department of Veterans Affairs to determine benefit eligibility, the custodian must provide a certified copy without charge to the applicant, their representative, or the VA.**<br><br>_“Whenever a copy of any public record is required by the Department of Veterans Affairs to be used in determining the eligibility of any person to participate in benefits made available by the Department of Veterans Affairs, the official charged with the custody of such public record shall, without charge, provide the applicant for the benefit or any person acting on behalf of the applicant or the representative of the Department of Veterans Affairs with a certified copy or copies of such records.”_ | Nev. Rev. Stat. § 239.020 |

### Appeals

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `NV-0028` | structural | none | **If a request is denied or unreasonably delayed, or the requester believes the fee is excessive or improper, the requester may apply to the district court in the county where the record is located for an order permitting inspection/copying, requiring a copy, or granting fee relief.**<br><br>_“If a request for inspection, copying or copies of a public book or record open to inspection and copying is denied or unreasonably delayed or if a person who requests a copy of a public book or record believes that the fee charged by the governmental entity for providing the copy of the public book or record is excessive or improper, the requester may apply to the district court in the county in which the book or record is located for an order: (a) Permitting the requester to inspect or copy the book or record; (b) Requiring the person who has legal custody or control of the public book or record to provide a copy to the requester; or (c) Providing relief relating to the amount of the fee, as applicable.”_ | Nev. Rev. Stat. § 239.011(1) |
| `NV-0029` | structural | none | **For a record held in governmental custody or control for at least 30 years, a person may apply to district court for an order to inspect or copy it notwithstanding a confidentiality declaration, with a rebuttable presumption of entitlement (subject to stated exceptions).**<br><br>_“notwithstanding any provision of law that has declared a public book or record, or a part thereof, to be confidential, if a public book or record has been in the legal custody or control of one or more governmental entities for at least 30 years, a person may apply to the district court of the county in which the governmental entity that currently has legal custody or control of the public book or record is located for an order directing that governmental entity to allow the person to inspect or copy the public book or record, or a part thereof. ... There is a rebuttable presumption that a person who applies for an order as described in subsection 1 is entitled to inspect or copy the public book or record, or a part thereof, that the person seeks to inspect or copy.”_ | Nev. Rev. Stat. § 239.0115(1)-(2) |

### Material negatives

- `deadline.acknowledgment_window` — No separate acknowledgment/receipt step distinct from the substantive response. NRS 239.0107(1) sets a single 5-business-day window within which the entity must produce, refer, extend, or deny; there is no earlier statutory duty merely to acknowledge receipt.
- `payment.deposit_advance` — No statutory authority in Chapter 239 for an advance deposit or prepayment before search/production of ordinary copies. Fee tender is expressly required only for certified copies (NRS 239.030, NV-0018).
- `fee.commercial_purpose_distinction` — Chapter 239 draws no distinction between commercial and non-commercial requesters and creates no purpose-based fee tier; the actual-cost ceiling (NRS 239.052) applies without regard to requester purpose.
- `fee.personnel_labor_charge` — No fee is authorized for staff time, labor, or overhead. The &#x27;actual cost&#x27; definition (NRS 239.005(1)) excludes costs incurred regardless of a request, and 2019 SB287 repealed the former extraordinary-use-of-personnel fee (NRS 239.055). Absence of a labor charge is affirmative, not merely unfound.
- `enforcement.deemed_denial_clock` — No fixed constructive/deemed-denial trigger (no &#x27;silence for N days = denial&#x27;). The only silence-related trigger is the soft &#x27;unreasonably delayed&#x27; standard for a district-court application under NRS 239.011(1) (NV-0028).
- `appeal.administrative.internal_stage` — No internal or administrative appeal stage exists in Chapter 239; the exclusive review mechanism is a district-court application (NRS 239.011). Emitted as a negative so it does not falsely merge with other states&#x27; internal-appeal stages.
- `deadline.time_computation` — Chapter 239 does not define &#x27;business day&#x27; or &#x27;working day&#x27;, nor does it provide tolling, holiday-exclusion, or day-counting rules for the 5-business-day (NRS 239.0107) or 3-working-day (NRS 239.015(2)) periods. Later pass may need general NRS time-computation rules or municipal practice.
- `clarification.nonresponse_abandonment` — No provision deeming a request abandoned or closed if the requester fails to respond to a narrowing/clarification effort. NRS 239.0107(1)(c)(2) imposes a duty to assist but attaches no requester-side deadline or abandonment consequence.
- `preservation.litigation_hold` — Chapter 239 contains no preservation/litigation hold triggered by the making of a records request or by an appeal on that request. (General records-retention schedules exist under NRS 239.051-239.125 but are routine retention, excluded.)

### Structural branches

- `deadline.extension_notice` — NRS 239.0107(1)(c)(1): a required written notice, given when the entity cannot produce by day five, declaring the earliest reasonably-believed availability date — a distinct processing stage that pauses production against a self-set soft date (NV-0008).
- `communications.availability_reexplanation` — NRS 239.0107(1)(c)(1): a recurring follow-up written explanation plus a new availability date whenever a previously represented date lapses (NV-0009).
- `clarification.narrowing_assistance` — NRS 239.0107(1)(c)(2): a mandatory &#x27;reasonable effort&#x27; to help the requester focus the request when production cannot occur by day five (NV-0010).
- `routing.no_custody_referral` — NRS 239.0107(1)(b): a referral branch — written notice of no custody plus name/address of the entity that has the record (NV-0011).
- `denial.written_notice_with_citation` — NRS 239.0107(1)(d): the confidential-denial branch requiring written notice with a specific legal citation (NV-0012).
- `redaction.segregability_required` — NRS 239.010(3): mandatory partial-disclosure/redaction path barring denial of a record that mixes confidential and non-confidential content (NV-0013).
- `production.electronic_format_on_request` — NRS 239.010(4): electronic-format production branch with its four statutory exceptions (NV-0014).
- `appeal.judicial.district_court_application` — NRS 239.011(1): the sole external review path — district-court application on denial, unreasonable delay, or fee dispute; Nevada has no internal administrative appeal stage (NV-0028).
- `appeal.judicial.thirty_year_confidential_records` — NRS 239.0115: a separate district-court path to reach records held 30+ years, carrying a rebuttable presumption of access and enumerated exceptions (NV-0029).
- `fee.veterans_affairs_exemption` — NRS 239.020: a mandatory free-certified-copy branch for federal VA benefit-eligibility determinations (NV-0027).

## New York (NY) — 41 rules

### Eligibility

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `NY-0001` | structural | none | **Any member of the public may request access to the records of government; access is a public right exercisable by any person.**<br><br>_“The legislature therefore declares that government is the public&#x27;s business and that the public, individually and collectively and represented by a free press, should have access to the records of government in accordance with the provisions of this article.”_ | N.Y. Pub. Off. Law § 84 |
| `NY-0002` | structural | none | **A requester need not state a reason, need, good faith, or legitimate purpose to obtain accessible records.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“New York&#x27;s Court of Appeals and the Committee on Open Government hold that FOIL does not require the party requesting records to make any showing of need, good faith or legitimate purpose as a condition to access.”_ | Matter of M. Farbman &amp; Sons v. N.Y.C. Health &amp; Hosps. Corp., 62 N.Y.2d 75 (1984); Committee on Open Government advisory opinions |
| `NY-0003` | structural | none | **Accessible records are equally available to any person regardless of residency, citizenship, or status; an agency may not condition access on proof of residency or identity.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“When records are accessible under the Freedom of Information Law, they must be made equally available to any person, irrespective of the status, residence, interest or intended use of the person making the request.”_ | Committee on Open Government advisory opinions (construing N.Y. Pub. Off. Law art. 6) |

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `NY-0004` | structural | none | **The agency&#x27;s five-business-day response duty is triggered by receipt of a written request for a record.**<br><br>_“Each entity subject to the provisions of this article, within five business days of the receipt of a written request for a record reasonably described, shall make such record available to the person requesting it, deny such request in writing or furnish a written acknowledgement of the receipt of such request...”_ | N.Y. Pub. Off. Law § 89(3)(a) |
| `NY-0006` | structural | none | **An entity with reasonable means available shall accept requests submitted by email and respond by email, unless the request seeks a response in another form.**<br><br>_“All entities shall, provided such entity has reasonable means available, accept requests for records submitted in the form of electronic mail and shall respond to such requests by electronic mail, using forms, to the extent practicable, consistent with the form or forms developed by the committee on open government pursuant to subdivision one of this section and provided that the written requests do not seek a response in some other form.”_ | N.Y. Pub. Off. Law § 89(3)(b) |
| `NY-0007` | structural | none | **The Committee on Open Government develops a request form, made available on the internet, that the public may use to request a record.**<br><br>_“The committee shall: ... v. develop a form, which shall be made available on the internet, that may be used by the public to request a record;”_ | N.Y. Pub. Off. Law § 89(1)(b)(v) |

Pub. Off. Law § 89(3)(a) |

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `NY-0036` | structural | none | **A person denied access to a record may appeal in writing to the head, chief executive, or governing body of the entity (or its designee).**<br><br>_“Except as provided in subdivision five of this section, any person denied access to a record may within thirty days appeal in writing such denial to the head, chief executive or governing body of the entity, or the person therefor designated by such head, chief executive, or governing body...”_ | N.Y. Pub. Off. Law § 89(4)(a) |
| `NY-0037` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 30 days from denial (requester&#x27;s filing window) | **The requester must file the written administrative appeal within thirty days of the denial.**<br><br>_“...any person denied access to a record may within thirty days appeal in writing such denial to the head, chief executive or governing body of the entity...”_ | N.Y. Pub. Off. Law § 89(4)(a) |
| `NY-0038` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 10 business-days from receipt of appeal | **Within ten business days of receiving the appeal, the appeal authority shall fully explain in writing the reasons for further denial or provide access to the record.**<br><br>_“...who shall within ten business days of the receipt of such appeal fully explain in writing to the person requesting the record the reasons for further denial, or provide access to the record sought.”_ | N.Y. Pub. Off. Law § 89(4)(a) |
| `NY-0039` | structural | sets-deadline · undefined-soft: &quot;immediately&quot; from receipt of appeal / determination | **Each agency shall immediately forward to the Committee on Open Government a copy of the appeal when received and the ensuing determination.**<br><br>_“In addition, each agency shall immediately forward to the committee on open government a copy of such appeal when received by the agency and the ensuing determination thereon.”_ | N.Y. Pub. Off. Law § 89(4)(a) |
| `NY-0040` | structural | terminal · on lapse of the § 89(4)(a) 10-business-day appeal determination | **An agency&#x27;s failure to conform to the appeal provisions of paragraph (a) constitutes a denial (opening judicial review).**<br><br>_“Failure by an agency to conform to the provisions of paragraph (a) of this subdivision shall constitute a denial.”_ | N.Y. Pub. Off. Law § 89(4)(b) |
| `NY-0041` | structural | none | **A person denied access in an administrative appeal determination may bring a proceeding to review that denial under CPLR Article 78; the agency bears the burden of proving the record falls within an exemption.**<br><br>_“Except as provided in subdivision five of this section, a person denied access to a record in an appeal determination under the provisions of paragraph (a) of this subdivision may bring a proceeding for review of such denial pursuant to article seventy-eight of the civil practice law and rules. In the event that access to any record is denied pursuant to the provisions of subdivision two of section eighty-seven of this article, the agency involved shall have the burden of proving that such record falls within the provisions of such subdivision two.”_ | N.Y. Pub. Off. Law § 89(4)(b) |

### Material negatives

- `clarification.cure_period` — No explicit statutory clarification/cure clock. FOIL&#x27;s only intake-adequacy standard is that a record be &#x27;reasonably described&#x27; (§ 89(3)(a)); there is no statutory deadline for a requester to narrow or clarify a request. Absence of a found rule is not proof none exists (COOG guidance encourages agencies to assist).
- `clarification.nonresponse_abandonment` — No statutory abandonment/closure trigger for requester non-response (e.g., failure to pay or clarify). Statute conditions production on &#x27;payment of, or offer to pay&#x27; (§ 89(3)(a)) but sets no time limit after which a request is deemed abandoned.
- `payment.deposit_threshold` — No statutory advance-deposit percentage or dollar threshold in Article 6. Production is gated on &#x27;payment of, or offer to pay&#x27; the prescribed fee (§ 89(3)(a)); no formula (e.g., &#x27;50% deposit if over $X&#x27;) is provided by statute.
- `deadlines.final_production_hard_deadline` — No fixed numeric outer deadline for actual production. After the 5-business-day response and the 20-business-day checkpoint, the operative standard is a &#x27;date certain within a reasonable period&#x27; (§ 89(3)(a)) — a soft standard, not a hard statewide cap.
- `fee.fixed_statewide_rate` — There is no fixed statewide per-page fee. The 25 cents/page figure is a CEILING (§ 87(1)(b)(iii)); municipalities set their own per-page fee up to that cap, and no statutory floor exists. (Captured affirmatively as NY-0024.)
- `production.rolling_installments` — No statutory requirement or prohibition governing rolling/installment production of voluminous requests; the statute addresses timing via the acknowledgement/date-certain mechanism rather than mandated installments.
- `time.business_day_definition` — Article 6 uses &#x27;business days&#x27; for the 5-, 20-, and 10-day windows but &#x27;days&#x27; (unqualified) for the 30-day appeal-filing window (§ 89(4)(a)); the article does not itself define how business days are counted (e.g., holidays).
- EXCLUDED — § 89(4)(c) attorney&#x27;s fees and litigation costs (&#x27;may assess&#x27; / &#x27;shall assess&#x27; where petitioner &#x27;substantially prevailed&#x27; and agency &#x27;had no reasonable basis&#x27;): a Remedy on the Remove list. The linked constructive-denial trigger it references is captured at NY-0013/NY-0040.
- EXCLUDED — § 89(4)(d) and § 89(5)(d) appellate procedure (preference, 60-day abandonment, CPLR 5513 appeals): litigation procedure, not request-handling behavior.
- EXCLUDED — § 89(4)(b) / § 89(5)(f) burden of proof in an Article 78 proceeding (agency must prove exemption): litigation burden; the operational duty to give particularized justification is captured at NY-0034.
- EXCLUDED — § 87(3)(a)-(b) duty to maintain a final-vote record and an employee name/title/salary roster, and § 87(3)(c) annually-updated subject-matter list: general recordkeeping obligations (Remove list), not per-request processing.
- EXCLUDED — § 89(8) criminal violation for willful concealment/destruction of a record: a penalty provision (Remove list).
- EXCLUDED — § 89(7) non-disclosure of home addresses of officers/employees/retirees/applicants: an exemption (special record content), not a processing step; belongs to the exemption catalog rather than the workflow.
- SCOPE FLAG (not excluded, but municipally inapplicable) — § 89(3)(c) online-submission duty and § 87(3)(c)/§ 87(4)(c) website-posting duties apply to &#x27;state agency&#x27; only, so they do not bind ordinary municipalities. Municipal platforms should treat these as state-agency configuration.

### Structural branches

- `deadlines.acknowledgment_path` — Within 5 business days the agency takes ONE of three paths: (a) grant/make available, (b) written denial, or (c) written acknowledgement stating a reasonable approximate date. The acknowledgement path opens the 20-business-day checkpoint and the delay-notice/date-certain obligations (§ 89(3)(a)).
- `appeal.administrative.stage` — Mandatory internal FOIL appeal stage: initial determination -&gt; written appeal to head/CEO/governing body within 30 days -&gt; 10-business-day written appeal determination -&gt; immediate forwarding of appeal+determination to the Committee on Open Government. Exhaustion is a prerequisite to Article 78 (§ 89(4)(a)-(b)).
- `fee.actual_cost_calculation` — Composed actual-cost fee engine for non-standard reproduction: labor at the lowest-paid qualified employee&#x27;s hourly rate, only above a 2-hour preparation threshold; plus actual storage-device/media cost; plus outside-professional-service cost only when agency IT equipment is inadequate; excluding search time and administrative costs (§ 87(1)(c)).
- `special.trade_secret_exception_process` — STATE-AGENCY-ONLY pre-submission exception path (§ 89(5); &#x27;agency&#x27; there = state department/board/office and gubernatorially-appointed public corporations — NOT ordinary municipalities): submitter requests exception at submission; info maintained apart until 15 days after entitlement finally determined; on a later request, agency notifies submitter, who has 10 business days to submit a written statement; agency issues a written determination within 7 business days; a denial may be appealed within 7 business days and is determined within 10 business days; Article 78 review must be commenced within 15 days. Flag: largely inapplicable to municipal requests.
- `special.le_disciplinary_redaction` — Law-enforcement disciplinary records path (§ 86(6), § 89(2-b) mandatory and § 89(2-c) permissive redactions): before disclosure, a law-enforcement agency must redact enumerated items (officer medical history, home/personal contact info, SSNs, EAP/mental-health/substance-abuse use) and may redact technical-infraction records. Creates a record-type-specific redaction workflow and an employee-notification policy duty (§ 89 subd. relating to notification / § 87(6)).

---

## Ohio (OH) — 35 rules

### Intake

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `OH-0001` | structural | none | **Any person may request public records; upon such a request all responsive public records shall be promptly prepared and made available for inspection at all reasonable times during regular business hours.**<br><br>_“Upon request by any person and subject to division (B)(8) of this section, all public records responsive to the request shall be promptly prepared and made available for inspection to the requester at all reasonable times during regular business hours.”_ | Ohio Rev. Code § 149.43(B)(1) |
| `OH-0002` | structural | none | **A public office may not condition availability of records on the requester disclosing the intended use of the record; requiring intended-use disclosure constitutes a denial.**<br><br>_“Unless specifically required or authorized by state or federal law or in accordance with division (B) of this section, no public office or person responsible for public records may limit or condition the availability of public records by requiring disclosure of the requester&#x27;s identity or the intended use of the requested public record. Any requirement that the requester disclose the requester&#x27;s identity or the intended use of the requested public record constitutes a denial of the request.”_ | Ohio Rev. Code § 149.43(B)(4) |
| `OH-0003` | structural | none | **A public office may not condition availability of records on the requester disclosing the requester&#x27;s identity; requiring identity disclosure constitutes a denial.**<br><br>_“Unless specifically required or authorized by state or federal law or in accordance with division (B) of this section, no public office or person responsible for public records may limit or condition the availability of public records by requiring disclosure of the requester&#x27;s identity or the intended use of the requested public record. Any requirement that the requester disclose the requester&#x27;s identity or the intended use of the requested public record constitutes a denial of the request.”_ | Ohio Rev. Code § 149.43(B)(4) |
| `OH-0004` | structural | none | **Access is available to any person with no residency limitation; the statute extends the right to &#x27;any person&#x27; without an Ohio-residency qualifier.**<br><br>_“Upon request by any person and subject to division (B)(8) of this section, all public records responsive to the request shall be promptly prepared and made available for inspection to the requester at all reasonable times during regular business hours.”_ | Ohio Rev. Code § 149.43(B)(1) |
| `OH-0005` | structural | none | **A written request is not mandatory; a requester may make an oral request and the office must honor it.**<br><br>_“A public office or person responsible for public records may ask a requester to make the request in writing, may ask for the requester&#x27;s identity, and may inquire about the intended use of the information requested, but may do so only after disclosing to the requester that a written request is not mandatory, that the requester may decline to reveal the requester&#x27;s identity or the intended use, and when a written request or disclosure of the identity or intended use would benefit the requester by enhancing the ability of the public office or person responsible for public records to identify, locate, or deliver the public records sought by the requester.”_ | Ohio Rev. Code § 149.43(B)(5) |
| `OH-0006` | structural | none | **A public office may ask for a written request, identity, or intended use only after first disclosing to the requester that these are not mandatory and may be declined.**<br><br>_“A public office or person responsible for public records may ask a requester to make the request in writing, may ask for the requester&#x27;s identity, and may inquire about the intended use of the information requested, but may do so only after disclosing to the requester that a written request is not mandatory, that the requester may decline to reveal the requester&#x27;s identity or the intended use, and when a written request or disclosure of the identity or intended use would benefit the requester by enhancing the ability of the public office or person responsible for public records to identify, locate, or deliver the public records sought by the requester.”_ | Ohio Rev. Code § 149.43(B)(5) |

| `OH-0035` | structural | none | **A public office need not permit a person incarcerated pursuant to a criminal conviction to inspect or copy a record concerning a criminal investigation or prosecution unless the sentencing judge (or successor) finds the information is necessary to support what appears to be a justiciable claim of the person.**<br><br>_“unless the request to inspect or to obtain a copy of the record is for the purpose of acquiring information that is subject to release as a public record under this section and the judge who imposed the sentence or made the adjudication with respect to the person, or the judge&#x27;s successor in office, finds that the information sought in the public record is necessary to support what appears to be a justiciable claim of the person.”_ | Ohio Rev. Code § 149.43(B)(8) |

### Material negatives

- `response.acknowledgment_window` — Ohio sets NO fixed numeric acknowledgment or response deadline. The only processing timing standards are the soft &#x27;promptly&#x27; (inspection) and &#x27;within a reasonable period of time&#x27; (copies) in R.C. 149.43(B)(1), captured as soft-standard rules OH-0007 and OH-0008. No day-count for acknowledgment exists.
- `denials.written_explanation_deadline` — R.C. 149.43(B)(3) requires a written denial explanation for written requests but attaches NO numeric deadline to providing it. Absence of a found day-count is not proof none exists elsewhere.
- `clarification.revise_response_clock` — The opportunity-to-revise duty (R.C. 149.43(B)(2)) carries no statutory clock — no deadline by which the office must offer revision or the requester must respond.
- `enforcement.constructive_denial_daycount` — Ohio has no fixed &#x27;silence for N days = denial&#x27; trigger. A failure to respond within a reasonable time may be treated as a denial for mandamus/Court-of-Claims purposes, but the statute states no numeric constructive-denial day count.
- `fee.copy_rate_per_page` — No statewide statutory per-page copy rate. R.C. 149.43(B)(1) sets only an &#x27;at cost&#x27;/actual-cost ceiling (OH-0018); the city fills in the operative rate. (Older R.C. 149.43(B)(1) practice historically referenced actual cost; no fixed cents-per-page in statute.)
- `payment.deposit_percentage` — R.C. 149.43(B)(6) permits requiring advance payment of the full cost but sets no partial-deposit percentage or threshold above which prepayment may be demanded.
- `custody.wrong_department_forwarding` — No explicit statutory duty to forward a misdirected request to the correct office/custodian. The duty is stated as resting on the office or &#x27;person responsible for public records&#x27; (OH-0013); routing/forwarding is not statutorily prescribed.
- `intake.request_tracking_log` — No statutory requirement to log/track requests or assign tracking numbers for an individual request under R.C. 149.43. (A public-records policy is required at the office level but tracking of individual requests is not mandated.)

### Structural branches

- `intake.identity_purpose_inquiry_precondition` — Before asking a requester for a written request, identity, or intended use, the engine must present a disclosure that all three are optional and may be declined (R.C. 149.43(B)(5)).
- `clarification.opportunity_to_revise` — On an ambiguous/overly-broad denial, the engine must branch into a revise-opportunity state that informs the requester how records are maintained and accessed (R.C. 149.43(B)(2)).
- `redaction.notice_of_redaction` — When exempt content is redacted, the engine must attach a redaction notice to the requester or render the redaction plainly visible (R.C. 149.43(B)(9)).
- `delivery.commercial_use_certification_exception` — A written non-commercial-use certification is a branch that lifts the ten-per-month delivery cap (R.C. 149.43(B)(7)).
- `enforcement.pre_suit_cure_period` — Service of a complaint opens a 3-business-day cure state before the requester may proceed to mandamus or the Court of Claims (R.C. 149.43(C)(1)).
- `appeal.administrative.court_of_claims_path` — Ohio-specific expedited review path: Court of Claims complaint -&gt; mandatory mediation -&gt; office response -&gt; special master report -&gt; objections, distinct from ordinary court action (R.C. 2743.75).
- `appeal.administrative.mediation_referral` — A mandatory mediation stage the special master must open immediately upon assignment, before any adjudication (R.C. 2743.75(E)).
- `appeal.judicial.mandamus_action` — The alternative court path — a mandamus action to compel compliance — that a requester may elect instead of the Court of Claims route (R.C. 149.43(C)(1)).
- `classification.incarcerated_requester_screening` — A status+record-type gate: incarcerated-person requests for criminal investigation/prosecution records route to a sentencing-judge justiciable-claim finding before release (R.C. 149.43(B)(8)).

|---|---|---|---|---|

Designate the days of the week when records are available for inspection, copying or mechanical reproduction; 2. Set forth the name, mailing address, and telephone number of the individual in charge of the records; and 3. Stat. tit. 51, § 24A.6(B) |

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `OK-0028` | structural | none | **A person denied access to records may bring a civil suit for declaratory or injunctive relief, or both, limited to records requested and denied prior to filing the suit.**<br><br>_“Any person denied access to records of a public body or public official: 1. May bring a civil suit for declarative or injunctive relief, or both, but such civil suit shall be limited to records requested and denied prior to filing of the civil suit; and 2. If successful, shall be entitled to reasonable attorney fees.”_ | Okla. Stat. tit. 51, § 24A.17(B) |

### Material negatives

- `deadline.initial_response_window` — Oklahoma sets NO fixed numeric response or determination deadline. The only timing standard is the soft &#x27;prompt, reasonable access&#x27; of §24A.5(6). Absence of a found rule is not proof none exists, but the Act contains no day-count.
- `intake.acknowledgment_window` — No statutory duty to acknowledge a request or issue an estimated completion date. The Act specifies only prompt, reasonable access.
- `denial.deemed_denial_on_nonresponse` — No statutory &#x27;silence = denial&#x27; trigger. §24A.17(B) provides a civil remedy for records &#x27;requested and denied,&#x27; but the Act fixes no clock after which non-response is deemed a denial (Oklahoma case law has treated unreasonable delay as constructive denial; not codified — flagged for a later case-law pass).
- `appeal.administrative.review_right` — No administrative or Attorney-General records-review/appeal process exists (contrast Texas&#x27;s AG-decision regime). Enforcement is judicial only (§24A.17(B)).
- `clarification.request_permitted` — No statutory clarification/narrowing procedure, clarification clock, or reasonably-describes-identifiable threshold is stated in the Act.
- `preservation.hold_pending_request` — No statutory preservation/litigation-hold triggered by a pending request. §24A.8(C) and §24A.18 expressly disclaim any additional recordkeeping requirement; retention is governed by separate records-management law (out of scope).
- `production.format_medium` — No general &#x27;produce in the requester&#x27;s chosen electronic format&#x27; mandate. The Act addresses electronic format only for narrow record types (news-media electronic data and DPS computerized copies), both capped at direct cost — not a general format-choice right.
- `payment.advance_deposit_before_production` — The Act contains no provision authorizing or governing advance deposits or pre-payment before production; only the fee caps of §24A.5(4) are stated.
- `deadline.determination_extension` — No statutory extension mechanism or unusual-circumstances tolling; consistent with the absence of any fixed response clock.
- `intake.written_request_required` — The Act does not require a written request or any particular form; §24A.2 bars imposing procedures beyond those the Act specifies, and none is specified for intake form or channel.

### Structural branches

- `intake.limited_hours_notice` — OK-0025 (§24A.6(A)): distinct intake/availability path for public bodies open fewer than 30 hours/week — posted notice, designated in-charge individual, and a minimum-two-days-per-week access floor.
- `intake.appointment_by_agreement` — OK-0026 (§24A.6(B)): mutual-agreement appointment path for access outside the designated limited-hours days.
- `fee.search_fee_conditional` — OK-0019 (§24A.5(4)): fee-basis fork — a search fee is unlockable ONLY when the request is solely commercial-purpose or would clearly cause excessive disruption; otherwise search cost is non-recoverable.
- `classification.commercial_purpose_news_exclusion` — OK-0020 (§24A.5(4)): classification branch excluding news-media/news-purpose use from &#x27;commercial purpose&#x27;, diverting it away from the search-fee path and into a direct-cost-only electronic-data rate.
- `handling.no_queue_blocking` — OK-0012 (§24A.5(6)): queue-management branch prohibiting a short current request from being blocked behind a substantially longer prior request.
- `production.website_redirect` — OK-0013 (§24A.5(6)): Internet-availability path that satisfies the prompt-access duty.
- `denial.law_enforcement_discretion` — OK-0027 (§24A.8(B)): record-specific discretionary-denial path for law-enforcement records not on the §24A.8(A) mandatory-disclosure list, subject to a court public-interest balancing test (human-legal-review flagged).

## Pennsylvania (PA) — 54 rules

### Eligibility

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PA-0001` | structural | none | **Only a person who is a legal resident of the United States (or an agency) may be a &#x27;requester&#x27; entitled to make a Right-to-Know request; US legal residency is a gate on standing.**<br><br>_“&quot;Requester.&quot; A person that is a legal resident of the United States and requests a record pursuant to this act. The term includes an agency.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.102 |
| `PA-0002` | structural | none | **A written request need not state the requester&#x27;s reason for requesting or intended use of the records, unless another law requires it.**<br><br>_“A written request need not include any explanation of the requester&#x27;s reason for requesting or intended use of the records unless otherwise required by law.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.703 |

### Intake

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PA-0003` | structural | none | **A written request may be submitted in person, by mail, by e-mail, by facsimile or, to the extent provided by agency rules, by any other electronic means.**<br><br>_“A written request for access to records may be submitted in person, by mail, by e-mail, by facsimile or, to the extent provided by agency rules, by any other electronic means.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.703 |
| `PA-0006` | structural | none | **An agency may fulfill verbal, written or anonymous requests, but a requester must submit a written request before pursuing the relief and remedies provided by the act.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“Agencies may fulfill verbal, written or anonymous requests; however, a written request is required to pursue the relief and remedies provided under the act.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.702 |

### Custody/Routing

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PA-0004` | structural | none | **A written request must be addressed to the designated open-records officer, and agency employees must be directed to forward requests for records to that officer.**<br><br>_“A written request must be addressed to the open-records officer designated pursuant to section 502. Employees of an agency shall be directed to forward requests for records to the open-records officer.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.703 |
| `PA-0018` | structural | none | **An agency shall designate an official or employee to act as the open-records officer.**<br><br>_“An agency shall designate an official or employee to act as the open-records officer.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.502(a) |
| `PA-0019` | structural | none | **Upon receiving a request, the open-records officer shall note the date received, compute the day the five-business-day response period expires, and maintain the request and related documents/response as an agency file.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“Upon receiving a request, the open-records officer shall note the date of receipt, compute the day on which the five-day period will expire, and maintain an electronic or paper copy of the request, including all documents submitted with the request.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.502(b) |
| `PA-0020` | structural | none | **A public record in the possession of a contractor or other person performing a governmental function on behalf of an agency is considered a record of the agency; the request is made to the agency&#x27;s open-records officer, who assesses applicable duplication fees.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“A public record that is not in the possession of an agency but is in the possession of a party with whom the agency has contracted to perform a governmental function on behalf of the agency, and which directly relates to the governmental function and is not exempt under this act, shall be considered a public record of the agency for purposes of this act.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.506(d) |

|---|---|---|---|---|
