### Classification

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PA-0007` | structural | none | **A record in the possession of a Commonwealth or local agency is presumed to be a public record, unless exempt under section 708, protected by privilege, or exempt under other Federal or State law or regulation or judicial order/decree.**<br><br>_“A record in the possession of a Commonwealth agency or local agency shall be presumed to be a public record.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.305 |
| `PA-0008` | structural | none | **An agency may exercise discretion to make an otherwise-exempt record accessible if disclosure is not prohibited by law/court order, the record is not privileged, and the agency head determines the public interest favoring access outweighs any interest favoring restriction.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“An agency may exercise its discretion to make any otherwise exempt record accessible for inspection and copying if the disclosure is not prohibited by law or court order, the record is not protected by privilege, and the agency head determines the public interest favoring access outweighs any interest favoring restriction.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.506(c) |

### Production

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PA-0009` | structural | none | **Unless otherwise provided by law, a public record, legislative record or financial record shall be accessible for inspection and duplication in accordance with the act.**<br><br>_“Unless otherwise provided by law, a public record, legislative record or financial record shall be accessible for inspection and duplication in accordance with this act.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.701 |
| `PA-0010` | structural | none | **A record shall be provided in the medium requested if it exists in that medium; otherwise it shall be provided in the medium in which it exists.**<br><br>_“A record being provided to a requester shall be provided in the medium requested if it exists in that medium; otherwise, it shall be provided in the medium in which it exists.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.701 |
| `PA-0012` | structural | none | **An agency is not required to create a record that does not currently exist, or to compile, maintain, format or organize a record in a manner in which the agency does not currently do so.**<br><br>_“When responding to a request for access, an agency shall not be required to create a record which does not currently exist or to compile, maintain, format or organize a record in a manner in which the agency does not currently compile, maintain, format or organize the record.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.705 |
| `PA-0013` | structural | none | **An agency may satisfy a request by notifying the requester that the record is available through publicly accessible electronic means, or may make records available through any publicly accessible electronic means.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“An agency may respond to a request by notifying the requester that the record is available through publicly accessible electronic means.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.704 |
| `PA-0014` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · requester: within 30 days of notice to request paper conversion; agency: within 5 days of that conversion request | **A requester unable to access an electronically-available record may, within 30 days, submit a written request to have the record converted to paper, and the agency shall provide access within five days of that request.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“If the requester is unable to access the record electronically, the requester may, within 30 days following receipt of the agency&#x27;s notice, submit a written request to the agency to have the record converted to paper. The agency shall provide access to the record in printed form within five days of the receipt of the written request for conversion to paper.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.704(b) |
| `PA-0015` | structural | none | **If an agency&#x27;s response grants access, the agency shall, upon request, provide a certified copy of the record if the requester pays the applicable fees under section 1307.**<br><br>_“If an agency&#x27;s response grants a request for access, the agency shall, upon request, provide the requester with a certified copy of the record if the requester pays the applicable fees under section 1307.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.904 |
| `PA-0016` | parameter · fixed | none | **An agency may dispose of copies that a requester has not retrieved after 60 days following notification that the record is available, and may retain fees already paid.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“An agency may dispose of a record that has not been retrieved by a requester after 60 days following notification that the record is available.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.905 |
| `PA-0030` | structural | none | **An agency may deny access where a timely response is impossible due to fire, flood or other disaster, or where the record is of such rarity/fragility that access could cause physical damage; contents should be made accessible when possible.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“An agency may deny a requester access to a record if timely access is not possible due to fire, flood or other disaster or to documents which are of such rarity or age that additional physical damage or loss might reasonably occur.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.506(b) |

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PA-0011` | parameter · soft-standard | none | **Public, legislative or financial records shall be available for access during the regular business hours of an agency.**<br><br>_“Public records, legislative records or financial records shall be available for access during the regular business hours of an agency.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.701 |

| `PA-0017` | structural | none | **A local agency (which includes municipalities) shall provide public records in accordance with the act.**<br><br>_“A local agency shall provide public records in accordance with this act.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.302 |
| `PA-0036` | structural | none | **Prior to an adjudication becoming final, binding and nonappealable, a transcript of an administrative proceeding shall be provided to a requester by the agency stenographer or a court reporter, in accordance with agency procedure or an applicable contract.**<br><br>_“Prior to an adjudication becoming final, binding and nonappealable, a transcript of an administrative proceeding shall be provided to a requester by the agency stenographer or a court reporter, in accordance with agency procedure or an applicable contract.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.707(c)(1) |
| `PA-0037` | structural | none | **Following an adjudication becoming final, binding and nonappealable, a transcript of an administrative proceeding shall be provided to a requester in accordance with the duplication rates established in section 1307(b).**<br><br>_“Following an adjudication becoming final, binding and nonappealable, a transcript of an administrative proceeding shall be provided to a requester in accordance with the duplication rates established in section 1307(b).”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.707(c)(2) |

### Deadlines

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PA-0021` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 5 business-days from date written request is received by the open-records officer | **The agency&#x27;s time to respond to a written request shall not exceed five business days from the date the written request is received by the open-records officer.**<br><br>_“The time for response shall not exceed five business days from the date the written request is received by the open-records officer for an agency.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.901 |
| `PA-0022` | structural | sets-deadline · undefined-soft: &quot;as promptly as possible under the circumstances existing at the time of the request&quot; from request receipt | **Upon receipt of a written request, an agency shall make a good faith effort to determine whether the requested item is a public/legislative/financial record and whether the agency has possession, custody or control of it, and respond as promptly as possible under the circumstances.**<br><br>_“Upon receipt of a written request for access to a record, an agency shall make a good faith effort to determine if the record requested is a public record, legislative record or financial record and whether the agency has possession, custody or control of the identified record, and to respond as promptly as possible under the circumstances existing at the time of the request.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.901 |
| `PA-0023` | structural | none | **The time for response may be extended if the open-records officer determines one of seven enumerated factors applies: (1) redaction under § 706; (2) retrieval from a remote location; (3) bona fide, specified staffing limitations; (4) legal review needed to determine access; (5) requester&#x27;s noncompliance with agency policies; (6) requester&#x27;s refusal to pay authorized fees; or (7) the extent or nature of the request precludes a timely response.**<br><br>_“(1) the request for access requires redaction of a record in accordance with section 706; (2) the request for access requires the retrieval of a record stored in a remote location; (3) a timely response to the request for access cannot be accomplished due to bona fide and specified staffing limitations; (4) a legal review is necessary to determine whether the record is a record subject to access under this act; (5) the requester has not complied with the agency&#x27;s policies regarding access to records; (6) the requester refuses to pay applicable fees authorized by this act; or (7) the extent or nature of the request precludes a response within the required time period.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.902(a) |
| `PA-0025` | parameter · ceiling | terminal · ceiling: 30 calendar-days beyond the initial 5 business days; deemed denied on excess absent written agreement | **If the expected response date is more than 30 days following the five business days allowed under § 901, the request is deemed denied unless the requester has agreed in writing to the extension.**<br><br>_“If the date that a response is expected to be provided is in excess of 30 days, following the five business days allowed for in section 901, the request for access shall be deemed denied unless the requester has agreed in writing to an extension to the date specified in the notice.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.902(b)(2) |

### Communications

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PA-0024` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 5 business-days from receipt of the request (to send extension notice) | **If an extension factor applies, the open-records officer shall send the requester written notice within five business days of receipt, stating that the request is under review, the reason, a reasonable expected response date, and an estimate of applicable fees.**<br><br>_“Upon a determination that one of the factors listed in subsection (a) applies, the open-records officer shall send written notice to the requester within five business days of receipt of the request for access under subsection (a). The notice shall include a statement notifying the requester that the request for access is being reviewed, the reason for the review, a reasonable date that a response is expected to be provided and an estimate of applicable fees owed when the record becomes available.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.902(b)(1)-(2) |
| `PA-0034` | structural | none | **If, in response to a request, an agency produces a record that is not a public, legislative or financial record, the agency shall notify any third party that provided the record, the person who is the subject of the record, and the requester.**<br><br>_“If, in response to a request, an agency produces a record that is not a public record, legislative record or financial record, the agency shall notify any third party that provided the record to the agency, the person that is the subject of the record and the requester.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.707(a) |
| `PA-0035` | structural | sets-deadline · notify third party within 5 business-days of request; third party has 5 business-days to respond; agency decides within 10 business-days of the notice | **Where a third party provided a record with a signed written statement that it contains a trade secret or confidential proprietary information, the agency shall notify the third party within five business days of the request; the third party has five business days from receipt of notification to provide input; and the agency shall deny or release the record within ten business days of providing notice, and notify the third party of the decision.**<br><br>_“An agency shall notify a third party of a request for a record if the third party provided the record and included a written statement signed by a representative of the third party that the record contains a trade secret or confidential proprietary information. Notification shall be provided within five business days of receipt of the request for the record. The third party shall have five business days from receipt of notification from the agency to provide input on the release of the record. The agency shall deny the request for the record or release the record within ten business days of the provision of notice to the third party and shall notify the third party of the decision.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.707(b) |

### Denials

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PA-0026` | structural | terminal · terminal: deemed denied on the day following the date specified in the extension notice | **If the requester agrees to the extension, the request is deemed denied on the day following the date specified in the notice if the agency has not provided a response by that date.**<br><br>_“If the requester agrees to the extension, the request shall be deemed denied on the day following the date specified in the notice if the agency has not provided a response by that date.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.902(b)(3) |
| `PA-0027` | structural | terminal · terminal: deemed denied when no response is sent within 5 business days of receipt by the open-records officer | **If the agency fails to send its response within five business days of receiving the written request, the request is deemed denied.**<br><br>_“If the agency fails to send the response within five business days of receipt of the written request for access, the written request for access shall be deemed denied.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.901 |
| `PA-0028` | structural | none | **A denial (in whole or in part) must be in writing and include: (1) a description of the record requested; (2) the specific reasons for denial including a citation of supporting legal authority; (3) the typed/printed name, title, business address, business telephone number and signature of the open-records officer; (4) the date of the response; and (5) the procedure to appeal the denial.**<br><br>_“If an agency&#x27;s response is a denial of a written request for access, whether in whole or in part, the denial shall be issued in writing and shall include: (1) A description of the record requested. (2) The specific reasons for the denial, including a citation of supporting legal authority. (3) The typed or printed name, title, business address, business telephone number and signature of the open-records officer on whose authority the denial is issued. (4) Date of the response. (5) The procedure to appeal the denial of access under this act.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.903 |
| `PA-0029` | structural | none | **An agency may deny a requester access to a record if the requester has made repeated requests for that same record and the repeated requests have placed an unreasonable burden on the agency.**<br><br>_“An agency may deny a requester access to a record if the requester has made repeated requests for that same record and the repeated requests have placed an unreasonable burden on the agency.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.506(a) |

### Redaction

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PA-0031` | structural | none | **If a record contains information subject to access and information not subject to access, the agency&#x27;s response shall grant access to the accessible information and deny access to the non-accessible information.**<br><br>_“If an agency determines that a public record, legislative record or financial record contains information which is subject to access as well as information which is not subject to access, the agency&#x27;s response shall grant access to the information which is subject to access and deny access to the information which is not subject to access.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.706 |
| `PA-0032` | structural | none | **Where non-accessible information cannot be separated, the agency shall redact the non-accessible information and grant access to the remainder; the agency may not deny access to the record if the non-accessible information can be redacted.**<br><br>_“The agency may not deny access to the record if the information which is not subject to access is able to be redacted.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.706 |
| `PA-0033` | structural | none | **Information that an agency redacts under § 706 shall be deemed a denial under Chapter 9, and is therefore appealable.**<br><br>_“Information which an agency redacts in accordance with this subsection shall be deemed a denial under Chapter 9.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.706 |

| `PA-0039` | parameter · ceiling | none | **Duplication fees for Commonwealth and local agencies (absent an agency-specific fee schedule) are established by the Office of Open Records and must be reasonable and based on prevailing fees for comparable duplication services provided by local business entities.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“Fees for duplication by a Commonwealth agency or a local agency shall be established by the Office of Open Records. Fees shall be reasonable and based on prevailing fees for comparable duplication services provided by local business entities.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.1307(b) |
| `PA-0040` | parameter · ceiling | none | **Under the OOR Official RTKL Fee Schedule, an agency may charge up to $0.25 per copy for black-and-white photocopies (8.5x11 or 8.5x14), reduced to $0.20 per copy beyond the first 1,000 copies.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“Up to $0.25 per copy for black and white copies (a &#x27;copy&#x27; is one side of an 8.5x11 or 8.5x14 page); up to $0.20 per copy beyond the first 1,000 copies.”_ | OOR Official RTKL Fee Schedule (adopted Dec. 30, 2024), issued under 65 P.S. § 67.1307(b) |
| `PA-0041` | parameter · soft-standard | none | **An agency may impose reasonable fees for official certification of copies where the certification is at the requester&#x27;s behest and for the purpose of legally verifying the public record.**<br><br>_“An agency may impose reasonable fees for official certification of copies if the certification is at the behest of the requester and for the purpose of legally verifying the public record.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.1307(c) |
| `PA-0042` | parameter · fixed | none | **No fee may be imposed for an agency&#x27;s review of a record to determine whether it is a public, legislative or financial record subject to access.**<br><br>_“No fee may be imposed for an agency&#x27;s review of a record to determine whether the record is a public record, legislative record or financial record subject to access.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.1307(g) |
| `PA-0043` | structural | none | **An agency may waive duplication fees, including when the requester duplicates the record or when the agency deems it in the public interest to do so.**<br><br>_“An agency may waive the fees for duplication of a record, including, but not limited to, when: (1) the requester duplicates the record; or (2) the agency deems it is in the public interest to do so.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.1307(f) |
| `PA-0044` | parameter · ceiling | none | **If a record is maintained only electronically or in nonpaper media, duplication fees are limited to the lesser of the paper-duplication fee or the original-media fee under § 1307(b), unless the requester specifically requests the more expensive medium.**<br><br>_“If a record is only maintained electronically or in other nonpaper media, duplication fees shall be limited to the lesser of the fee for duplication on paper or the fee for duplication in the original media as provided by subsection (b) unless the requester specifically requests for the record to be duplicated in the more expensive medium.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.1307(d) |
| `PA-0045` | parameter · soft-standard | none | **If an agency offers enhanced electronic access to records in addition to the inspection-and-duplication access required by the act, it may establish user fees specifically for the provision of that enhanced electronic access.**<br><br>_“If an agency offers enhanced electronic access to records in addition to making the records accessible for inspection and duplication by a requester as required by this act, the agency may establish user fees specifically for the provision of the enhanced electronic access.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.1307(e) |

### Appeals

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PA-0047` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 15 business-days from mailing date of the agency&#x27;s response, or from a deemed denial | **If a written request is denied or deemed denied, the requester may file an appeal with the Office of Open Records (or the designated judicial/legislative/other appeals officer) within 15 business days of the mailing date of the agency&#x27;s response or of a deemed denial.**<br><br>_“If a written request for access to a record is denied or deemed denied, the requester may file an appeal with the Office of Open Records or judicial, legislative or other appeals officer designated under section 503(d) within 15 business days of the mailing date of the agency&#x27;s response or within 15 business days of a deemed denial.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.1101(a) |
| `PA-0048` | structural | none | **The appeal shall state the grounds upon which the requester asserts that the record is a public record and shall address any grounds the agency stated for delaying or denying the request.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“The appeal shall state the grounds upon which the requester asserts that the record is a public record, legislative record or financial record and shall address any grounds the agency stated for delaying or denying the request.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.1101(a)(1) |
| `PA-0049` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 30 calendar-days from receipt of the appeal | **Unless the requester agrees otherwise, the appeals officer shall make a final determination, mailed to the requester and the agency, within 30 days of receipt of the appeal.**<br><br>_“Unless the requester agrees otherwise, the appeals officer shall make a final determination which shall be mailed to the requester and the agency within 30 days of receipt of the appeal filed under subsection (a).”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.1101(b) |
| `PA-0050` | structural | terminal · terminal: appeal deemed denied if no final determination issued within 30 days of receipt | **If the appeals officer fails to issue a final determination within 30 days, the appeal is deemed denied.**<br><br>_“If the appeals officer fails to issue a final determination within 30 days, the appeal is deemed denied.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.1101(b) |
| `PA-0051` | structural | none | **The appeals officer shall set a schedule for the requester and open-records officer to submit documents, review all information filed relating to the request, may hold a hearing at the officer&#x27;s discretion, and shall issue a final determination on behalf of the Office of Open Records or other agency.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“The appeals officer shall (1) set a schedule for the requester and the open-records officer to submit documents in support of their positions; (2) review all information filed relating to the request; and (4) issue a final determination on behalf of the Office of Open Records or other agency.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.1102(a) |
| `PA-0052` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 30 calendar-days from mailing of the final determination, or from the deemed-denied date | **Within 30 days of the mailing date of the appeals officer&#x27;s final determination relating to a Commonwealth, legislative or judicial agency (or the date the request is deemed denied), a requester or the agency may file a petition for review with the Commonwealth Court.**<br><br>_“Within 30 days of the mailing date of the final determination of the appeals officer relating to a decision of a Commonwealth agency, a legislative agency or a judicial agency issued under section 1101(b) or the date a request for access is deemed denied, a requester or the agency may file a petition for review or other document as might be required by rule of court with the Commonwealth Court.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.1301 |
| `PA-0053` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 30 calendar-days from mailing of the final determination, or from the deemed-denied date | **Within 30 days of the mailing date of the appeals officer&#x27;s final determination relating to a local agency (or the date the request is deemed denied), a requester or the local agency may file a petition for review with the court of common pleas for the county where the local agency is located.**<br><br>_“Within 30 days of the mailing date of the final determination of the appeals officer relating to a decision of a local agency issued under section 1101(b) or of the date a request for access is deemed denied, a requester or local agency may file a petition for review or other document as required by rule of court with the court of common pleas for the county where the local agency is located.”_ | 65 P.S. § 67.1302 |
| `PA-0054` | structural | pauses · pauses release of records from filing of the petition until the court&#x27;s decision | **A petition for judicial review filed under § 1301 or § 1302 stays the release of documents until a court issues its decision.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“A petition for review filed under this section shall stay the release of documents until a decision is issued by the court.”_ | 65 P.S. §§ 67.1301, 67.1302 |

### Material negatives

- `intake.acknowledgment_window` — No separate acknowledgment deadline distinct from the substantive response. The five-business-day § 901 window is the only initial deadline; silence for five business days is a deemed denial, not a missed acknowledgment.
- `intake.no_mandatory_form` — The statute does not mandate a particular form; § 703 allows any written request by several channels, and the OOR provides an optional standard form. No explicit statutory prohibition on requiring a form was located — treat &#x27;form optional&#x27; as guidance-level, verify.
- `deadline.receipt_day_not_counted` — OOR guidance/case law states the day the request is received is not counted and the five-business-day period starts the next business day. This computation rule was not confirmed in the codified statute text this pass — flag for OOR-regulation / case-law verification.
- `clarification.clarification_clock` — No express statutory clarification-and-tolling clock. A vague request is handled via the § 902(a)(7) extension ground and the specificity standard in § 703, not a dedicated clarification pause with its own deadline.
- `fee.commercial_purpose_surcharge` — The RTKL does not impose a distinct commercial-purpose fee surcharge on ordinary duplication; fees are governed by the OOR schedule and actual-cost limits regardless of purpose. (Contrast states with explicit commercial-use fee tiers.)
- `fee.search_time_charge` — No search/retrieval labor fee is authorized for ordinary requests; § 1307(g) bars charging for access-determination review, and duplication fees are the primary permitted charge. Staff-time charges appear only in narrow contexts — verify against OOR guidance.
- `preservation.litigation_hold_on_request` — No explicit statutory record-preservation/litigation-hold triggered by the filing of a request or appeal was located in the RTKL text this pass; general retention is governed by separate records-management law (out of scope).

### Structural branches

- `appeal.administrative.appeals_officer_duties` — PA-specific administrative appeal stage before the Office of Open Records (Commonwealth/local agencies) or a designated judicial/legislative/other appeals officer — a mandatory review path with its own scheduling, document exchange, discretionary hearing, and 30-day final determination (§§ 1101-1102).
- `appeal.judicial.common_pleas_window` — Two-track judicial review fork the engine must express: local-agency decisions go to the court of common pleas of the county (§ 1302), while Commonwealth/legislative/judicial-agency decisions go to Commonwealth Court (§ 1301).
- `deadline.extension_grounds` — Extension branch: on any of seven enumerated § 902(a) factors the request moves into a review state requiring a written extension notice within 5 business days, an expected-response date, and a 30-day cap gated on written requester agreement.
- `production.trade_secret_notice_window` — Third-party trade-secret/confidential-proprietary sub-process (§ 707(b)): a chained notice-and-input workflow (5 business days to notify, 5 for third-party input, 10 to decide) that the request enters when a flagged third-party record is implicated.
- `redaction.redaction_is_denial` — Any redaction is legally reclassified as a partial denial under Chapter 9 (§ 706), so a redacted production must branch into the denial/appeal machinery, not close as a grant.
- `classification.discretionary_release_exempt` — Agency-head discretionary-release path (§ 506(c)): a balancing branch allowing release of otherwise-exempt records when public interest outweighs restriction — requires human legal judgment, not auto-configuration.
- `production.electronic_means_response` — Electronic-availability response fork (§ 704): agency may close a request by pointing to publicly accessible electronic means, which opens a requester right to demand paper conversion (30-day request / 5-day agency turnaround).

## Tennessee (TN) — 50 rules

### Intake

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TN-0001` | structural | none | **Access under the TPRA runs to any citizen of Tennessee; those in charge of records shall not refuse inspection to any citizen unless otherwise provided by state law.**<br><br>_“All state, county and municipal records shall, at all times during business hours, which for public hospitals shall be during the business hours of their administrative offices, be open for personal inspection by any citizen of this state, and those in charge of the records shall not refuse such right of inspection to any citizen, unless otherwise provided by state law.”_ | Tenn. Code Ann. § 10-7-503(a)(2)(A) |
| `TN-0002` | structural | none | **The TPRA does not distinguish requests based on the requester&#x27;s intended use; a requester need not state a purpose to access open records.**<br><br>_“The TPRA does not distinguish requests for inspection of records based on intended use, be it for research, personal, or commercial purposes. Likewise, this Schedule of Reasonable Charges does not make a distinction in the charges assessed based on the purpose of a record request.”_ | OORC Schedule of Reasonable Charges § I.F (implementing Tenn. Code Ann. § 8-4-604); Tenn. Code Ann. § 10-7-503 |
| `TN-0003` | structural | none | **A governmental entity may determine, in its public records policy, whether to respond to TPRA requests from persons who are not citizens of Tennessee.**<br><br>_“The TPRA authorizes a governmental entity to determine the following with respect to each entity&#x27;s public records policy: A. Whether to respond to TPRA requests by persons who are not citizens of Tennessee;”_ | OORC Model Public Records Policy, Policy Considerations § I.A |
| `TN-0004` | structural | none | **A governmental entity may require government-issued photo identification as a prerequisite to providing access to records.**<br><br>_“The TPRA authorizes a governmental entity to determine the following with respect to each entity&#x27;s public records policy: ... B. Whether to require government-issued photo identification as a prerequisite to providing access to records;”_ | OORC Model Public Records Policy, Policy Considerations § I.B; statutory basis Tenn. Code Ann. § 10-7-503(a)(7)(A)(vi) |
| `TN-0006` | structural | none | **Requests to view public records may be submitted in person, by telephone, fax, mail, or email (if the entity uses such means for official business), or via internet portal (if the entity maintains one for records requests).**<br><br>_“Requests to view public records may be submitted in person or by telephone, fax, mail, or email if the governmental entity uses such means of communication to transact official business, or via internet portal if the governmental entity maintains an internet portal that is used for accepting public records requests.”_ | Tenn. Code Ann. § 10-7-503(a)(7)(A)(i) |
| `TN-0007` | structural | none | **A governmental entity may require a request for COPIES of public records to be in writing, on a form complying with subsection (c), or on a form developed by the Office of Open Records Counsel.**<br><br>_“A governmental entity may require a request for copies of public records to be: In writing; On a form that complies with subsection (c); or On a form developed by the office of open records counsel.”_ | Tenn. Code Ann. § 10-7-503(a)(7)(A)(ii) |
| `TN-0046` | structural | none | **Every county and municipal governmental entity shall establish a written public records policy, adopted by its governing authority, that is not more burdensome than state law and includes the request process and any required form, the response process (including redaction), a statement of fees and billing/payment procedures, and the PRRC&#x27;s name/title and contact information.**<br><br>_“No later than July 1, 2018, every county and municipal governmental entity subject to this section shall establish a written public records policy properly adopted by the appropriate governing authority. The public records policy shall not impose requirements on those requesting records that are more burdensome than state law and shall include: The process for making requests to inspect public records or receive copies of public records and a copy of any required request form; The process for responding to requests, including redaction practices; A statement of any fees charged for copies of public records and the procedures for billing and payment; and The name or title and the contact information of the individual or individuals within such governmental entity designated as the public records request coordinator.”_ | Tenn. Code Ann. § 10-7-503(g)(1) |

### Deadlines

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TN-0014` | structural | sets-deadline · undefined-soft: &quot;promptly&quot; from request receipt | **The custodian or the custodian&#x27;s designee shall promptly make available for inspection any public record not specifically exempt from disclosure.**<br><br>_“The custodian of a public record or the custodian&#x27;s designee shall promptly make available for inspection any public record not specifically exempt from disclosure.”_ | Tenn. Code Ann. § 10-7-503(a)(2)(B) |
| `TN-0015` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · fixed: 7 business-days from custodian&#x27;s receipt of the request | **When it is not practicable for a record to be promptly available, the custodian shall within seven (7) business days take one of three actions: (i) make it available, (ii) deny in writing with basis, or (iii) furnish in writing the time reasonably necessary to produce it.**<br><br>_“In the event it is not practicable for the record to be promptly available for inspection, the custodian shall, within seven (7) business days: Make the information available to the requestor; Deny the request in writing or by completing a records request response form developed by the office of open records counsel. The response shall include the basis for the denial; or Furnish the requester in writing, or by completing a records request response form developed by the office of open records counsel, the time reasonably necessary to produce the record or information.”_ | Tenn. Code Ann. § 10-7-503(a)(2)(B)(i)-(iii) |

### Communications

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TN-0016` | structural | sets-deadline · undefined-soft: &quot;time reasonably necessary to produce&quot; stated by custodian, from request receipt | **As the third permitted response, the custodian furnishes the requester, in writing or on the OORC response form, the time reasonably necessary to produce the record or information.**<br><br>_“Furnish the requester in writing, or by completing a records request response form developed by the office of open records counsel, the time reasonably necessary to produce the record or information.”_ | Tenn. Code Ann. § 10-7-503(a)(2)(B)(iii) |

### Denials

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TN-0017` | structural | terminal · terminal: silence past 7 business-days = denial, triggering § 10-7-505 action | **Failure to respond as described in subdivision (a)(2) constitutes a denial, and the requester has the right to bring an action under § 10-7-505.**<br><br>_“Failure to respond to the request as described in subdivision (a)(2) shall constitute a denial and the person making the request shall have the right to bring an action as provided in § 10-7-505.”_ | Tenn. Code Ann. § 10-7-503(a)(2)(C) |
| `TN-0026` | structural | none | **A denial must be in writing (or on the OORC-developed records request response form) and shall include the basis for the denial.**<br><br>_“Deny the request in writing or by completing a records request response form developed by the office of open records counsel. The response shall include the basis for the denial.”_ | Tenn. Code Ann. § 10-7-503(a)(2)(B)(ii) |
| `TN-0027` | structural | none | **A request may be denied where an exemption makes the record not subject to disclosure under the TPRA; the applicable exemption must be provided in the written denial.**<br><br>_“An exemption makes the record not subject to disclosure under the TPRA. (Provide the exemption in written denial)”_ | OORC Model Public Records Policy § III.A.2.b.iii |
| `TN-0028` | structural | none | **A request may be denied where the governmental entity is not the custodian of the requested records, or the records do not exist.**<br><br>_“The Governmental Entity is not the custodian of the requested records. ... The records do not exist.”_ | OORC Model Public Records Policy §§ III.A.2.b.iv, III.A.2.b.v |

### Fees

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TN-0018` | parameter · fixed | none | **Records custodians may not charge for inspection of public records except as provided by law.**<br><br>_“Records custodians may not charge for inspection of public records except as provided by law.”_ | OORC Schedule of Reasonable Charges § I.A; Tenn. Code Ann. § 10-7-503(a)(7)(A)(i) |
| `TN-0029` | parameter · ceiling | none | **For each standard 8.5x11 or 8.5x14 black-and-white copy produced, a records custodian may assess a per-page charge of up to 15 cents ($0.15).**<br><br>_“For each standard 8 1/2&quot; x 11&quot; or 8 1/2&quot; x 14&quot; copy produced, a records custodian may assess a per page charge of up to 15 cents ($0.15) for black and white copies ... If producing duplex (front and back) copies, a charge for two separate pages may be imposed for each single duplex copy.”_ | OORC Schedule of Reasonable Charges § II.A |
| `TN-0030` | parameter · ceiling | none | **For each standard 8.5x11 or 8.5x14 color copy produced, a records custodian may assess a per-page charge of up to 50 cents ($0.50).**<br><br>_“For each standard 8 1/2&quot; x 11&quot; or 8 1/2&quot; x 14&quot; copy produced, a records custodian may assess a per page charge of ... up to 50 cents ($0.50) for color copies.”_ | OORC Schedule of Reasonable Charges § II.A |
| `TN-0031` | parameter · soft-standard | none | **If actual costs are higher than the schedule amounts, or records are produced on a non-standard medium, the entity may develop its own charges but must document &#x27;actual cost&#x27; calculation and reasoning in an adopted policy; charges above $0.15 (B&amp;W) / $0.50 (color) require documented actual-cost analysis.**<br><br>_“If a governmental entity&#x27;s actual costs are higher than those reflected above ... the governmental entity may develop its own charges. The governmental entity must establish a schedule of charges documenting &quot;actual cost&quot; and state the calculation and reasoning for its charges in a properly adopted policy. ... Charges greater than 15 cents ($0.15) for black and white copies and 50 cents ($0.50) for color copies can be assessed or collected only when there is documented analysis of the fact that the higher charges represent the governmental entity&#x27;s actual cost of producing such material, unless there exists another basis in law for such charges.”_ | OORC Schedule of Reasonable Charges § II.C |
| `TN-0032` | parameter · floor | none | **The labor threshold is the first (1st) hour of labor reasonably necessary to produce requested material; a governmental entity may adopt a higher threshold, and labor may be charged only for time exceeding the threshold.**<br><br>_“&quot;Labor threshold&quot; is the first (1st) hour of labor reasonably necessary to produce requested material(s). A governmental entity may adopt a higher labor threshold than one (1) hour. A records custodian is only permitted to charge for labor exceeding the labor threshold established by the governmental entity.”_ | OORC Schedule of Reasonable Charges § IV.C |
| `TN-0033` | parameter · soft-standard | none | **Labor above the threshold is charged at the hourly wage of the employee(s) reasonably necessary, using the lowest practicable wage; the hourly wage is the employee&#x27;s base salary (excluding benefits), computed by dividing annual salary by required hours per year.**<br><br>_“&quot;Labor&quot; is the time (in hours) reasonably necessary to produce requested records, including the time spent locating, retrieving, reviewing, redacting, and reproducing records. ... &quot;Hourly wage of an employee&quot; is based upon the base salary of the employee and does not include benefits. If an employee is not paid on an hourly basis, the hourly wage shall be determined by dividing the employee&#x27;s annual salary by the required hours to be worked per year. ... In calculating labor charges, a records custodian should determine the total amount of labor for each employee and subtract the labor threshold from the labor of the highest paid employee(s).”_ | OORC Schedule of Reasonable Charges §§ IV.B, IV.D, IV.E |
| `TN-0034` | structural | none | **The records custodian shall provide the requestor an estimate of the reasonable costs to provide copies of the requested material before producing them.**<br><br>_“The records custodian shall provide a requestor an estimate of the reasonable costs to provide copies of the requested material.”_ | Tenn. Code Ann. § 10-7-503(a)(7)(B); OORC Schedule of Reasonable Charges § I.G; OORC Model Public Records Policy § VI.B |
| `TN-0036` | structural | none | **A records custodian may reduce or waive charges, in whole or in part, in accordance with the governmental entity&#x27;s public records policy.**<br><br>_“A records custodian may reduce or waive charges, in whole or in part, in accordance with the governmental entity&#x27;s public records policy.”_ | OORC Schedule of Reasonable Charges § I.D |
| `TN-0037` | parameter · fixed | none | **A custodian may charge actual out-of-pocket cost for flash drives/storage devices provided; if internal production is not practicable, may use an outside vendor and charge those actual costs; and may recover actual retrieval charges assessed by an archive or other entity holding the records.**<br><br>_“A records custodian may charge its actual out-of-pocket costs for flash drives or similar storage devices on which electronic copies are provided. ... If it is not practicable or feasible for the records custodian to produce copies internally, the records custodian may use an outside vendor and charge the costs to the requester. ... If a records custodian is assessed a charge to retrieve requested records from archives or any other entity having possession of requested records, the records custodian may recover from the requestor the costs assessed for retrieval.”_ | OORC Schedule of Reasonable Charges §§ III.B, III.E, III.F |
| `TN-0038` | parameter · floor | none | **A governmental entity&#x27;s policy shall specify the number of requests within a calendar month that triggers aggregation; that amount must be no lower than four (4) requests.**<br><br>_“A governmental entity&#x27;s public record policy shall indicate the number of requests within a calendar month that will trigger aggregation; that amount must be no lower than four (4) requests. This amount is the aggregation threshold.”_ | OORC Reasonable Charges for Frequent and Multiple Requests § I.D |
| `TN-0039` | structural | none | **Once a records custodian reaches the aggregation threshold, the custodian is no longer required to deduct the labor threshold or any other minimum charge per request that would ordinarily be waived.**<br><br>_“Once a records custodian reaches the aggregation threshold, the records custodian is no longer required to deduct the labor threshold set forth in the Schedule of Reasonable Charges or any other minimum charge per request that would ordinarily be waived.”_ | OORC Reasonable Charges for Frequent and Multiple Requests § II.A |
| `TN-0040b` | structural | none | **An entity may aggregate multiple requests by a single requestor, and may aggregate requests by multiple requestors if the coordinator determines they are acting in concert with each other or as agents of another person, entity, or organization.**<br><br>_“A governmental entity may aggregate multiple public record requests made by a single requestor. A governmental entity may also aggregate public record requests made by multiple requestors, if the public records request coordinator determines the requestors are acting in concert with each other or as the agents of another person, entity, or organization.”_ | OORC Reasonable Charges for Frequent and Multiple Requests § I.C |
| `TN-0049` | parameter · fixed | none | **Although standard copy/labor charges do not vary by purpose, other statutory provisions (e.g., § 10-7-506(c)) enumerate fees that may be assessed when specific documents are requested for a specific use.**<br><br>_“However, other statutory provisions, such as Tenn. Code Ann. § 10-7-506(c), enumerate fees that may be assessed when specific documents are requested for a specific use. Any distinctions made, or waiver of charges permitted, based upon the type of records requested should be expressly set forth and permitted in the adopted public records policy.”_ | OORC Schedule of Reasonable Charges § I.F, citing Tenn. Code Ann. § 10-7-506(c) |
| `TN-0050` | structural | none | **A records custodian may require a requestor to pay the custodian&#x27;s reasonable costs incurred in producing the requested material, assessed in the manner established by the Office of Open Records Counsel pursuant to § 8-4-604.**<br><br>_“A records custodian may require a requestor to pay the custodian&#x27;s reasonable costs incurred in producing the requested material and to assess the reasonable costs in the manner established by the office of open records counsel pursuant to § 8-4-604.”_ | Tenn. Code Ann. § 10-7-503(a)(7)(B) |

### Production

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TN-0019` | structural | none | **When assessing fees for copies, records custodians shall utilize the most economical and efficient method of producing the requested records.**<br><br>_“When assessing a fee for items covered under this section, records custodians shall utilize the most economical and efficient method of producing the requested records.”_ | OORC Schedule of Reasonable Charges § III.A |
| `TN-0020` | structural | sets-deadline · undefined-soft: production schedule &quot;as expeditiously as practicable&quot; from response form notice | **If production should be segmented because of a large volume of records, the custodian uses the response form to notify the requestor that production will be in segments and a production schedule will be provided as expeditiously as practicable.**<br><br>_“If a records custodian reasonably determines production of records should be segmented because the records request is for a large volume of records, or additional time is necessary to prepare the records for access, the records custodian shall use the Public Records Request Response Form to notify the requestor that production of the records will be in segments and that a records production schedule will be provided as expeditiously as practicable.”_ | OORC Model Public Records Policy § III.B.4 |
| `TN-0021` | structural | sets-deadline · undefined-soft: produce omitted records &quot;as quickly as practicable&quot; from discovery | **If the custodian discovers responsive records were omitted, the custodian should contact the requestor about the omission and produce the records as quickly as practicable.**<br><br>_“If a records custodian discovers records responsive to a records request were omitted, the records custodian should contact the requestor concerning the omission and produce the records as quickly as practicable.”_ | OORC Model Public Records Policy § III.B.5 |
| `TN-0022` | structural | none | **If a requestor chooses not to personally retrieve copies and the actual delivery cost plus other permitted charges are paid or waived, the custodian must deliver the copies via USPS First-Class Mail; other means (including electronic) are discretionary and may carry delivery costs.**<br><br>_“If a requestor chooses not to personally retrieve records and the actual cost of delivering the copies, in addition to any other permitted charges, have been paid by the requestor or otherwise waived pursuant to the public records policy, then a records custodian is obligated to deliver the copies via USPS First-Class Mail. It is within the discretion of a records custodian to agree to deliver copies of records through other means, including electronically, and to assess the costs related to such delivery.”_ | OORC Schedule of Reasonable Charges § III.D |

Code Ann. § 10-7-503(a)(4) |
| `TN-0024` | structural | none | **The redaction of confidential information shall not constitute the creation of a new record.**<br><br>_“The redaction of confidential information shall not constitute the creation of a new record.”_ | Tenn. Code Ann. § 10-7-503(a)(4) |
| `TN-0025` | structural | none | **Whenever a redacted record is provided, the custodian should provide the requestor the basis for redaction, stated generally so as not to disclose the confidential information.**<br><br>_“Whenever a redacted record is provided, a records custodian should provide the requestor with the basis for redaction. The basis given for redaction shall be general in nature and not disclose confidential information.”_ | OORC Model Public Records Policy § III.C.2 |

### Appeals

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TN-0041` | structural | none | **When aggregating, the custodian must inform the requestor(s) of the determination to aggregate and of the right to appeal that decision to the Office of Open Records Counsel; disputes regarding aggregation shall be brought to the OORC.**<br><br>_“When the aggregation threshold is met, a records custodian choosing to aggregate requests must inform the requestor(s) of the determination to aggregate and of the right of the requestor(s) to appeal the records custodian&#x27;s decision to aggregate to the OORC. ... Disputes regarding aggregation shall be brought to the OORC.”_ | OORC Reasonable Charges for Frequent and Multiple Requests §§ II.B, II.D |
| `TN-0042` | structural | none | **A Tennessee citizen whose request under § 10-7-503 has been denied in whole or in part may petition for access to the record and to obtain judicial review of the actions taken to deny access.**<br><br>_“Any citizen of Tennessee who shall request the right of personal inspection of any state, county or municipal record as provided in § 10-7-503, and whose request has been in whole or in part denied by the official and/or designee of the official or through any act or regulation of any official or designee of any official, shall be entitled to petition for access to any such record and to obtain judicial review of the actions taken to deny the access.”_ | Tenn. Code Ann. § 10-7-505(a)-(b) |
| `TN-0043` | structural | none | **The petition is filed in chancery or circuit court (or other equity court) of the county where the county/municipal records are situated; for state records, in Davidson County, the county of the records&#x27; situs, or the petitioner&#x27;s county of residence.**<br><br>_“Such petition shall be filed in the chancery court or circuit court for the county in which the county or municipal records sought are situated, or in any other court of that county having equity jurisdiction. In the case of records in the custody and control of any state department, agency or instrumentality, such petition shall be filed in the chancery court or circuit court of Davidson County; or in the chancery court or circuit court for the county in which the state records are situated ...; or in the chancery court or circuit court in the county of the petitioner&#x27;s residence ...”_ | Tenn. Code Ann. § 10-7-505(b) |
| `TN-0044` | structural | sets-deadline · undefined-soft: respondent to &quot;immediately appear and show cause&quot; upon court order | **Upon filing, at the petitioner&#x27;s request the court shall issue an order requiring the respondent to immediately appear and show cause why the petition should not be granted; no formal written response is required and ordinary response-filing periods do not apply, in the interest of expeditious hearings.**<br><br>_“Upon filing of the petition, the court shall, upon request of the petitioning party, issue an order requiring the defendant or respondent party or parties to immediately appear and show cause, if they have any, why the petition should not be granted. A formal written response to the petition shall not be required, and the generally applicable periods of filing such response shall not apply in the interest of expeditious hearings.”_ | Tenn. Code Ann. § 10-7-505(b) |
| `TN-0045` | structural | none | **The burden of proof for justification of nondisclosure is on the official/designee, who must show justification by a preponderance of the evidence.**<br><br>_“The burden of proof for justification of nondisclosure of records sought shall be upon the official and/or designee of the official of those records and the justification for the nondisclosure must be shown by a preponderance of the evidence.”_ | Tenn. Code Ann. § 10-7-505(c) |

### Special Records

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TN-0047` | structural | sets-deadline · fixed: 3 days from the date of inspection to notify the officer | **Whenever a law-enforcement officer&#x27;s personnel records are inspected, the custodian shall make a record of the inspection and, within three (3) days of the inspection, notify the officer that the inspection took place, the inspector&#x27;s name/address/telephone number, for whom the inspection was made, and the date.**<br><br>_“whenever the personnel records of a law enforcement officer are inspected as provided in subsection (a), the custodian shall make a record of such inspection and provide notice, within three (3) days from the date of the inspection, to the officer whose personnel records have been inspected: That such inspection has taken place; The name, address and telephone number of the person making such inspection; For whom the inspection was made; and The date of such inspection.”_ | Tenn. Code Ann. § 10-7-503(c) |
| `TN-0048` | structural | none | **A person inspecting law-enforcement personnel records must provide their name, address, business and home telephone numbers, driver license number, or other appropriate identification prior to inspecting the records.**<br><br>_“Any person making an inspection of such records shall provide such person&#x27;s name, address, business telephone number, home telephone number, driver license number or other appropriate identification prior to inspecting such records.”_ | Tenn. Code Ann. § 10-7-503(c) |

## Texas (TX) — 35 rules

### Eligibility

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TX-0001` | structural | none | **Any person may apply to the officer for public information and the officer shall promptly produce public information for inspection, duplication, or both.**<br><br>_“An officer for public information of a governmental body shall promptly produce public information for inspection, duplication, or both on application by any person to the officer.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.221(a) |
| `TX-0002` | structural | none | **The governmental body may not inquire into the purpose for which the requested information will be used.**<br><br>_“If a large amount of information has been requested, the governmental body may discuss with the requestor how the scope of a request might be narrowed, but the governmental body may not inquire into the purpose for which information will be used.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.222(b) |
| `TX-0003` | structural | none | **There is no residency requirement; the right to request runs to &#x27;any person&#x27; regardless of residence.**<br><br>_“An officer for public information of a governmental body shall promptly produce public information for inspection, duplication, or both on application by any person to the officer.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.221(a) |
| `TX-0004` | structural | none | **A governmental body is not required to accept or comply with a request from an incarcerated/confined individual or that individual&#x27;s agent (other than the individual&#x27;s attorney seeking disclosable information).**<br><br>_“A governmental body is not required to accept or comply with a request for information from: (1) an individual who is imprisoned or confined in a correctional facility; or (2) an agent of that individual, other than that individual&#x27;s attorney when the attorney is requesting information that is subject to disclosure under this chapter.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.028(a) |

### Intake

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TX-0005` | structural | none | **A request must be in writing and delivered by U.S. mail, e-mail, hand delivery, or another method the governmental body has approved (e.g., fax, submission through its website).**<br><br>_“A person may make a written request for public information under this chapter only by delivering the request by one of the following methods to the applicable officer for public information or a person designated by that officer: (1) United States mail; (2) electronic mail; (3) hand delivery; or (4) any other appropriate method approved by the governmental body, including: (A) facsimile transmission; and (B) electronic submission through the governmental body&#x27;s Internet website.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.234(a) |

### Custody/Routing

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TX-0006` | structural | none | **A governmental body may designate one mailing address and one e-mail address for requests; if it posts/prints them, it need not respond to a request unless received at a designated address, by hand delivery, or by an approved (a)(4) method.**<br><br>_“A governmental body may designate one mailing address and one electronic mail address for receiving written requests for public information. The governmental body shall provide the designated mailing address and electronic mailing address to any person on request.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.234(c), (d) |

### Inspection

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TX-0007` | parameter · floor | none | **Public information must be available to the public at a minimum during the governmental body&#x27;s normal business hours.**<br><br>_“Public information is available to the public at a minimum during the normal business hours of the governmental body.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.021 |

### Deadlines

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TX-0008` | structural | sets-deadline · undefined-soft: &quot;promptly ... as soon as possible under the circumstances, that is, within a reasonable time, without delay&quot; from request receipt | **The officer must produce public information promptly, i.e., as soon as possible under the circumstances, within a reasonable time, without delay.**<br><br>_“An officer for public information of a governmental body shall promptly produce public information for inspection, duplication, or both on application by any person to the officer. ... &#x27;promptly&#x27; means as soon as possible under the circumstances, that is, within a reasonable time, without delay.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.221(a), (b) |

### Communications

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TX-0009` | structural | sets-deadline · 10 business-days from request receipt (certification trigger) | **If information cannot be produced within 10 business days after receipt, the officer must certify that fact in writing to the requestor and set a date and hour, within a reasonable time, when it will be available.**<br><br>_“If public information cannot be produced for inspection or duplication within 10 business days after the date the information is requested by a member of the public, the officer for public information shall certify that fact in writing to the requestor and set a date and hour within a reasonable time when the information will be available for inspection or duplication.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.221(d) |
| `TX-0010` | structural | none | **If requested information is in active use or in storage and therefore unavailable when requested, the officer must certify this in writing and set a date and hour, within a reasonable time, when it will be available.**<br><br>_“If the requested information is unavailable at the time of the request to examine because it is in active use or in storage, the officer for public information shall certify this fact in writing to the requestor and set a date and hour within a reasonable time when the information will be available for inspection or duplication.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.221(c) |
| `TX-0019` | structural | sets-deadline · 10 business-days from receipt of the written request | **Within 10 business days of the request the body must give the requestor a written statement that it is withholding and has asked the AG, plus a copy (redacted if needed) of its AG communication.**<br><br>_“provide the requestor within a reasonable time but not later than the 10th business day after the date of receiving the requestor&#x27;s written request: (1) a written statement that the governmental body wishes to withhold the requested information and has asked for a decision from the attorney general about whether the information is within an exception to public disclosure; and (2) a copy of the governmental body&#x27;s written communication to the attorney general asking for the decision or, if the governmental body&#x27;s written communication to the attorney general discloses the requested information, a redacted copy of that written communication.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.301(d) |
| `TX-0021` | structural | sets-deadline · 15 business-days from receipt of the written request | **A body that submits written comments to the AG must send a copy of those comments to the requestor not later than the 15th business day after receiving the written request.**<br><br>_“A governmental body that submits written comments to the attorney general under Subsection (e)(1)(A) shall send a copy of those comments to the person who requested the information from the governmental body not later than the 15th business day after the date of receiving the written request.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.301(e-1) |

### Production

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TX-0011` | parameter · fixed | terminal · 60 days from date information made available (or from cost notice) | **A request is considered withdrawn if the requestor fails to inspect/duplicate the information within 60 days after it is made available, or fails to pay applicable charges within 60 days after being informed of the costs.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“A request is considered withdrawn if the requestor fails to inspect or duplicate the public information in the offices of the governmental body within 60 days after the date the information is made available, or fails to pay the applicable charges within 60 days after the date the requestor is informed of the costs.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.221(e) |

### Clarification

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TX-0012` | structural | none | **If a request is unclear the body may ask the requestor to clarify; if a large amount is requested the body may discuss narrowing the scope.**<br><br>_“If what information is requested is unclear to the governmental body, the governmental body may ask the requestor to clarify the request. If a large amount of information has been requested, the governmental body may discuss with the requestor how the scope of a request might be narrowed, but the governmental body may not inquire into the purpose for which information will be used.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.222(b) |
| `TX-0013` | structural | none | **A written clarification/discussion request (or a request for additional info) must include a statement of the consequences of the requestor&#x27;s failure to timely respond.**<br><br>_“A written request for clarification or discussion under Subsection (b) or a written request for additional information under Subsection (c) must include a statement as to the consequences of the failure by the requestor to timely respond to the request for clarification, discussion, or additional information.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.222(e) |
| `TX-0014` | parameter · fixed | terminal · 61 days from date clarification/discussion request sent | **If the body receives no written response by the 61st day after sending a clarification/discussion request (or additional-information request), the underlying request is considered withdrawn.**<br><br>_“If by the 61st day after the date a governmental body sends a written request for clarification or discussion under Subsection (b) or an officer for public information or agent sends a written request for additional information under Subsection (c) the governmental body, officer for public information, or agent, as applicable, does not receive a written response from the requestor, the underlying request for public information is considered to have been withdrawn by the requestor.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.222(d) |
| `TX-0015` | structural | tolls · tolls the 10 business-day AG-request clock (TX-0018) from clarification sent until requestor responds | **A governmental body&#x27;s good-faith request for clarification tolls the 10-business-day period for requesting an attorney general decision until the requestor responds.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“When a governmental body, acting in good faith, requests clarification or narrowing of an unclear or over-broad request for public information, the ten-day period to request an attorney general decision is measured from the date the request is clarified or narrowed.”_ | City of Dallas v. Abbott, 304 S.W.3d 380 (Tex. 2010) |

### Review

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TX-0016` | structural | none | **A body that receives a written request for information it wishes to withhold under a Subchapter C exception must ask the attorney general for a decision, unless there has been a previous determination.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“A governmental body that receives a written request for information that it wishes to withhold from public disclosure and that it considers to be within one of the exceptions under Subchapter C must ask for a decision from the attorney general about whether the information is within that exception if there has not been a previous determination about whether the information falls within one of the exceptions.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.301(a) |
| `TX-0017` | structural | none | **No new AG decision is required where there has already been a previous determination that the information falls within an exception (or is public).** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“... must ask for a decision from the attorney general about whether the information is within that exception if there has not been a previous determination about whether the information falls within one of the exceptions.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.301(a) |
| `TX-0018` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 10 business-days from receipt of the written request | **The body must ask the attorney general for a decision and state the applicable exceptions within a reasonable time but not later than the 10th business day after receiving the written request.**<br><br>_“The governmental body must ask for the attorney general&#x27;s decision and state the exceptions that apply within a reasonable time but not later than the 10th business day after the date of receiving the written request.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.301(b) |
| `TX-0020` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 15 business-days from receipt of the written request | **Within 15 business days of the request the body must submit to the AG written comments on the exceptions, a copy of the request, a dated receipt statement, and a copy (or representative sample) of the requested information.**<br><br>_“submit to the attorney general within a reasonable time but not later than the 15th business day after the date of receiving the written request: (1) written comments stating the reasons why the stated exceptions apply that would allow the information to be withheld; (2) a copy of the written request for information; (3) a signed statement as to the date on which the written request for information was received by the governmental body or evidence sufficient to establish that date; and (4) a copy of the specific information requested, or submit representative samples of the information if a voluminous amount of information was requested.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.301(e) |
| `TX-0023` | parameter · ceiling | sets-deadline · 45 business-days from AG receipt of the decision request (+ optional 10 business-day extension) | **The attorney general must render the decision not later than the 45th business day after receiving the request, extendable once by 10 business days on timely notice to the body and requestor.**<br><br>_“The attorney general shall render the decision ... not later than the 45th business day after the date the attorney general received the request for a decision. ... If the attorney general is unable to issue the decision within the 45-day period, the attorney general may extend the period for issuing the decision by an additional 10 business days by informing the governmental body and the requestor, during the original 45-day period, of the reason for the delay.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.306 |

Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.261(a) |

Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.262(a), (c) |

Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.2615(a) |
| `TX-0029` | parameter · fixed | terminal · 10 business-days from date the itemized statement is sent | **The request is considered withdrawn if the requestor does not respond in writing within 10 business days after the itemized statement is sent (accepting charges, modifying the request, or filing an overcharge complaint).**<br><br>_“A request described by Subsection (a) is considered to have been withdrawn by the requestor if the requestor does not respond in writing to the itemized statement by informing the governmental body within 10 business days after the date the statement is sent to the requestor that: (1) the requestor will accept the estimated charges; (2) the requestor is modifying the request in response to the itemized statement; or (3) the requestor has sent to the attorney general a complaint alleging that the requestor has been overcharged for being provided with a copy of the public information.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.2615(b) |
| `TX-0030` | structural | none | **If, before making the copy available, the body determines estimated charges will exceed the itemized statement by 20% or more, it must send a written updated itemized statement; no timely response to the update means the request is withdrawn.**<br><br>_“If the governmental body later determines, but before it makes the copy or the paper record available, that the estimated charges will exceed the charges detailed in the written itemized statement by 20 percent or more, the governmental body shall send to the requestor a written updated itemized statement that details all estimated charges that will be imposed, including any allowable charges for labor or personnel costs. If the requestor does not respond in writing to the updated estimate in the time and manner described by Subsection (b), the request is considered to have been withdrawn by the requestor.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.2615(c) |
| `TX-0031` | parameter · floor | none | **A body that sets time limits on uncompensated personnel time must allow at least 36 hours per requestor over a 12-month period and at least 15 hours per requestor in a one-month period before charging for personnel time.**<br><br>_“[The yearly time limit] may not be less than 36 hours for a requestor during the 12-month period ... [the monthly time limit] may not be less than 15 hours for a requestor for a one-month period.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.275(a), (b) |
| `TX-0032` | structural | none | **The body must provide a copy without charge or at a reduced charge if it determines waiver/reduction is in the public interest because providing the copy primarily benefits the general public.**<br><br>_“A governmental body shall provide a copy of public information without charge or at a reduced charge if the governmental body determines that waiver or reduction of the charge is in the public interest because providing the copy of the information primarily benefits the general public.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.267(a) |
| `TX-0033` | structural | none | **The body may waive the charge if the cost of processing the collection of the charge would exceed the amount of the charge.**<br><br>_“If the cost to a governmental body of processing the collection of a charge for providing a copy of public information will exceed the amount of the charge, the governmental body may waive the charge.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.267(b) |

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TX-0034` | parameter · fixed | none | **The officer may require a deposit or bond for anticipated copy costs once an itemized estimate has been provided and the estimated charge exceeds $100 (body with more than 15 full-time employees) or $50 (fewer than 16 full-time employees).**<br><br>_“An officer for public information or the officer&#x27;s agent may require a deposit or bond for payment of anticipated costs for the preparation of a copy of public information if: (1) the officer for public information or the officer&#x27;s agent has provided the requestor with the written itemized statement required under Section 552.2615 ... detailing the estimated charge for providing the copy; and (2) the charge for providing the copy of the public information specifically requested by the requestor is estimated by the governmental body to exceed: (A) $100, if the governmental body has more than 15 full-time employees; or (B) $50, if the governmental body has fewer than 16 full-time employees.”_ | Tex. Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.263(a) |
