Gov&#x27;t Code § 552.263(a-1) |

### Material negatives

- `deadline.acknowledgment_window` — Texas imposes NO fixed statutory acknowledgment or final-response day-count. The only response duty is to &#x27;promptly produce&#x27; (soft standard, § 552.221(a)-(b)); the sole hard 10-business-day event is a certification duty when records cannot be produced in that time (§ 552.221(d)), not a decision/production deadline. Absence of a found rule is not proof none exists.
- `appeal.administrative.requestor_appeal` — There is no requestor-initiated administrative appeal of a denial. The Act instead compels the governmental body to seek an AG ruling to withhold (§ 552.301); a requestor&#x27;s recourse for noncompliance is a complaint to the AG or a mandamus suit under § 552.321 (external litigation, excluded).
- `fee.copy_rate_per_page` — No statewide per-page copy rate is fixed in the statute itself; the concrete rates/ceilings are set by attorney general cost rules (1 Tex. Admin. Code ch. 70), which were not opened this pass. § 552.262 only supplies the ceiling mechanism. Verify the current TAC rate schedule in a later pass.
- `deadline.time_calculation` — The Act repeatedly uses &#x27;business day&#x27; but a general statutory definition/holiday-exclusion rule for computing the PIA clocks was not separately captured this pass; confirm how &#x27;business day&#x27; and skip-days are defined for §§ 552.221/552.301.
- Preservation/litigation-hold triggered by the request itself: no distinct PIA preservation-hold provision was located this pass (routine retention schedules are out of scope); flag for the retention/records-authority pass.

### Structural branches

- `eligibility.incarcerated_exclusion` — Requests from incarcerated/confined individuals (or their non-attorney agents) may be declined — a routing/decline branch the engine must express (§ 552.028).
- `appeal.administrative.ag_ruling_required` — To withhold under a Subchapter C exception the body must proactively obtain an attorney general decision; there is no requestor-initiated administrative appeal (§ 552.301(a)).
- `appeal.administrative.previous_determination` — A &#x27;previous determination&#x27; short-circuits the AG-decision requirement — a branch that skips the § 552.301 workflow (§ 552.301(a)).
- `denial.deemed_disclosure_on_noncompliance` — Failure to timely seek an AG decision / give the required notices flips the request into a &#x27;presumed public, must release&#x27; terminal state (§ 552.302).
- `clarification.tolls_deadline` — A good-faith clarification request tolls the 10-business-day AG-request clock until the requestor responds (City of Dallas v. Abbott).
- `redaction.ssn_no_ag_decision` — SSNs may be redacted without invoking the AG-decision workflow — a redaction path parallel to, and bypassing, § 552.301 (§ 552.147(b)).
- `fee.itemized_estimate_over_threshold` — Charges over $40 route through a mandatory itemized-estimate / requestor-response / updated-estimate flow that can terminate the request by withdrawal (§ 552.2615).
- `fee.small_request_labor_bar` — Requests of 50 or fewer pages follow a distinct fee-calc path (photocopy charge only, no labor/materials/overhead) unless multi-building/remote (§ 552.261(a)).
- `fee.waiver_public_interest` — A discretionary public-interest determination routes the request into free/reduced-charge production (§ 552.267(a)).

## Virginia (VA) — 39 rules

### Intake

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `VA-0001` | structural | none | **Public records are open only to citizens of the Commonwealth and to representatives of newspapers/magazines with circulation in, and radio/TV stations broadcasting in or into, the Commonwealth; the engine may restrict standing to these requester classes.**<br><br>_“Except as otherwise specifically provided by law, all public records shall be open to citizens of the Commonwealth, representatives of newspapers and magazines with circulation in the Commonwealth, and representatives of radio and television stations broadcasting in or into the Commonwealth during the regular office hours of the custodian of such records.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704(A) |
| `VA-0002` | structural | none | **A requester need not state a purpose or reason to obtain public records; the custodian may require only the requester&#x27;s name and legal address.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“The custodian may require the requester to provide his name and legal address.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704(A) |
| `VA-0004` | structural | none | **The custodian may require the requester to provide his name and legal address.**<br><br>_“The custodian may require the requester to provide his name and legal address.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704(A) |
| `VA-0005` | structural | none | **A request for public records shall identify the requested records with reasonable specificity.**<br><br>_“A request for public records shall identify the requested records with reasonable specificity.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704(B) |
| `VA-0006` | structural | none | **A request need not reference VFOIA to invoke the chapter&#x27;s provisions or to impose the response time limits on the public body.**<br><br>_“The request need not make reference to this chapter in order to invoke the provisions of this chapter or to impose the time limits for response by a public body.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704(B) |

### Production

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `VA-0003` | structural | none | **Access is provided by inspection or by providing copies of the requested records, at the option of the requester.**<br><br>_“Access to such records shall be provided by the custodian in accordance with this chapter by inspection or by providing copies of the requested records, at the option of the requester.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704(A) |
| `VA-0014` | structural | none | **No public body is required to create a new record that does not already exist, but it may abstract or summarize information under terms agreed with the requester.**<br><br>_“Subject to the provisions of subsection G, no public body shall be required to create a new record if the record does not already exist. However, a public body may abstract or summarize information under such terms and conditions as agreed between the requester and the public body.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704(D) |
| `VA-0016` | structural | none | **Records maintained in an electronic data processing system or database shall be made available at a reasonable cost not exceeding actual cost; where databases combine exempt and nonexempt records, the body shall provide access to the nonexempt records.**<br><br>_“Public records maintained by a public body in an electronic data processing system, computer database, or any other structured collection of data shall be made available to a requester at a reasonable cost, not to exceed the actual cost in accordance with subsection F. When electronic or other databases are combined or contain exempt and nonexempt records, the public body may provide access to the exempt records if not otherwise prohibited by law, but shall provide access to the nonexempt records as provided by this chapter.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704(G) |
| `VA-0017` | structural | none | **Excising exempt fields from a database, or converting data from one available format to another, is not deemed the creation, preparation, or compilation of a new public record.**<br><br>_“The excision of exempt fields of information from a database or the conversion of data from one available format to another shall not be deemed the creation, preparation, or compilation of a new public record.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704(G) |
| `VA-0018` | structural | none | **The body shall produce nonexempt database records in any tangible medium identified by the requester — including, where it has the capability, posting on a website or delivery to a requester-provided email address — if that medium is used in the regular course of business.**<br><br>_“Public bodies shall produce nonexempt records maintained in an electronic database in any tangible medium identified by the requester, including, where the public body has the capability, the option of posting the records on a website or delivering the records through an electronic mail address provided by the requester, if that medium is used by the public body in the regular course of business.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704(G) |
| `VA-0019` | structural | none | **No public body is required to produce records from an electronic database in a format not regularly used by it, but it shall make reasonable efforts to provide records in any format under terms agreed with the requester, including payment of reasonable costs.**<br><br>_“No public body shall be required to produce records from an electronic database in a format not regularly used by the public body. However, the public body shall make reasonable efforts to provide records in any format under such terms and conditions as agreed between the requester and public body, including the payment of reasonable costs.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704(G) |

### Deadlines

| `VA-0008` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · fixed: additional 7 work days from the original 5-working-day response | **If, within the five-work-day period, the body responds in writing that it is not practically possible to provide the records or determine availability and specifies the conditions making a response impossible, it gets an additional seven work days to make one of the four responses.**<br><br>_“It is not practically possible to provide the requested records or to determine whether they are available within the five-work-day period. | `VA-0009` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · fixed: 60 work days (in lieu of 7) from the original 5-working-day response | **For a request for criminal investigative files under § 2.2-3706.1, the additional extension when a response is not practically possible is 60 work days rather than seven.**<br><br>_“the public body shall have an additional seven work days or, in the case of a request for criminal investigative files pursuant to § 2.2-3706.1, 60 work days in which to provide one of the four preceding responses.”_ | Va. | `VA-0010` | structural | none | **A public body may petition the appropriate court for additional time to respond when the request is for an extraordinary volume of records or requires an extraordinarily lengthy search that would prevent it from meeting operational responsibilities, but must first make reasonable efforts to reach agreement with the requester.**<br><br>_“Any public body may petition the appropriate court for additional time to respond to a request for records when the request is for an extraordinary volume of records or requires an extraordinarily lengthy search, and a response by the public body within the time required by this chapter will prevent the public body from meeting its operational responsibilities. Code § 2.2-3704(C) |
| `VA-0027` | structural | tolls · tolls: from notice of cost estimate until requester responds | **The response period is tolled for the amount of time that elapses between the public body&#x27;s notice of the cost estimate and the requester&#x27;s response.**<br><br>_“The period within which the public body shall respond under this section shall be tolled for the amount of time that elapses between notice of the cost estimate and the response of the requester.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704(F) |
| `VA-0032` | structural | tolls · tolls: from notice of advance determination until requester responds | **The response period is tolled for the amount of time that elapses between notice of the advance determination and the requester&#x27;s response.**<br><br>_“The period within which the public body shall respond under this section shall be tolled for the amount of time that elapses between notice of the advance determination and the response of the requester.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704(H) |

### Redaction

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `VA-0020` | structural | none | **A public body may not withhold an entire record because some portion is excluded; a record may be withheld in full only where an exclusion applies to its entire content, otherwise only the excluded portions are withheld and all nonexcluded portions shall be disclosed.**<br><br>_“No provision of this chapter is intended, nor shall it be construed or applied, to authorize a public body to withhold a public record in its entirety on the grounds that some portion of the public record is excluded from disclosure by this chapter or by any other provision of law. A public record may be withheld from disclosure in its entirety only to the extent that an exclusion from disclosure under this chapter or other provision of law applies to the entire content of the public record. Otherwise, only those portions of the public record containing information subject to an exclusion under this chapter or other provision of law may be withheld, and all portions of the public record that are not so excluded shall be disclosed.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704.01 |

| `VA-0022` | parameter · soft-standard | none · soft-standard: &#x27;lowest possible cost&#x27; | **The body shall make all reasonable efforts to supply the requested records at the lowest possible cost.**<br><br>_“and shall make all reasonable efforts to supply the requested records at the lowest possible cost.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704(F) |
| `VA-0023` | structural | none | **No public body shall impose extraneous, intermediary, or surplus fees or expenses to recoup the general costs of creating or maintaining records or transacting the general business of the public body.**<br><br>_“No public body shall impose any extraneous, intermediary, or surplus fees or expenses to recoup the general costs associated with creating or maintaining records or transacting the general business of the public body.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704(F) |
| `VA-0024` | parameter · ceiling | none · ceiling: actual cost of duplication | **Any duplicating fee charged by a public body shall not exceed the actual cost of duplication.**<br><br>_“Any duplicating fee charged by a public body shall not exceed the actual cost of duplication.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704(F) |
| `VA-0025` | structural | none | **Prior to conducting a search for records, the body shall notify the requester in writing that it may make reasonable charges not to exceed actual cost and inquire whether the requester would like a cost estimate in advance.**<br><br>_“Prior to conducting a search for records, the public body shall notify the requester in writing that the public body may make reasonable charges not to exceed its actual cost incurred in accessing, duplicating, supplying, or searching for requested records and inquire of the requester whether he would like to request a cost estimate in advance of the supplying of the requested records.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704(F) |
| `VA-0026` | structural | none | **The public body shall provide the requester with a cost estimate if requested.**<br><br>_“The public body shall provide the requester with a cost estimate if requested.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704(F) |
| `VA-0029` | structural | none | **Any costs incurred by the public body in estimating the cost of supplying the records shall be applied toward the overall charges to be paid by the requester.**<br><br>_“Any costs incurred by the public body in estimating the cost of supplying the requested records shall be applied toward the overall charges to be paid by the requester for the supplying of such requested records.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704(F) |
| `VA-0033` | structural | none | **The reasonable-charge fee provisions do not apply to scholastic records that must be made available under FERPA when requested by a parent or legal guardian of a minor student or by a student who is 18 or older.**<br><br>_“Except with regard to scholastic records requested pursuant to subdivision A 1 of § 2.2-3705.4 that must be made available for inspection pursuant to the federal Family Educational Rights and Privacy Act (20 U.S.C. § 1232g) and such requests for scholastic records by a parent or legal guardian of a minor student or by a student who is 18 years of age or older, a public body may make reasonable charges ...”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704(F) |
| `VA-0034` | parameter · ceiling | none · ceiling: actual cost; exception — pro rata per-acre for body-developed topo maps over 50 contiguous acres | **A body may make a reasonable charge, not exceeding actual cost, for records produced from a geographic information system at the request of anyone other than the owner of the subject land, except that it may charge on a pro rata per-acre basis for topographical maps it developed that encompass a contiguous area greater than 50 acres.**<br><br>_“The public body may also make a reasonable charge for the cost incurred in supplying records produced from a geographic information system at the request of anyone other than the owner of the land that is the subject of the request. However, such charges shall not exceed the actual cost to the public body in supplying such records, except that the public body may charge, on a pro rata per acre basis, for the cost of creating topographical maps developed by the public body, for such maps or portions thereof, which encompass a contiguous area greater than 50 acres.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704(F) |

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `VA-0028` | parameter · fixed | terminal · fixed terminal: 30 days from sending the cost estimate = deemed withdrawn | **If the public body receives no response from the requester within 30 days of sending the cost estimate, the request is deemed withdrawn.**<br><br>_“If the public body receives no response from the requester within 30 days of sending the cost estimate, the request shall be deemed to be withdrawn.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704(F) |

### Payment

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `VA-0030` | structural | none | **Any local public body that charges for producing records may provide an electronic method of payment (noncash, non-paper-check — credit/debit cards, direct deposit/debit, electronic checks, telephonic payment).**<br><br>_“Any local public body that charges for the production of records pursuant to this section may provide an electronic method of payment through which all payments for the production of such records to such locality may be made.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704(F) |
| `VA-0031` | parameter · fixed | none · fixed threshold: $200; deposit ceiling = the advance-determination amount | **Where a public body determines in advance that charges are likely to exceed $200, it may, before continuing to process the request, require the requester to pay a deposit not to exceed the amount of the advance determination.**<br><br>_“In any case where a public body determines in advance that charges for producing the requested records are likely to exceed $200, the public body may, before continuing to process the request, require the requester to pay a deposit not to exceed the amount of the advance determination.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3704(H) |

### Appeals

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `VA-0036` | structural | none | **An aggrieved requester may file a petition for mandamus or injunction in the appropriate court (venue keyed to the type of public body) to enforce the chapter&#x27;s records-access rights.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“Venue for a petition against a local public body lies in the court of the county or city from which the public body has been elected or appointed to serve; for a regional body, where its principal business office is located; and for a state agency or institution of higher education, in the court of the residence of the aggrieved party or of the City of Richmond.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3713(A) |
| `VA-0037` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · fixed: 7 days from date petition is made; ≥3 working days&#x27; notice to defendant | **The petition for mandamus or injunction shall be heard within seven days of the date it is made, provided the defendant receives at least three working days&#x27; notice (notice waived for open-meetings violations).**<br><br>_“The petition for mandamus or injunction shall be heard within seven days of the date when the same is made”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3713(D) |
| `VA-0038` | structural | none | **In an enforcement proceeding the public body bears the burden of proving an exclusion by a preponderance of the evidence, and any failure by the body to follow the chapter&#x27;s procedures is presumed to be a violation.**<br><br>_“The public body shall bear the burden of proof to establish an exclusion by a preponderance of the evidence. ... any failure by a public body to follow the procedures established by this chapter shall be presumed to be a violation.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3713(E) |
| `VA-0039` | structural | none | **The enforcement proceeding shall be given precedence on the court&#x27;s docket over all cases not otherwise given precedence by law.**<br><br>_“shall be given precedence on the docket of such court over all cases that are not otherwise given precedence by law.”_ | Va. Code § 2.2-3713(D) |

### Material negatives

- `intake.acknowledgment_window` — VFOIA has no acknowledgment step distinct from the substantive response. The five-working-day response IS the first required communication (§ 2.2-3704(B)); there is no separate &#x27;we received your request&#x27; clock. Absence of a found rule is not proof none exists.
- `appeal.administrative.internal_appeal` — VFOIA provides NO internal administrative appeal and no binding agency/AG review of a denial. The only formal remedy is the judicial mandamus/injunction petition (§ 2.2-3713). The Virginia FOIA Advisory Council (§ 30-179) issues only nonbinding advisory opinions. Emit no appeal.administrative.* rule for Virginia.
- `fee.copy_rate_per_page` — No statewide fixed or ceiling per-page copy rate. Fees are bounded only by the &#x27;actual cost&#x27; ceiling (VA-0021, VA-0024); the per-page amount, if any, is set by each locality under that ceiling.
- `fee.commercial_purpose_surcharge` — VFOIA draws no commercial-vs-noncommercial requester distinction and authorizes no commercial-purpose fee surcharge; &#x27;commercial purpose&#x27; is not a defined term in § 2.2-3701. Charges turn on actual cost regardless of requester purpose.
- `eligibility.any_person` — Deliberately NOT emitted as a rule: Virginia does not grant universal any-person access. Standing is restricted to citizens of the Commonwealth plus in-state media (see VA-0001). Emitting the shared any_person/no_residency key here would misjoin Virginia with open-access states.
- `classification.mrr_or_recurring_request` — No statutory classification for multi-record, recurring, or subscription requests, and no distinct processing track for them. Each request runs the single § 2.2-3704 pipeline.
- `preservation.litigation_hold_on_request` — No VFOIA provision imposes a preservation/litigation hold triggered by the request or by an enforcement petition. Records retention runs under the separate Public Records Act (Title 42.1) on routine schedules, which are out of scope. No request-triggered hold rule found.
- `intake.submission_channel_required` — VFOIA prescribes no mandatory submission channel (no required form, portal, or writing). A request need not even cite the chapter (VA-0006); the statute is silent on medium, so no channel rule is emitted.

### Structural branches

- `response.four_response_branch` — The § 2.2-3704(B) response is a four-way branch the engine must express within the 5-day clock: (1) provide records, (2) withhold entirely with citation, (3) provide/withhold in part with citation, (4) records not found/not practically possible. Each leg has its own required notice content (VA-0011/0012/0013) and the (4) leg opens the extension path.
- `response.court_petition_extension_path` — § 2.2-3704(C) court-petition-for-additional-time is a state-specific external fork (extraordinary volume / lengthy search) gated by a prior duty to attempt agreement with the requester; it produces no fixed day-count (VA-0010).
- `response.deemed_withdrawal_state` — A request enters a terminal &#x27;deemed withdrawn&#x27; state after 30 days of requester silence following a cost estimate (VA-0028) — a distinct end-state separate from denial or fulfillment.
- `response.tolled_awaiting_payment_state` — The statutory clock pauses (tolls) in two payment-negotiation states: awaiting the requester&#x27;s answer to a cost estimate (VA-0027) and awaiting the requester&#x27;s answer to a &gt;$200 advance-deposit determination (VA-0032). The request sits in a paused-for-payment state, resuming on requester response.
- `custody.foia_officer_routing_anchor` — § 2.2-3704.2 requires a designated, publicly-identified FOIA officer as the intake point of contact (VA-0035); routing anchors to that officer, and a not-found response must hand off to a known alternate custodian (VA-0013).
- `appeal.judicial_enforcement_path` — Virginia&#x27;s only formal denial-review path is judicial — a mandamus/injunction petition (VA-0036) with a 7-day expedited hearing (VA-0037), burden on the public body (VA-0038), and docket precedence (VA-0039). There is no administrative-appeal stage to model.

---

## Washington (WA) — 44 rules

### Intake

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WA-0001` | structural | none | **Upon request for identifiable public records, the agency shall make them promptly available to any person.**<br><br>_“Public records shall be available for inspection and copying, and agencies shall, upon request for identifiable public records, make them promptly available to any person including, if applicable, on a partial or installment basis as records that are part of a larger set of requested records are assembled or made ready for inspection or disclosure.”_ | RCW 42.56.080(2) |
| `WA-0002` | structural | none | **A requester shall not be required to provide the purpose of the request, except to establish whether disclosure would violate the commercial-purpose list prohibition or another statute restricting disclosure to certain persons.**<br><br>_“Agencies shall not distinguish among persons requesting records, and such persons shall not be required to provide information as to the purpose for the request except to establish whether inspection and copying would violate RCW 42.56.070(8) or 42.56.240(14), or other statute which exempts or prohibits disclosure of specific information or records to certain persons.”_ | RCW 42.56.080(2) |
| `WA-0003` | structural | none | **An agency shall not distinguish among persons requesting records.**<br><br>_“Agencies shall not distinguish among persons requesting records, and such persons shall not be required to provide information as to the purpose for the request except to establish whether inspection and copying would violate RCW 42.56.070(8) or 42.56.240(14), or other statute which exempts or prohibits disclosure of specific information or records to certain persons.”_ | RCW 42.56.080(2) |
| `WA-0004` | structural | none | **Records must be made available to any person with no residency or citizenship qualification.**<br><br>_“agencies shall, upon request for identifiable public records, make them promptly available to any person”_ | RCW 42.56.080(2) |
| `WA-0005` | structural | none | **No official format is required to make a records request, though an agency may recommend a form or web page.**<br><br>_“No official format is required for making a records request; however, agencies may recommend that requestors submit requests using an agency provided form or web page.”_ | RCW 42.56.080(2) |
| `WA-0006` | structural | none | **An agency shall honor requests for identifiable public records received in person during normal office hours, or by mail or email, unless exempted by the chapter.**<br><br>_“Agencies shall honor requests received in person during an agency&#x27;s normal office hours, or by mail or email, for identifiable public records unless exempted by provisions of this chapter.”_ | RCW 42.56.080(2) |
| `WA-0007` | structural | none | **A request must be for identifiable records; a request for all or substantially all of an agency&#x27;s records is not valid, but a request for all records on a particular topic, keyword, or name is not treated as a request for all records.**<br><br>_“A public records request must be for identifiable records. A request for all or substantially all records prepared, owned, used, or retained by an agency is not a valid request for identifiable records under this chapter, provided that a request for all records regarding a particular topic or containing a particular keyword or name shall not be considered a request for all of an agency&#x27;s records.”_ | RCW 42.56.080(1)(a) |
| `WA-0008` | structural | none | **An agency shall not deny a request for identifiable public records solely because the request is overbroad.**<br><br>_“Agencies shall not deny a request for identifiable public records solely on the basis that the request is overbroad.”_ | RCW 42.56.080(2) |
| `WA-0009` | structural | none | **An agency may deny a bot request that is one of multiple requests from the same requester within a 24-hour period if it establishes that responding would cause excessive interference with other essential functions.**<br><br>_“An agency may deny a bot request that is one of multiple requests from the requestor to the agency within a twenty-four hour period, if the agency establishes that responding to the multiple requests would cause excessive interference with other essential functions of the agency. For purposes of this subsection, &quot;bot request&quot; means a request for public records that an agency reasonably believes was automatically generated by a computer program or script.”_ | RCW 42.56.080(3) |

### Deadlines

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WA-0010` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 5 business-days from request receipt | **Within five business days of receiving a request, the agency must respond in one of the five statutory ways.**<br><br>_“Within five business days of receiving a public record request, an agency, the office of the secretary of the senate, or the office of the chief clerk of the house of representatives must respond in one of the ways provided in this subsection (1):”_ | RCW 42.56.520(1) |
| `WA-0011` | structural | none | **The initial response must take one of five forms: provide the record; provide a website link to it; acknowledge and give a reasonable time estimate; acknowledge and request clarification with an estimate; or deny.**<br><br>_“(a) Providing the record; (b) Providing an internet address and link on the agency&#x27;s website to the specific records requested, except that if the requester notifies the agency that he or she cannot access the records through the internet, then the agency must provide copies of the record or allow the requester to view copies using an agency computer; (c) Acknowledging that the agency ... has received the request and providing a reasonable estimate of the time the agency ... will require to respond to the request; (d) Acknowledging that the agency ... has received the request and asking the requestor to provide clarification for a request that is unclear, and providing, to the greatest extent possible, a reasonable estimate of the time ... to respond to the request if it is not clarified; or (e) Denying the public record request.”_ | RCW 42.56.520(1)(a)-(e) |
| `WA-0012` | structural | sets-deadline · undefined-soft: &quot;reasonable estimate of the time ... to respond&quot; from acknowledgment | **When acknowledging a request rather than fulfilling it, the agency must provide a reasonable estimate of the time it will require to respond.**<br><br>_“Acknowledging that the agency, the office of the secretary of the senate, or the office of the chief clerk of the house of representatives has received the request and providing a reasonable estimate of the time the agency, the office of the secretary of the senate, or the office of the chief clerk of the house of representatives will require to respond to the request;”_ | RCW 42.56.520(1)(c) |
| `WA-0013` | structural | sets-deadline · undefined-soft: &quot;promptly&quot; from request receipt | **Responses to public records requests shall be made promptly.**<br><br>_“Responses to requests for public records shall be made promptly by agencies, the office of the secretary of the senate, and the office of the chief clerk of the house of representatives.”_ | RCW 42.56.520(1) |
| `WA-0014` | structural | none | **Additional time to respond may be based on the need to clarify the request, to locate and assemble records, to notify affected third persons or agencies, or to determine whether information is exempt.**<br><br>_“Additional time required to respond to a request may be based upon the need to clarify the intent of the request, to locate and assemble the information requested, to notify third persons or agencies affected by the request, or to determine whether any of the information requested is exempt and that a denial should be made as to all or part of the request.”_ | RCW 42.56.520(2) |

### Clarification

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WA-0015` | structural | none | **When acknowledging an unclear request, the agency may ask the requester to clarify what information is sought.**<br><br>_“In acknowledging receipt of a public record request that is unclear, an agency, the office of the secretary of the senate, or the office of the chief clerk of the house of representatives may ask the requestor to clarify what information the requestor is seeking.”_ | RCW 42.56.520(3)(a) |
| `WA-0016` | structural | terminal · terminal: agency need not respond; no statutory waiting period specified | **If the requester fails to respond to a clarification request and the entire request is unclear, the agency need not respond to it.**<br><br>_“If the requestor fails to respond to an agency request to clarify the request, and the entire request is unclear, the agency, the office of the secretary of the senate, or the office of the chief clerk of the house of representatives need not respond to it. Otherwise, the agency must respond, pursuant to this section, to those portions of the request that are clear.”_ | RCW 42.56.520(3)(b) |
| `WA-0017` | structural | none | **Where only part of a request is unclear, the agency must respond to the portions that are clear.**<br><br>_“Otherwise, the agency must respond, pursuant to this section, to those portions of the request that are clear.”_ | RCW 42.56.520(3)(b) |

### Production

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WA-0018` | structural | none | **Records may be provided on a partial or installment basis as records in a larger set are assembled or made ready.**<br><br>_“agencies shall, upon request for identifiable public records, make them promptly available to any person including, if applicable, on a partial or installment basis as records that are part of a larger set of requested records are assembled or made ready for inspection or disclosure.”_ | RCW 42.56.080(2) |

### Fees

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WA-0019` | parameter · fixed | none | **No fee may be charged for inspecting public records or for locating documents and making them available for copying.**<br><br>_“No fee shall be charged for the inspection of public records or locating public documents and making them available for copying, except as provided in RCW 42.56.240(14) and subsection (3) of this section.”_ | RCW 42.56.120(1) |
| `WA-0020` | parameter · ceiling | none | **A reasonable charge may be imposed for copies and for use of agency copying equipment, not exceeding the amount necessary to reimburse the agency for its actual costs directly incident to copying.**<br><br>_“A reasonable charge may be imposed for providing copies of public records and for the use by any person of agency equipment or equipment of the office of the secretary of the senate or the office of the chief clerk of the house of representatives to copy public records, which charges shall not exceed the amount necessary to reimburse the agency, the office of the secretary of the senate, or the office of the chief clerk of the house of representatives for its actual costs directly incident to such copying.”_ | RCW 42.56.120(1) |
| `WA-0021` | parameter · ceiling | none | **Absent a published actual-cost calculation, the agency may charge up to fifteen cents per page for photocopies or printed copies of electronic records.**<br><br>_“Fifteen cents per page for photocopies”_ | RCW 42.56.120(2)(b)(i) |
| `WA-0022` | parameter · ceiling | none | **Absent a published actual-cost calculation, the agency may charge up to ten cents per page for public records scanned into an electronic format.**<br><br>_“Ten cents per page for public records scanned”_ | RCW 42.56.120(2)(b)(ii) |
| `WA-0023` | parameter · ceiling | none | **Absent a published actual-cost calculation, the agency may charge up to five cents per each four electronic files or attachments uploaded for electronic delivery.**<br><br>_“Five cents per each four electronic files”_ | RCW 42.56.120(2)(b)(iii) |
| `WA-0024` | parameter · ceiling | none | **Absent a published actual-cost calculation, the agency may charge up to ten cents per gigabyte for transmission of records in an electronic format.**<br><br>_“Ten cents per gigabyte for the transmission”_ | RCW 42.56.120(2)(b)(iv) |
| `WA-0025` | parameter · ceiling | none | **An agency may charge a flat fee of up to two dollars for a request as payment for the costs of providing the copies, in lieu of itemized charges.**<br><br>_“An agency may charge a flat fee of up to two dollars for any request”_ | RCW 42.56.120(2)(d) |
| `WA-0026` | parameter · fixed | none | **An agency may not impose copying charges for access to or downloading of records it routinely posts on its public internet website.**<br><br>_“An agency shall not impose copying charges under this section for access to or downloading of records that the agency routinely posts on its public internet website.”_ | RCW 42.56.120(2)(e) |
| `WA-0027` | structural | none | **An agency may charge actual copying costs only in accordance with costs it has established and published; it may establish and make available a statement of the actual costs it charges for copies.**<br><br>_“Each agency may establish, maintain, and make available for public inspection and copying a statement of the actual costs that it charges for providing photocopies or electronically produced copies, of public records.”_ | RCW 42.56.070(7); RCW 42.56.120(2)(a) |
| `WA-0028` | structural | none | **In determining actual copy costs, an agency may not include staff salaries, benefits, or general administrative or overhead charges unless directly related to the actual cost of copying.**<br><br>_“In determining the actual costs for providing copies of public records, an agency may not include staff salaries, benefits, or other general administrative or overhead charges, unless those costs are directly related to the actual cost of copying the public records.”_ | RCW 42.56.070(7)(b) |
| `WA-0029` | structural | none | **An agency may impose a customized service charge for a request that requires information-technology expertise to prepare data compilations or provide customized electronic access not used by the agency for other purposes, only after notifying the requester of the charge and the reason it applies.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“Agencies may charge for customized services (data compilations, custom electronic access) only when services aren&#x27;t used internally, after notifying requestors of costs and reasons.”_ | RCW 42.56.120(3) |
| `WA-0030` | structural | none | **The chapter does not authorize giving, selling, or providing access to lists of individuals requested for commercial purposes, and agencies shall not do so unless specifically authorized or directed by law.**<br><br>_“This chapter shall not be construed as giving authority to any agency, the office of the secretary of the senate, or the office of the chief clerk of the house of representatives to give, sell or provide access to lists of individuals requested for commercial purposes, and agencies, the office of the secretary of the senate, or the office of the chief clerk of the house of representatives shall not do so unless specifically authorized or directed by law.”_ | RCW 42.56.070(8) |

### Payment

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WA-0031` | parameter · ceiling | none | **An agency may require a deposit not exceeding ten percent of the estimated cost of providing copies for a request.**<br><br>_“An agency may require a deposit in an amount not to exceed ten percent of the estimated cost of providing copies for a request.”_ | RCW 42.56.120(4) |
| `WA-0032` | structural | terminal · terminal: balance of request not fulfilled when an installment is unclaimed | **If the requester fails to inspect, pay for, or claim an installment of a records request, the agency need not fulfill the balance of the request.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“Agencies may charge for partial fulfillment installments and needn&#x27;t complete requests if portions go unclaimed.”_ | RCW 42.56.120(4) |

### Redaction

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WA-0033` | structural | none | **The chapter&#x27;s exemptions are inapplicable to the extent that information whose disclosure would violate personal privacy or vital governmental interests can be deleted from the specific records sought; the non-exempt remainder must be disclosed.**<br><br>_“Except for information described in RCW 42.56.230(3)(a) and confidential income data exempted from public inspection pursuant to RCW 84.40.020, the exemptions of this chapter are inapplicable to the extent that information, the disclosure of which would violate personal privacy or vital governmental interests, can be deleted from the specific records sought. No exemption may be construed to permit the nondisclosure of statistical information not descriptive of any readily identifiable person or persons.”_ | RCW 42.56.210(1) |

### Denials

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WA-0034` | structural | none | **A response refusing inspection in whole or in part must include a statement of the specific exemption authorizing the withholding and a brief explanation of how the exemption applies to the record withheld.**<br><br>_“Agency responses refusing, in whole or in part, inspection of any public record shall include a statement of the specific exemption authorizing the withholding of the record (or part) and a brief explanation of how the exemption applies to the record withheld.”_ | RCW 42.56.210(3) |
| `WA-0035` | structural | none | **Denials of requests must be accompanied by a written statement of the specific reasons for the denial.**<br><br>_“Denials of requests must be accompanied by a written statement of the specific reasons therefor.”_ | RCW 42.56.520(4) |

### Appeals

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WA-0036` | structural | sets-deadline · internal review deemed complete at end of 2nd business day following denial | **An agency must establish a mechanism for the most prompt possible review of a denial of inspection; the review is deemed completed at the end of the second business day following the denial and constitutes final agency action for purposes of judicial review.**<br><br>_“Agencies, the office of the secretary of the senate, and the office of the chief clerk of the house of representatives shall establish mechanisms for the most prompt possible review of decisions denying inspection, and such review shall be deemed completed at the end of the second business day following the denial of inspection and shall constitute final agency action or final action by the office of the secretary of the senate or the office of the chief clerk of the house of representatives for the purposes of judicial review.”_ | RCW 42.56.520(4) |
| `WA-0037` | structural | none | **A person denied the opportunity to inspect or copy a record may move the superior court to require the agency to show cause why it refused; the agency bears the burden of proving the refusal is authorized by an exempting or prohibiting statute.**<br><br>_“the superior court ... may require the responsible agency to show cause why it has refused to allow inspection or copying of a specific public record or class of records. The burden of proof shall be on the agency to establish that refusal to permit public inspection and copying is in accordance with a statute that exempts or prohibits disclosure in whole or in part of specific information or records.”_ | RCW 42.56.550(1) |
| `WA-0038` | structural | none | **A person may petition the superior court to challenge an agency&#x27;s reasonable estimate of the time to respond or its copying charges; the agency must show the estimate or charge is reasonable.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“A person challenging an agency&#x27;s time estimate or copying charges may petition superior court. The agency must demonstrate its estimate is reasonable.”_ | RCW 42.56.550(2) |
| `WA-0039` | parameter · fixed | sets-deadline · 1 year from the agency&#x27;s claim of exemption or the last production on a partial/installment basis | **An action under the judicial-review section must be filed within one year of the agency&#x27;s claim of exemption or the last production of a record on a partial or installment basis.**<br><br>_“Actions under this section must be filed within one year of the agency&#x27;s claim of exemption or the last production of a record on a partial or installment basis.”_ | RCW 42.56.550(6) |
| `WA-0044` | structural | pauses · production held pending the court&#x27;s ruling on the injunction motion | **An agency, its representative, or a person named in or to whom a record specifically pertains may move the superior court by affidavit to enjoin inspection of a specific record, which the court may grant on finding that disclosure would clearly not be in the public interest and would substantially and irreparably damage any person or vital governmental functions.**<br><br>_“would clearly not be in the public interest and would substantially and irreparably damage any person, or would substantially and irreparably damage vital governmental functions.”_ | RCW 42.56.540 |

### Custody/Routing

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WA-0040` | structural | none | **Each state and local agency shall appoint and publicly identify a public records officer to serve as the point of contact for records requests and to oversee the agency&#x27;s compliance with the chapter.**<br><br>_“Each state and local agency shall appoint and publicly identify a public records officer whose responsibility is to serve as a point of contact for members of the public in requesting disclosure of public records and to oversee the agency&#x27;s compliance with the public records disclosure requirements of this chapter.”_ | RCW 42.56.580(1) |

### Communications

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WA-0041` | structural | sets-deadline · undefined-soft: &quot;most timely possible action&quot; from request receipt | **Agency rules must provide for the fullest assistance to inquirers and the most timely possible action on requests for information.**<br><br>_“Such rules and regulations shall provide for the fullest assistance to inquirers and the most timely possible action on requests for information.”_ | RCW 42.56.100 |
| `WA-0043` | structural | none | **An agency has discretion to notify persons named in, or to whom a requested record specifically pertains, that release of the record has been requested, except where notice is required by law.** _(paraphrase)_<br><br>_“Agencies have discretion to notify persons named in or specifically pertained to by records when release is requested, except where law requires such notice.”_ | RCW 42.56.540 |

### Special Records

| Rule ID | Home / Basis | Clock | Atomic rule &amp; verbatim source | Authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WA-0042` | structural | pauses · destruction schedule held until the request is resolved | **If a requested record exists but is scheduled for destruction, the agency must retain it and may not destroy or erase it until the request is resolved.**<br><br>_“If a public record request is made at a time when such record exists but is scheduled for destruction in the near future, the agency, the office of the secretary of the senate, or the office of the chief clerk of the house of representatives shall retain possession of the record, and may not destroy or erase the record until the request is resolved.”_ | RCW 42.56.100 |

### Material negatives

- `response.final_production_deadline` — Washington sets no fixed statutory deadline for FINAL production of records. The only hard deadline is the five-business-day initial response (WA-0010); the time to actually produce is governed by the agency&#x27;s &#x27;reasonable estimate&#x27; (WA-0012) and the soft &#x27;promptly&#x27;/&#x27;most timely possible action&#x27; standards (WA-0013, WA-0041). Absence of a found rule is not proof none exists.
- `clarification.requester_response_clock` — No statutory numeric period within which a requester must answer a clarification request. RCW 42.56.520(3)(b) states only that if the requester fails to respond and the request is wholly unclear the agency need not respond (WA-0016) — no day-count is fixed.
- `appeal.administrative.requester_filing_deadline` — No statutory deadline for a requester to REQUEST internal review of a denial. The internal-review mechanism (WA-0036) is agency-established and self-completes at the end of the second business day after denial; the statute imposes no filing window on the requester.
- `request.tracking_log_required` — No statute requires municipalities to maintain a request tracking log or assign a tracking number. The AG model rules (WAC 44-14) recommend logging as a best practice, but that is advisory guidance, not a statutory requirement.
- `search.staff_time_chargeable` — No provision authorizes charging for staff search/location time; RCW 42.56.120(1) bars any fee for locating records and RCW 42.56.070(7)(b) excludes staff salaries/overhead from copy costs. Recorded as the absence of a search-fee rule, consistent with WA-0019/WA-0028.
- `intake.acknowledgment_separate_from_response` — Washington has no acknowledgment step separate from the five-business-day response; acknowledgment is itself one of the five permitted first responses (RCW 42.56.520(1)(c)-(d)), so there is no distinct acknowledgment deadline to configure.

### Structural branches

- `response.response_options_enumerated` — The five-way initial-response fork (provide / link / acknowledge+estimate / acknowledge+clarify / deny) the engine must be able to execute within five business days. RCW 42.56.520(1).
- `clarification.nonresponse_abandonment` — A terminal branch where a wholly-unclear request with no requester clarification lets the agency close it without responding, while partly-clear requests still require partial response. RCW 42.56.520(3)(b).
- `appeal.administrative.internal_review` — Washington-specific internal-review stage that auto-completes at the end of the second business day after a denial and ripens the matter for judicial review. RCW 42.56.520(4).
- `appeal.judicial.show_cause_review` — Superior-court show-cause path with the burden on the agency; distinct from any internal appeal. RCW 42.56.550(1).
- `appeal.judicial.charge_or_estimate_review` — Separate judicial path to challenge the reasonableness of a time estimate or copying charge. RCW 42.56.550(2).
- `appeal.judicial.third_party_injunction` — Pre-production injunction branch: a named third party or the agency moves the superior court to restrain disclosure, pausing production pending the ruling. RCW 42.56.540.
- `communications.third_party_notice` — Discretionary notice-to-affected-third-party step that feeds the injunction branch. RCW 42.56.540.
- `preservation.hold_on_scheduled_destruction` — A preservation-hold state a request enters when responsive records are scheduled for destruction, pausing the destruction schedule until the request is resolved. RCW 42.56.100.
- `fee.customized_service_charge` — A conditional fee branch requiring an advance notice/explanation/estimate before an IT-intensive customized service charge may be imposed. RCW 42.56.120(3).
- `custody.public_records_officer` — Mandatory routing target: each agency&#x27;s publicly identified public records officer is the point of contact and compliance overseer. RCW 42.56.580(1).
